### FU-093 | CRITICAL: adapter never reached the pod -- 99.6% of the scored moat is garbage (root cause + fix shipped)
- date: 2026-07-25 - source: daily-chairman-review (chairman-triggered) - status: open - priority: P0
- class: defect
- detail: ROOT CAUSE: the SFT repo .gitignore lists *.safetensors/*.pt/*.bin, so ph_bundle's plain `git add score_transfer` SILENTLY skipped the adapter weights+heads. The pushed score branch carried only adapter_config.json+README (verified via GH API on score-job-20260724-184947). On the pod PEFT could not attach -> HF repo-id fallback -> 401 Repository Not Found -> 'heads have random init' -> scored on base Qwen2.5-3B + RANDOM HEADS. WHY GATES MISSED IT: I5 hashed the LOCAL FILESYSTEM copy (not what git staged) so it passed green; degraded=false only checked preds COUNT; eval_phase2 only WARNs on attach failure; and 3+ daily reviews reported milestones off ROW COUNTS without ever opening the eval log or sanity-checking distributions. BLAST RADIUS (prod overall_risk by import date): 7/18=86,050 rows 100% HIGH; 7/21=65,045 100% CRITICAL; 7/24=125,731 100% LOW; only ~1,200 pre-7/18 scores are REAL => 276,826 of 278,026 (99.6%) are noise. Same class as the chairman-recalled RunPod-era 'adapter weights frequently lost'. FIX FU-093 PR #1804 (bash -n verified): git add -f; committed-TREE verify via ls-tree (>=1MB catches the 133-byte LFS-pointer class); post-push REMOTE re-fetch verify (never trust push exit code); pod-side ARRIVAL gate; pod-side FATAL grep on attach-failure warnings. Adapter is 28.2MB < 100MB so a forced PLAIN BLOB suffices (no LFS, sidesteps pointer class). NEXT: canary run 20260725-034955 (400 servers, $1 cap) must PROVE attach + non-degenerate labels BEFORE any full re-score; then chairman decision on rolling back the 3 garbage batches. Related: [[FU-091]] (HF prefetch, separate defect), [[paid_job_launch_confirmation_policy]].
- verify: `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\zo_probe.py" --sql "SELECT ROUND(100.0*COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE risk_tier IS NOT NULL AND confidence IS NOT NULL)/NULLIF(COUNT(*),0),2) FROM server_scores" --assert "v is not None and float(v)>50"`  # over half the scored moat carries a defensible signal, not adapter garbage
- verify_seen_red: NEVER
- log:
  - 2026-07-27T12:20Z plan-200k-count-tracker: **WAVE 1 LANDED and the trust recovery is now MEASURED, not projected.** Read-only cohort audit against prod (`mcp_llm_axis_scores`, `axis_name=overall_risk`, judged by `weekly_rescore.cohort_trust` — the same PR #2033 module that gates landing) returns: `2026-07-27 07:21:18` = **120,509 servers VALID**; `2026-07-26 23:14:21` = 20,576 VALID; `2026-07-24 23:29:53` = 125,731 DEGENERATE; `2026-07-21 06:09:50` = **11,095** DEGENERATE (was 65,045 — overwritten in place as designed); the `2026-07-18` cohort of 66,050 is **GONE, fully displaced**. So trustworthy assessed servers went **20,576 -> 141,085 in one wave, 7.4% -> 50.5% of the scored moat**, and distrusted fell 258K -> **136,826**. TRACKER-SCOPE POINT, and the reason this belongs in the count ledger: public `scored_servers` moved only **+1,085** today (278,026 -> 279,111) while the defensible count moved **+120,509** — because a rescore OVERWRITES in place, so the headline metric is structurally blind to the single most valuable day of assessment work this project has had. A flat-looking day was in fact the largest real advance. Corollary for anyone grading PLAN_200K off `/freshness` alone: `scored_servers` measures rows touched ever, not signal quality, and on rescore days the two diverge completely. Recorded as a new `trusted_servers` column in `plan_200k_count_log.csv` (additive, blank for pre-audit rows, `2026-07-26`=20576, `2026-07-27`=141085) so the basis ships with the figure rather than being re-derived. No spend, no writes to prod — SELECT-only via the fly proxy.

### FU-235 | The prod schema has TWO owners, and alembic runs as the one that owns neither `users` nor `orgs` -- so the migration lane could only ever migrate the tables it had created itself
- date: 2026-08-02 · source: attended Class-B prod fire of 9d365abd, aborted by the release_command · status: open · priority: P0
- class: defect
- detail: **MEASURED INSIDE THE RUNNING FLY MACHINE AT 18:18Z, WITH THE ROLE IT ACTUALLY CONNECTS AS -- not read off a config file (R1).** `current_user` = `session_user` = **`mcplookup_app`**, `rolsuper=false`. Table ownership in `public` splits **8 / 11**: owner **`mcplookup`** (a SUPERUSER) holds `users`, `orgs`, `api_keys`, `api_usage`, `app_stats`, `mcp_llm_axis_scores`, `mcp_score_disputes`, `mcp_server_registry` -- the entire ORIGINAL product schema. Owner **`mcplookup_app`** holds the other 11, **including `alembic_version` itself**: `ask_corpus_index`, `cadence_job_runs`, `perspective_events`, `perspective_snapshots`, `perspectives`, `score_change_events`, `score_change_runs` and more.
- detail: **THE SHAPE IS THE FINDING: THE MIGRATION LANE CAN MIGRATE EXACTLY WHAT IT CREATED, AND NOTHING THAT PREDATES IT.** `ALTER TABLE` requires ownership in Postgres; it is not a GRANT-able privilege. So `alembic upgrade head` running as `mcplookup_app` has always been structurally incapable of altering those eight tables, and structurally capable of altering the rest. **That is why this has never fired in the ten prior migrations** -- 0001..0010 happen to touch only alembic-created tables. `0011_clerk_identity` is the FIRST migration to touch one of the original eight, and it died on its first statement: `psycopg.errors.InsufficientPrivilege: must be owner of table users` on `ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN clerk_id VARCHAR(64)`.
- detail: **A LATENT WALL IS NOT A NEW ONE, AND THE BLAST RADIUS IS THE PRODUCT, NOT THIS FEATURE.** Every future migration touching `users`, `orgs`, `api_keys`, `api_usage`, `app_stats`, `mcp_score_disputes`, `mcp_server_registry` or `mcp_llm_axis_scores` fails identically -- and the last two are the moat's own tables, 469,975 and 1,965,677 rows. So this is not "the Clerk feature is blocked"; it is **the schema-evolution path for the core product has been dead since the second owner appeared, and nothing observed it** because the only observation that discriminates is a migration that happens to touch the old half.
- detail: **THE FAILURE MODE WAS THE SAFE ONE AND THAT IS WORTH RECORDING, because the whole Class-B ATTENDED-ONLY rule exists in fear of the opposite.** Alembic ran under **transactional DDL** and Fly aborts the deployment when `release_command` exits non-zero: no release was created, no machine was swapped, `alembic_version` is still **`0010_canonical_family`**, and there is no partial migration to unwind. `flyctl releases` still tops out at v66. The rollback anchor is the running image, so no rollback was performed or needed. **The IRREVERSIBLE EDGE that makes Class B attended-only did not bite -- but it was one `op.execute` away from a non-transactional statement where it would have.**
- detail: **THE COST OF THE GAP, so it is not filed as a tidy-up.** `select count(*) from users` on the prod moat returns **1**. Three real users exist in Clerk. Signups have been landing in Clerk's browser session and nowhere else, and they will keep doing so until 0011 applies.
- resolution: **DECISION REQUIRED -- THREE OPTIONS, AND I AM NOT PICKING ONE, because it is a privilege change on prod and the chairman is present.** (A) **NARROW, RECOMMENDED**: `ALTER TABLE users OWNER TO mcplookup_app` as `mcplookup`. One table, one statement, reverses in one statement, verifies with one query -- but leaves the other seven armed and this recurs on the next migration that touches them. (B) **CLASS FIX**: reassign all eight, `ALTER TABLE <t> OWNER TO mcplookup_app` x8 (NOT `REASSIGN OWNED BY`, which is unbounded and would also move anything else that role owns). Kills the defect class; touches the moat's two big tables, though ownership transfer is a catalog write and does not rewrite rows. (C) **AVOID THE QUESTION**: run 0011 by hand as `mcplookup` and stamp alembic. Ships the feature tonight, fixes nothing, and guarantees a repeat. **Explicitly NOT on the list: `GRANT mcplookup TO mcplookup_app`.** It would work, and it makes the app role a member of a SUPERUSER -- a standing privilege escalation to solve a one-table problem, which is `new_standing_credentials` and is HELD.
- verify: the privilege itself is not directly queryable, so verify by OWNERSHIP and then by the migration reaching head: `select pg_get_userbyid(relowner) from pg_class where relname='users'` must return **`mcplookup_app`**, and `select version_num from alembic_version` must reach **`0011_clerk_identity`** after the next release. Run the ownership query READ-ONLY inside the machine with `powershell -File D:\zo\_predeploy\step10_dbprobe.ps1` (re-run 2026-08-03T00:50Z: still `mcplookup`). Definitive end-to-end predicate is FU-234's: `python D:\zo\_predeploy\clerk_webhook_live.py` exits 0.
- verify_seen_red: **YES, AND IT IS THE MOTIVATING INCIDENT ITSELF, NOT A LATER REPRODUCTION.** RED measured 18:16:31Z as the release_command's own traceback against live prod Postgres, naming the exact statement. The ownership query was run in the SAME session and is two-sided by construction -- it returned `mcplookup` for `users` and `mcplookup_app` for `alembic_version`, so the probe distinguishes the two owners rather than reporting one value for everything. Confirmed no state moved: `alembic_version` = 0010 after the failure.
- links: [[FU-234]] (the attended fire this aborted), [[FU-039]], [[FU-191]]
- log:
  - 2026-08-02 * **I ALMOST REPORTED THIS AS 'NO DB ACCESS', WHICH IS THE CLAUSE THE CHAIRMAN ADDED TODAY.** The first reading of `InsufficientPrivilege` invites exactly the imagined wall `no_access_is_a_measurement_not_an_assumption` describes. It survived the four required measurements only because they were taken: the exact statement (`ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN clerk_id VARCHAR(64)`), the exact error BODY (`must be owner of table users`, not merely a status), WHICH LAYER produced it (Postgres, not Fly, not the app), and the sanctioned credential path tried (`fetch_secret.py fly` -> `flyctl ssh console` -> the app's own `DATABASE_URL`, never a hardcoded one). **The tell in that clause fired precisely as written: a real permission problem is SELECTIVE.** Eleven tables were writable and eight were not, and it is the selectivity that located the defect in ten minutes instead of producing a false escalation about credentials.

But from that same log the CI database starts as `-e POSTGRES_USER=zo -e POSTGRES_DB=zo_app postgres:16` -- **ONE role, which creates every table in 0001..0010 and therefore OWNS `users` when 0011 alters it.** Prod splits ownership 8/11 across `mcplookup` and `mcplookup_app`. So the pre-flight ran the failing statement AS AN OWNER and prod runs it as a non-owner. **Any migration touching one of the eight `mcplookup`-owned tables has NO pre-flight anywhere that would catch it** -- deploy-compat's green on such a migration is not evidence, it is agreement. ### FU-036 | goose_runner was DEAD for 61.6 hours and nothing detected it
- date: 2026-07-20 · source: daily-chairman-review · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: The builder daemon stopped after `Cycle 586` at **2026-07-17T02:17:29Z** and did not run again until **2026-07-19T15:53:44Z** — **61.6 hours**, with literally zero log lines for the whole of 07-18. Corroborated by three independent surfaces: `goose_runner.log` line counts per day (07-16: 6098, 07-17: 588, 07-18: **0**, 07-19: 1849, 07-20: 2903); a matching 3-day hole in `build_provenance` (rows on 07-16, none 07-17/18/19, 37 on 07-20); and the SAME hole in `mesh_events` (250 rows 07-16, none 07-17/18/19, 139 on 07-20). The mesh_events control read is what distinguishes outage from data loss — both tables stop and resume together, and the daemon log independently confirms the process was not running, so this is an outage, not a lost store. **Nothing alarmed, and three consecutive daily chairman reviews (07-18, 07-19, 07-20-until-now) reported the factory healthy.** Why every existing check missed it: the watchdog restarts named daemons — it restarted `threat_intel_ingestor` today at 11:55Z, so it was alive and working the whole time — but `goose_runner` is evidently not in its supervised set; `zo-sentinel-loop-watch`, the E2E stall watcher built for exactly this, has been **disabled since 2026-06-24** (lastRunAt 06-24T13:09Z, enabled: false); and `pipeline-watch` inspects the Fly app, data freshness and PR hygiene but has no builder-liveness assertion. The common shape is the one the 2026-07-19 reachability postmortem already named: every check we own is **differential or local** ("did the last build pass?", "is this PR green?") and none is a **global liveness invariant** ("has ANY build happened in the last 24h?"). Absence is invisible to a system that only inspects what is present. Note this is the same class as FU-021 (rug_pull_monitor not in the go.sh roster, heartbeat cadence a lie) — that entry found one uncovered daemon; this one shows the coverage gap reaches the single most important daemon in the mesh. Cost: ~2.5 days of factory throughput, silently. Fix landing today: `factory_liveness_continuity_probe.py` and `daemon_roster_coverage_report.py` lanes in the PHASE 9 anchor (#1670) — the first asserts FACTORY_DEAD / CONTINUITY_HOLE from build_provenance and reports UNKNOWN rather than OK when it cannot read, the second reports UNSUPERVISED (running, in roster, not watchdog-supervised) which is goose_runner's exact 07-17 state. Neither is wired to an alarm yet — that is the open half of this entry. Also open and NOT decided: whether `zo-sentinel-loop-watch` should simply be re-enabled (it was built for this and is 4 weeks dormant — find out WHY it was disabled before re-enabling it, or the same reason will disable it again).
- verify: `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\zo_probe.py" --sql "SELECT EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (now()-last_heartbeat)) FROM service_health WHERE service='goose_runner'" --assert "v is not None and float(v)<900"`  # goose_runner heartbeat under 15 min -- the 61.6h blindness cannot recur silently
- verify_seen_red: NEVER
- log:
  - 2026-07-20 triage: two facts pinned down that the entry left as assertions. (1) `zo-sentinel-loop-watch` confirmed from the scheduler: `enabled: false`, `lastRunAt 2026-06-24T13:09:31Z`, cron `0 * * * *` -- so it is dormant exactly as described, and the entry's instruction to find out WHY before re-enabling still stands (nothing in the 48h sweep explains the 06-24 disable). (2) The watchdog-roster fix is the same defect as FU-021(a) -- both are 'the watchdog can only restart what it is told about' -- and `daemon_roster_coverage_report.py` (PHASE 9, #1670) is the shared measurement for both. Suggest FU-021(a) and this entry's roster half be implemented as ONE change to go.sh/watchdog config rather than twice; that edit is shared launch infra and stays attended per FU-021.
- resolution:

### FU-058 | 99.5% of the scored corpus is HIGH or CRITICAL — the risk tier carries almost no information
- date: 2026-07-21 · source: zo-sentinel-pipeline-watch · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: Straight from today's completed rescore, `backfill.log` for run `20260719-003024`: tier distribution across the 172,295 scored representatives is **CRITICAL 65,269 · HIGH 106,118 · MEDIUM 854 · LOW 54**. That is **99.47% HIGH-or-CRITICAL**, with LOW at 0.03% — fifty-four servers out of a hundred and seventy-two thousand. The registry-wide view agrees (`HIGH 106,117 · CRITICAL 65,269 · unassessed 59,915 · MEDIUM 851 · LOW 54`), so this is not a backfill artifact. **Why this is P1 and not a tuning nit:** the product's stated position is that we sell provenance or we sell nothing, and a tier that assigns the same verdict to essentially every server cannot discriminate — a customer filtering for "show me the risky ones" gets the entire corpus back. Every downstream surface that ranks, filters, badges or alerts on tier is inheriting a constant. It also means the 50%-of-corpus re-rating in FU-059 is happening *within* a band that was already saturated, so neither number is currently defensible on its own. **Three candidate causes, in the order they should be separated, none yet tested:** (1) the axis-to-tier aggregation thresholds are mis-set, so realistic axis scores are being funneled into the top two bands — cheapest to check, since it needs no model work, just the tier cut-points against the axis-score histogram; (2) the student model itself is mode-collapsed toward high risk on this corpus, which the SFT leaderboard's acceptance bar (`decisions/v2_1_acceptance_bar.json` in `rob531/zomesh-sentinel-sft`) may or may not have been designed to catch — worth checking whether the bar scores calibration at all or only per-axis accuracy; (3) the corpus genuinely is that dangerous, which is the null hypothesis and should be stated explicitly rather than assumed away, but a 54-server LOW tail makes it hard to believe without evidence. **Recommended first step, deliberately small:** dump the distribution of `overall_risk` axis values (not tiers) and the tier cut-points, and see whether the axis scores are themselves bimodal or whether a spread is being flattened at the threshold layer. That single query distinguishes (1) from (2) and needs no GPU spend. Related: `improve_*_discrimination` directives have appeared and been quarantined in the builder lane before, so the concern has surfaced from the factory side without ever reaching a decision.
- verify: `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\zo_probe.py" --sql "SELECT ROUND(100.0*COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE risk_tier IN ('HIGH','CRITICAL'))/NULLIF(COUNT(*),0),2) FROM server_scores" --assert "v is not None and float(v)<90"`  # HIGH+CRITICAL below 90% -- the tier carries information again
- verify_seen_red: NEVER
- log:
  - 2026-07-21 daily-chairman-review: lifted into the builder lane as `risk_tier_threshold_calibration_probe.py` in the PHASE 10 anchor (spec PR #1702, MERGED, deployed to the runtime by single-file checkout). The lane implements this entry's own recommended first step verbatim -- dump the `overall_risk` axis LABEL histogram alongside the tier histogram and the cut-points, and return THRESHOLD_SUSPECT (axis labels spread, tiers collapsed -- a flattening at the mapping layer, fixable with zero model work) vs MODEL_SUSPECT (axis labels themselves >90% on one class -- mode collapse, which belongs with the SFT acceptance bar) vs BOTH vs UNKNOWN. Spec requires reading class enums from the `schemas/risk_axis_mapping_v1.json` contract rather than inferring them from observed values, per the auth_strength-has-4-classes scar. Diagnoses only: changes no thresholds, rescores nothing, spends nothing. Independently raised in council today -- Seat 2 (product integrity) argued this is the larger falsehood: "I hold the ratchet flip because it is cheap and stops the bleeding. I concede it is not the biggest lie we are telling."
- resolution:

### FU-065 | The runtime checkout is chronically behind main, and the deploy task's own description contradicts its cron
- date: 2026-07-21 · source: daily-chairman-review · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: Two separable defects that compound into the starvation floor being unable to tell exhaustion from staleness. **(1) The schedule drift.** The `deploy-runtime-from-main` scheduled task's description reads "**Every 3h**: deploy latest merged main to the zo-sentinel runtime (refresh_code) so spec/code changes actually reach the architect" — but its `cronExpression` is `0 5 * * *`, i.e. **once daily**. Whichever is intended, one of them is wrong, and the description is the thing a human reads when deciding whether staleness is plausible. **(2) The consequence, observed today.** The runtime sat at `HEAD=17304059` from the 09:09Z deploy while `origin/main` advanced through at least `5a83b52b` → `b5029745` → `2ac29b56` → `37bfa904` during a single review — the factory merges ~15 PRs/day against a runtime refreshed once. **Why this is more than untidiness:** the gaps map is `PRODUCT_SPEC − disk`, computed on that stale checkout, and the starvation floor's own message admits it cannot resolve the ambiguity: `"gaps map is EXHAUSTED ... CHECKOUT: HEAD=17304059 != origin/main=b5029745 (behind by an uncounted number of commits (no local fetch)) -- the anchor may NOT be spent; this checkout may simply predate the refill. DEPLOY FIRST (safe_ff.sh), then re-check before extending the spec."` It emits an exhaustion claim and a staleness caveat in the same breath with no way to choose between them — so "the anchor is spent" is currently an *unfalsifiable* assertion, and today it happened to be true only because a human went and checked. `runtime_checkout_drift_probe.py` (PHASE 10, spec merged #1702) makes the choice observable: a checkout behind main by a commit that touched `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` **cannot honestly report exhaustion**. **Not yet fixed here, deliberately:** changing the deploy cadence touches deploy config, which is a guardrailed surface; the description/cron contradiction should be resolved by whoever owns the task, and the direction (raise the cron to 3h, or correct the description to "daily") is a real decision, not a typo fix. Related: [[FU-060]] (the sibling assertion-not-measurement defect in the same log line).

- log:

The description/cron contradiction is untouched (description says "Every 3h", `cronExpression` is `0 5 * * *`), no behind-by alarm exists, and the drift will recur by tomorrow exactly as it did today. Today it cost a fired halt condition and 12 red PRs (FU-068); the cost was paid in full and the mechanism that produced it is unchanged. `runtime_checkout_drift_probe.py` merged today (#1706) and makes the drift *observable*, which is necessary and not sufficient — it does not deploy. ### FU-075 | COMPETITIVE THREAT: conduid.com is a direct MCP-trust-scoring rival with a better shopfront and a two-sided flywheel we lack
- date: 2026-07-22 · source: chairman (attended) — competitive-intel sweep of conduid.com · status: open · priority: P1
- class: directive
- detail: **Read before FU-076..079 — they are the response to this.** conduid.com ("The Trust Layer for AI Agents") does the same core thing mcprisky/mcplookup does: indexes MCP servers and assigns a 0–100 **Trust Score** (credit-score framing; bands 80–100 production-ready / 50–79 caution / 0–49 high risk). Live count **46.7K servers scored and climbing** (the homepage ticker rose 45.7K→46.7K within one session). Scores on 6 legible, largely **deterministic** trust signals — Authentication (OAuth/API-keys), Transport security (HTTPS/TLS), **Maintenance** (commit frequency, issue response), Documentation, License, **Permissions** (over-permissioned / scoped) — plus per-server boolean badges (Has License, Has Tests, Scoped Permissions, Pinned Dependencies, No Eval Usage). Also ships what we do not: a **ConduID cryptographic-identity protocol** (verified ownership boosts score up to 15 pts), a **claim-your-server builder flywheel** (GitHub-OAuth ownership → analytics dashboard, editable listing, "prepare for monetization"), a free open JSON API (`/api/v2/servers`, `/servers/:slug` incl. security audit, `/stats`, Meilisearch `/search`), an SDK install-tracking endpoint, 27 categories, and an "Ask AI about <server>" Claude-grounded chat. **When they stood it up:** the domain is old (Wayback 2009–2014) but dormant since 2014, and the CURRENT product has ZERO Wayback captures = too new to have been crawled (weeks old). Founder `ill-ion` (old GH account, id ~6.1M; production repo private) public trail: `rcpt-protocol` ("Receipt Chain Protocol for Trust — cryptographic proof-of-action for AI agents") created 2026-03-30; `condu-id` (Astro landing) created 2026-05-10, pushed 2026-05-12. So concept seeded ~late-March, brand ~mid-May, live scored marketplace after that and growing now. `@ConduidHQ` on X; `github.com/ill-ion`. **Where we stand:** OUR edge is corpus scale — ~232,206 registry / ~172,295 scored, ≈**5×** their 46.7K — plus discovery, EXCEPT discovery has collapsed to ~2/day (FU-054), so the 5× lead is NOT compounding. THEIR edge is (a) **axis legibility** — separate deterministic signals vs our single LLM risk-tier that carries almost no information (FU-058: 99.5% HIGH/CRITICAL), (b) a **far better shopfront** (ticker, credit-score frame, animated scorecard hero, top-rated scroll — vs our flopped 200K robot campaign, FU-041), and (c) a **builder-side flywheel** (claim/verify/analytics/monetize) we have no equivalent to. **Why "mortal":** their advantages are all copyable in OUR direction (legible axes, landing, flywheel = engineering/design), whereas our corpus lead is copyable in THEIRS only slowly — and with our discovery dead (FU-054) they close the corpus gap while we stand still on presentation and flywheel. The positioning overlap is near-verbatim: our "200K MCPs SCORED FOR YOU / KNOW BEFORE YOU CONNECT" vs their "servers scored / Know what you're connecting to — before it breaks, leaks, or fails" — not provably copied (their public trail predates our July robot campaign; convergent framing), but it means we do not even own the messaging. Related: [[FU-076]] (legible axes), [[FU-077]] (landing), [[FU-078]] (flywheel), [[FU-079]] (intel watch), [[FU-058]], [[FU-054]], [[FU-041]].

### FU-076 | Adopt a legible multi-axis scorecard (deterministic signals) — conduid parity AND the fix for FU-058
- date: 2026-07-22 · source: chairman (attended) — conduid competitive assessment · status: in-progress · priority: P1
- class: directive
- detail: The single most strategically urgent product gap, because it is BOTH competitive parity and the fix for a standing P1 truth defect. conduid scores on separate, mostly-deterministic signals and shows them as a per-server scorecard; we blend everything into one LLM risk-tier that FU-058 proves is non-discriminating (99.5% of the corpus HIGH/CRITICAL — the tier tells a user nothing). Proposal: compute and EXPOSE a small set of cheap, individually-legible axes alongside (not replacing) the LLM axes — candidate set from what conduid surfaces and what we can derive from repo/manifest metadata we likely already ingest: **maintenance** (last-commit age, commit frequency, issue-response), **permissions/scopes** (declared vs least-privilege; over-permissioned flag), **tests present**, **pinned dependencies**, **no `eval`/dynamic-exec**, **transport security** (TLS/HTTPS), **auth method**, **license present**, and our existing **has_known_cve** facet (FU-005, already wired). Each is a boolean or a bounded number a user can read WITHOUT trusting a model — precisely what "CRITICAL on 99.5% of servers" fails to give. The per-signal extractors are builder-lane-shaped (schema-bounded, one module each — same shape as the has_known_cve facet); the SCORING-MODEL change (how axes roll into the headline number/tier, and re-tiering 232K servers) is a spec/CofC decision and must sequence with FU-058's `risk_tier_threshold_calibration_probe` — do NOT hand-build that. Cheap safe first step: pick the 3–4 signals already computable from ingested metadata, ship them as report-only facets, and measure their distribution (unlike the tier, these should actually spread). Related: [[FU-058]], [[FU-075]], [[FU-005]] (has_known_cve precedent), builder_lane.md.
- log:
  - 2026-07-22 attended (conduid response, git lane): **step 1 SHIPPED + MERGED — PR #1742 (squash `dffde78`), all CI green (both pytest jobs, capmap-check ratchet +0, no-hollow, smoke-ladder).** Built `deterministic_signal_report.py` (root, no router → ratchet-safe; copies the working vuln-facet convention, NOT the broken `*_report.py` stubs): a REPORT-ONLY module over `mcp_server_registry` giving the distribution of the deterministic signals derivable from stored columns TODAY — transport (https/http), public-repo presence, our scan-recency buckets, and community-signal coverage in the free-form `meta` blob — printed beside `risk_tier`/`verdict` with the tier's combined HIGH|CRITICAL `elevated_share` (the exact FU-058 number). No score change, no re-tier, no route, pure read; `__main__` self-test + `tests/test_deterministic_signal_report.py` (3 tests) pass. **Key finding from the data-model map (Explore subagent):** the four signals this entry named — repo maintenance-age, scoped-permissions, tests-present, pinned-dependencies — are NOT in the app Postgres tier; they live only in the builder DuckDB (`github_velocity`, `mcp_fingerprints`, `mcp_registry_facts`) and were never migrated, so step 1 measures what we CAN compute and the module's `SIGNALS_NEEDING_INGESTION` constant names the gap → ledgered as **FU-080**. **Still open on THIS entry:** the scoring-MODEL change (how legible axes roll into the headline number/tier + re-tiering 232K) remains a spec/CofC decision sequenced with FU-058's calibration probe — do NOT hand-build. Status → in-progress.
  - 2026-07-22 attended (conduid response, git lane): **CVE axis added — PR #1743 (squash `f26a8758`), all CI green (both pytest, capmap-check +0, no-hollow, smoke-ladder).** Folded `has_known_cve` into the scorecard as a deterministic SECURITY axis computed from `vuln_links` membership (the exact linkage the existing has_known_cve facet already uses) — no ingestion, no LLM, no score/re-tier/route change; defensive (omitted, not raised, if `vuln_links` is absent). The report now carries FOUR deterministic axes (transport, has_public_repo, has_known_cve, scan_recency) beside the collapsed risk_tier. The marquee four (maintenance/scopes/tests/pinned-deps) still need FU-080 ingestion; the scoring-model roll-up stays CofC.
  - 2026-07-24 triage: scorecard is now **4/7 live** - the maintenance-age axis landed 07-23 as PR #1758 (computed report-side from `mcp_server_registry.meta`, no ingestion/migration/re-tier; tracked on [[FU-080]]), joining transport, public-repo and has_known_cve. The 3 remaining marquee axes (scoped-permissions, tests-present, pinned-deps) genuinely need ingestion (FU-080) and the scoring-model roll-up/re-tier stays CofC (sequenced with FU-058's calibration probe). ### FU-024 | Nightly moat backup has no retry window or staleness alarm when the Fly control plane is down
- date: 2026-07-20 - source: mcplookup-nightly-db-backup - status: open - priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: On 2026-07-20 the nightly backup could not run at all. flyctl v0.4.68 was authed (whoami = robin.craib@gmail.com, config.yml written 03:14:32) but EVERY org/app-scoped call failed: GraphQL 500/503 on apps/orgs/status, Machines API "authenticate: token validation error: no verified tokens; token 4f3a3f26-836a-5f62-96da-36f70948cb0d: verify: context deadline exceeded", wireguard agent "Not authorized to show this organization::feature", metrics 401. Fly status page showed All Systems Operational throughout, so status.flyio.net is NOT a usable signal for this failure mode. Retried 4x over ~12min with an agent stop/start, no recovery. Prod itself was serving (mcplookup.app / => 200), so this was control-plane only, not an app outage. Net effect: zero backup taken and the only thing that surfaced it was this report. Durable fix: (a) retry with exponential backoff across a wider window instead of failing the night on first error, (b) an age alarm that fires whenever the newest backup is >36h old regardless of cause, so a silent multi-night gap cannot accumulate, (c) classify "flyctl/control-plane unreachable" as its own alert distinct from "backup ran but failed verification".

- verify_seen_red: NEVER

The new FU-025 audit tool then found a SECOND, older hole nobody had noticed: `zo_sentinel_20260714T071105Z` exists but has **no manifest.json** -- an incomplete backup that has been sitting in the retention window since 7/14 looking like a valid restore point. That is the same shape as this entry's own finding (a gap that only surfaces when something explicitly looks for it), and it is an argument for the (b) age-alarm being an *integrity* alarm too: 'newest backup <36h old' would not have caught 7/14 either, because the directory exists. Suggest the alarm assert a readable manifest with restore_verify.ok, not merely a fresh directory. No spend, no writes, nothing pruned.
  - 2026-07-21 mcplookup-nightly-db-backup: **control plane recovered on its own; the gap was exactly one night, and again nothing but this report noticed.** flyctl authed and answered first try tonight -- DSN pulled from `mcplookup` and the `mcplookup-db` proxy came up on 15432 within 6s, no retries needed. Backup `zo_sentinel_20260721T071025Z` taken and verified (restore_verify.ok=true, 1,206,065 rows). So the 7/20 failure was transient control-plane, self-healing, and needed no intervention -- which is precisely the argument FOR fix (a): a backoff window of even ~30min would likely have caught it, and instead the night was lost. **What is unchanged and is the actual open scope here: fixes (a), (b) and (c) all still do not exist.** The 7/20 hole surfaced on 7/20 only because a human read the report, and it surfaced again tonight only because this run happened to list the directory and see 0719 -> 0721 with nothing between. A one-night gap that self-heals is the benign version; the same blindness passes a five-night gap identically. The dir sequence is now `...0718, 0719, [MISSING 0720], 0721` and that hole is permanent -- there is no backfill path for a night's snapshot. Also confirms the integrity half of the (b) suggestion is still needed: tonight's `backup_select.py audit` re-reported `zo_sentinel_20260714T071105Z` NO_MANIFEST (verdict FINDINGS, exit 1) -- 7 days after discovery it is still sitting in the window looking like a restore point, because reporting it is not the same as alarming on it. Note the 7/14 dir is now 7 days old and will become prune-eligible at 14 days, i.e. it will be silently deleted by an attended prune rather than ever being repaired or explained.
  - 2026-07-22 mcplookup-nightly-db-backup: **a NEW transient failure mode for fix (a) that self-recovered via retry — but step 1 as written would have false-ALERTed and lost the night.** The DSN fetch returned EMPTY on the first `flyctl ssh console -a mcplookup -C "printenv DATABASE_URL"`, then `Error: host unavailable at 7813ee2a33e978: host was not found in DNS` on the retry — a stale local WireGuard/6PN **agent** tunnel, NOT a control-plane outage: `flyctl auth whoami` and `fly status -a mcplookup` both answered correctly throughout (app had a started machine 7813ee2a33e978 + a stopped one). Per step 1 this empty/errored DSN triggers ALERT "flyctl not authed or mcplookup unreachable" and STOP — a **false failure**, since flyctl was authed and prod was reachable; only SSH's internal DNS was stale. Fix was a single `flyctl agent restart` + ~4s, after which the DSN pulled clean and the full backup ran (`zo_sentinel_20260722T071204Z`, restore_verify.ok=true, 1,206,065 rows, elapsed 212.3s). Two sharpenings for the durable fixes: (a)'s retry must include **`flyctl agent restart`**, not a bare re-invoke — a plain retry does not clear a stale agent DNS cache; (c)'s classifier must treat *empty-DSN / "host not found in DNS" while whoami+status succeed* as "transient local tunnel → restart-agent + retry", distinct from both "control-plane unreachable → alert" and "backup verify failed". Still no (b) age/integrity alarm: tonight's PASS surfaced only via this report, and `audit` again re-flagged `zo_sentinel_20260714T071105Z` NO_MANIFEST (now 8 days old, prune-eligible in 6). Housekeeping: pruned the sole >14-day candidate `zo_sentinel_20260708T071034Z` (valid manifest) since step 5 PASSED.
  - 2026-07-23 mcplookup-nightly-db-backup: **PASS** — `zo_sentinel_20260723T071122Z`, restore_verify.ok=true, mcp_llm_axis_scores=1,206,065 rows (identical to 7/22, 0% delta, same sha256 300e2bd0… — corpus SATURATED per FU-055), mcp_server_registry 234,047→234,062 (+15). prune-candidates at 14d = 0, nothing deleted. **(a)/(b)/(c) still do not exist**, and the integrity half of (b) earned its keep again: `audit` re-flagged `zo_sentinel_20260714T071105Z` NO_MANIFEST (verdict FINDINGS, exit 1) — now 9 days old, prune-eligible ~07-28, so it will be silently deleted rather than repaired/explained, exactly as this entry predicted on 7/21. NEW this run, and now the strongest case for (c) being an *in-progress* classification → see **FU-081**: the inline dump ran ~170s but the MCP transport caps ~60-90s, so step 4 returned a TIMEOUT mid-dump and a mid-flight `audit` false-flagged this run's OWN in-progress dir as NO_MANIFEST until the orphaned python wrote the manifest. Handled here by polling proc-liveness + manifest presence before verifying; the SKILL as written has no such guard.
  - 2026-07-25 mcplookup-nightly-db-backup: **FAIL (transport) — backup ran but produced an all-tables-errored manifest; no fresh restore point tonight.** flyctl authed (whoami=robin.craib@gmail.com), `mcplookup` reachable (machine 7813ee2a33e978 started), `mcplookup-db` up/primary (its 1 CRITICAL check is the known-benign disk/work_mem headroom per the prod-PG reframe, NOT an outage), and the DSN pulled clean on retry — so this is NEITHER the 7/20 control-plane mode NOR the 7/22 stale-agent-DNS mode. Failure is **flycast proxy throughput degradation**: the `mcp_llm_axis_scores` COPY stalled for ~4.3h (manifest `elapsed_sec=15543.4`) until Fly PG closed the connection (`OperationalError: server closed the connection unexpectedly`), cascading `connection is closed` to the other four tables; restore_verify.ok absent. Confirmed independently with a bounded probe — COPY of just 200k rows ran >3min and never completed over a freshly-restarted proxy. **Compounding factor, reported factually not alarmingly (growth is FU-089's beat, not this task's):** the moat came off its long saturation — live direct count `mcp_llm_axis_scores=1,946,182` (was 1,206,065 on 7/22–7/24; +740k/+61%, consistent with the registry doubling to 462,760 on 7/24), so the COPY payload is now ~60% heavier just as the tunnel degraded. **Moat verified INTACT despite the failed drill:** live count 1,946,182 >> 400k floor and UP vs prior (no data-loss), and the last-good backup `zo_sentinel_20260724T071136Z` is valid (restore_verify.ok=true, 1,206,065, expected==restored). The script's documented fast alternative — ZoComputer's direct 6PN — was UNAVAILABLE (`mcplookup-db.flycast` will not resolve on zocomputer; its Fly WireGuard peer is down). Per step 7, prune SKIPPED (no fresh backup): the 3 eligible >14d dirs (0710x2, 0711) left in place. This is the **transport variant of fix (c)**: "backup ran but COPY transport failed/stalled" must classify distinctly from verify-failure and from control-plane-down; and it is the strongest case yet for **(b)** an age/integrity alarm — newest valid manifest is now 7/24 (~33h old and climbing, will cross 36h before tomorrow's run if this recurs). 7/14 NO_MANIFEST re-flagged again (now 11 days old, prune-eligible ~07-28). Durable-fix candidate specific to this mode: for the ~2M-row table stop streaming a full COPY through the local flyctl proxy — run pg_dump server-side via `flyctl ssh` and scp the artifact, OR add a per-COPY transfer/statement timeout with resumable chunking, so a degraded tunnel fails fast and retries instead of stalling 4h into a poisoned manifest.

<!-- FU-072 status=open priority=P1 filed=2026-07-21 last_touch=2026-07-25 stale_days=39 -->
### FU-072 | STEER: files → services (SOA). The smaller mount/KL follow-ups are sub-tasks of one program, not independent edits
- date: 2026-07-21 · source: chairman (attended) · status: open · priority: P1
- class: directive
- detail: **This is a steering entry, not a task. Read it before actioning FU-039, FU-069, FU-070, FU-071, FU-058, FU-053 — they are facets of ONE design and must not be implemented as scattered point-fixes, or they will conflict.** Full design: `D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\SOA_SERVICE_REGISTRY_DESIGN_2026-07-21.md`, written for the 07-23 mount-lane review (FU-039). The thesis: move the atomic unit of work from a FILE to a SERVICE, but **without** asking the single-file builder to do multi-file wiring (the 2026-06-26 re-scope exists precisely because multi-file judgment work produced 142 hollow PRs). Mechanism: a fixed, human-authored, **fail-loud** folder-scanner in `app/main.py` (a generalization of the `_OPTIONAL_ROUTERS` loop it already has) mounts every service in `services/active/`; the builder drops self-contained service dirs into `services/staged/`; **`staged → active` promotion is the reachability gate, and it reuses the existing `proposed → pending → done` promoter topology** rather than new machinery. The folder scan beats `mounts.toml` on the CofC's own objection: presence in `active/` IS registration, so there is no second list to go stale. **HARD REQUIREMENT carried from the reachability postmortem:** the scan must RAISE in CI and surface failed mounts on `/version` + `mesh_events` in prod — inheriting `main.py`'s current `except Exception: pass` would rebuild the exact invisibility bug this whole line of work exists to kill.
  **The DB reframe (the reason this is P1 and actionable now):** the "prod PG is 3/3 CRITICAL, mounting 259 data-layer routes will kill it" premise — which is load-bearing in the CofC deferral (FU-064), FU-071, FU-053 and the `query_kl` design — is **mostly a misread.** Checked the live instance 07-21 (`fly status/checks/volumes -a mcplookup-db`): all 3 Fly health checks PASS; connections 17/300, CPU load 0.09, memory "0s/60s waiting" — the DB is **idle** on compute. The only real pressures are **disk 75.5% of 3GB** (read-only at 90% — this breaks first) and **`work_mem` 4MB** (the 512MB-RAM default — why big sorts spill to disk). Both are ~$9/mo to fix: `fly volumes extend` 3→20GB (+~$2.55/mo) and `fly machine update --vm-memory 2048` + raise `work_mem`→~48MB (+~$7.50/mo), taking the DB to ~$13/mo total. **Consequence for every entry that cites the interlock:** once sized up, admission control becomes a question of CORRECTNESS (is this the right service, is it a dupe) not SURVIVAL (will it take down prod). The graveyard was conflating those because the DB was too small to separate them. This does NOT license "mount all 246" — 22 are outright dupes and some do real full-table aggregations — but it removes resource-panic as the reason to hold a service back. **The DB resize (Step 0) is chairman-approved and should ship this week independent of the SOA decision; it is guardrailed deploy-config, so it needs the owner to run it, not the triage task.**
  **How the smaller FUs slot in (this is the steer):**
    - **FU-039** (07-23 mount review) — this doc IS its primary input; the review rules on folder-scan vs mounts.toml vs status quo, and on the staged→active *policy* (auto-on-green vs human-gated first cohort — recommend human-gated first, mirroring the ratchet's observe→enforce).
    - **FU-069** (dedup on filename) — fixed structurally by the promotion gate's collision check against `app_surface_kl.routes.taken_paths`, NOT by a separate dedup patch. The 22 `duplicate_paths` are the review's delete-candidate starting sample.
    - **FU-070** (architect prompt self-contradiction on wiring) — resolves itself under SOA: "wiring" ceases to exist as builder work (the spine auto-absorbs `active/`), so the contradictory sentence is simply deleted, and the architect's job becomes allocate-a-route-from-`render_for_architect()`. Do NOT hand-patch the recipe before the review; the correct second half of the sentence depends on what the review adopts.
    - **FU-071** (KL surfaces) — the route/mount/consumer/data half shipped (#1722); the remaining `query_kl` "no-unbounded-scans" linter should be built AGAINST the sized-up `work_mem`, i.e. its threshold is a function of Step 0, so sequence it after the resize.
    - **FU-058** (99.5% HIGH/CRITICAL — risk tier carries no info) and **FU-053** (PG at capacity) — FU-053 is substantially down-graded by the DB reframe above (it is a disk+work_mem sizing issue, not a wall); FU-058 is orthogonal to SOA and stays its own product-truth track.
  **Topology clarification (added 2026-07-22, chairman) — settle this before the review so it is not re-derived:** three planes operate on the SAME git tree from opposite ends and never need a network call between them. **Factory** (arch-goose, builder-goose, `graph_refresh`/Graphify, promoter) runs on **ZoComputer** against the git checkout. **KL emission** (`app_surface_kl`, `schema_kl`) runs on **ZoComputer via graph_refresh AND in GitHub Actions** — it reads the repo file tree, not the live app (`app_surface_kl.py` makes ZERO network calls — verified). **Spine** (`app/main.py` + `services/active/`) runs on **Fly (prod)** and at boot mounts whatever the merged repo shipped. Chain: `services/*` folders in git → Graphify reads the tree (ZoComputer) → KL → architect allocates / builder writes (ZoComputer) → PR → CI → merge → deploy to Fly. **So the spine running on Fly does NOT remove or bypass the Graphify KL** — the KL is built UPSTREAM of Fly, from the git tree, and is what tells the builder how to write the service that becomes the spine's cargo. The source of truth for "what services exist" is `services/active/` in git, never the live app. **The one real fork (now in §6.5 of the design doc): runtime discovery (Option A — `main.py` scans the folder at boot on Fly) vs build-time generation (Option B — a runner/GHA step reads `services/active/` and generates a static, fail-loud mount file that prod runs).** Given this system's failure history is entirely *silent runtime* faults, **Option B is recommended** — and it unifies the loop: the same build-time pass that generates the spine's mount manifest also emits the app_surface KL (one generator, two outputs, both from the repo, neither needing Fly). This directly answers "could the spine be a runner/GHA workflow" — under Option B, yes, and that is the safer shape. The review rules on A vs B.
  **Reference implementation landed (2026-07-22, chairman-attended, PR #1729 squash `3c1564d`):** `tools/spine_manifest.py` — a REPORT-ONLY working reference of Option B, so the 07-23 review rules on a running mechanism not prose. It scans the current live mounts (via app_surface_kl, no hand-list), emits `artifacts/spine_manifest.json` + `artifacts/spine_mounts.generated.py` (a preview of the fail-loud include prod would run), and validates every entry — fail-loud under `--strict`, report-only (exit 0) by default. It mounts nothing, touches no `app/main.py`, creates no `services/active/`; the A-vs-B decision and the move of the 31 live services stay the review's call. **Real finding it surfaced:** of the ~33 current LIVE mounts, **4 carry a duplicate route** (`app/main`, `verdict_breakdown_api`, `verdict_view_api`, `server_axis_scores_summary_router`) — so FU-069's duplicate class is present in the *live* set, not just the graveyard; `--strict` would block these. Lives in `tools/` deliberately (both gates scope to root-level `.py`, so a tool *about* routers is exempt there — avoids the raw-text scar app_surface_kl hit twice at root). 7 tests pass; armed ratchet stayed green (`effective=277 delta=+0`). This is the observe-first half; flipping `--strict` + having `app/main.py` adopt the generated file is the review's enforce step.
  **Guardrails for whoever implements:** Step 0 (DB) is a deploy action — owner only. Steps 1–2 (fail-loud folder scan + move the 31 live modules into `active/`) are pure refactor of running code and are the safe first build. Steps 3 & 5 (builder emits service dirs; inject `render_for_architect` into `context_json`) are agent-behaviour changes — gated, last, and NOT to be slipped in alongside an artifact PR. Step 6 (delete the dupes/dead) is the only hard-to-reverse step — do it last, deliberately, off the `duplicate_paths` list. Related: [[FU-039]], [[FU-064]], [[FU-069]], [[FU-070]], [[FU-071]], [[FU-053]], [[FU-058]].
- log:
  - 2026-07-22 chairman (attended): **Step 0 (DB resize) EXECUTED and verified.** flyctl is authenticated on the tower as robin.craib@gmail.com — no AgentVault key needed (a fly token does exist in AgentVault under `fly`, unused). Three changes to `mcplookup-db` (machine `784563efde3738`, volume `vol_4ql0xz1278zxejnr`, region ewr): (1) `fly volumes extend ... -s 20` → 3GB→20GB, **online, no restart**, disk-capacity 75.5%→15.5%; (2) `fly machine update ... --vm-memory 2048` → 512MB→2048MB (stays shared-cpu-1x, its max RAM), one clean ~30s restart, health 3/3 passing after; (3) `work_mem` 4MB→**48MB** via `ALTER SYSTEM SET work_mem='48MB'` + `pg_reload_conf()` (live, no restart). The RAM bump auto-scaled the flex tuning: `shared_buffers`→512MB, `effective_cache_size`→4GB. **Verified:** mcprisky.io/health returns `{"status":"ok",...}` after the restart (app reconnected clean); DB connections 7/300, memory not waiting. Cost ≈ **$13.70/mo** (20GB×$0.15 + shared-cpu-1x-2GB ~$10.70), the "$13 model" requested. **This closes the resource-survival objection in FU-064/FU-053** — admission control for the SOA mount lane is now a correctness question, not a load question. Two scars for anyone driving fly from the tower: `fly ssh console -C` mangles nested quotes through PowerShell→fly→bash (base64-encode the remote script and `echo B64 | base64 -d | bash`), and `fly pg connect` hangs on piped stdin; both, plus any fly connect command, exceed the ~45s shell timeout, so background them and poll. ### FU-107 | Nightly moat backup INFEASIBLE at current table size — COPY ~68 rows/s => ~8h
- date: 2026-07-26 · source: mcplookup-nightly-db-backup · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: The nightly backup+restore-verify drill (backup_zo_sentinel.py) FAILS silently once `mcp_llm_axis_scores` grew past ~1.2M rows. Measured tonight through the flyctl proxy tunnel (localhost:15542 -> mcplookup-db.flycast:5432): `select count(*)` = **1,946,182 rows** but took **42.1s** (nearly the 45s statement_timeout); a low-memory streaming `COPY ... TO STDOUT` gzip probe moved only **~5,000 rows in 74s (~68 rows/s)**, projecting **~8h** for the full table. The script buffers the ENTIRE table into an in-memory `io.StringIO` and sets NO statement_timeout on the COPY, so the run neither completes nor fails loud — it just hangs at 0 files written. This is why there is **no backup dir for 2026-07-25** and none tonight: the drill has been quietly broken since the table crossed the size threshold. Last GOOD backup = **zo_sentinel_20260724T071136Z** (moat=1,206,065 rows, restore_ok=true, elapsed 146.5s). The moat grew **+61% (740k rows) in 2 days** (1.206M on 7/24 -> 1.946M on 7/26) — consistent with the 7/24 registry doubling + a score-wave. Root bottleneck is SERVER-SIDE (1GB Fly PG, work_mem 4MB, heavy per-axis TOASTed columns detoasting under memory pressure), so no client-side change fixes throughput.
- verify: `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\zo_probe.py" --sql "SELECT EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (now()-MAX(created_at)))/3600 FROM mesh_events WHERE event_type LIKE '%backup%' AND event_type NOT LIKE '%fail%'" --assert "v is not None and float(v)<36"`  # a SUCCEEDING backup inside the nightly window, i.e. COPY throughput is feasible

Audit verdict FINDINGS: pre-existing NO_MANIFEST on zo_sentinel_20260714T071105Z (old incomplete 7/14 backup — candidate to sweep).  - 2026-07-26 07:54Z prod-drift-sentinel CONFIRMS from the deploy vantage: logical moat backup is STALE. CORRECTS the 01:20Z/04:50Z prod-drift 'backup fresh' claim - those cited the Fly block-level snapshot vs_LbVYJyj6ZNLotgenGDjlB, NOT this restore-verified logical drill. Not separately emailed: the deploy is already STAGE-BLOCKED on the FU-102 COPY gaps, and THIS run's migration is a GREEN no-op (prod at head 0010, release_command won't mutate the moat), so the stale logical backup is not gating this deploy's data safety; FU-107 owns the fix.
  - 2026-07-26 22:55Z prod-drift-sentinel: **FU-107 APPEARS RESOLVED - please confirm and close.** `db_backups/backup_zo_sentinel.py` was rewritten today and a **284.3MB** dump `moat_preimport_20260726T222832Z.dump` landed **22:38Z** with a valid `PGDMP` custom-format header - the first successful moat backup since 7/24. The rewrite's own docstring **refutes this FU's founding premise on both counts**: the Fly PG box is **2GB RAM with work_mem 48MB** (not 1GB/4MB), and a **server-side** `pg_dump -Fc -Z6` of the entire 2.9GB database - including the 1.9M-row moat - completes in **~2 MINUTES** producing ~285MB. The ~68 rows/s wall was never server-side capacity: it was the per-row CSV round-trip over a `flyctl proxy` TCP tunnel plus a 38-hour runaway analytics query saturating the single vCPU. The `LOGICAL_ROW_CAP = 1_500_000` guard added in response has been **DELETED** - and note the scar it left: that cap had been **silently SKIPPING the moat entirely on every run** while the job still reported success-with-a-flag, i.e. the most important table in the system was unbacked-up behind a green status. Dump now flows server-side to /data then over `flyctl ssh sftp get` with checksum verify. **Two residuals for the owner:** (a) `.sftp.err` records `Error: accepts at most 2 arg(s), received 3` from a `flyctl ssh sftp` invocation even though the artifact landed at correct size and magic - worth a look; (b) this sentinel could verify header magic + size only, **not** the full TOC, because `pg_restore` is not on the tower PATH - the script's own restore-verify (structural `pg_restore --list` + row-count assert into a scratch DB on the Fly machine) is the authority and its result should be recorded here before closing. Consequence for the deploy lane: the stale-backup STAGE-BLOCK condition is **cleared**; FU-102 is now the sole remaining item.
- candidate fixes (need review; several touch HARD GUARDRAILS):
  - (a) Run the dump SERVER-SIDE on the mcplookup-db machine (pg_dump -Fc of just the moat to the /data volume, then scp off) — avoids the tunnel entirely; validates whether the bottleneck is tunnel-per-row latency vs server detoast. LOWEST-RISK first probe. NOTE tonight's ~68 rows/s could be tunnel-round-trip-bound (psycopg yielded ~1 row/chunk), so server-side may be dramatically faster.
  - (b) Stream COPY directly to a gzip file on disk (constant memory) + set a server statement_timeout + fail-loud if throughput < floor — fixes the silent-hang and OOM-risk regardless of speed. Reversible tooling change (db_backups is tower-local; route via PR if it lives in repo).
  - (c) Column-prune the moat COPY (skip heavy TOASTed explanation/signal JSON, keep server_id/axis/score/version) — fast, but a partial backup; changes restore-verify semantics.
  - (d) HARD GUARDRAIL / chairman: bump the Fly PG machine memory + work_mem, or adopt Fly Managed Postgres native volume snapshots for DR and demote this drill to a lightweight integrity check. Touches cost + deploy-config.
- log-note: off-site copy is STILL manual for the backups that do exist — push to object storage for full DR.

I warmed the cache and re-ran `--phase collect-all`, which resumed straight to postcheck and closed the run in <1s. **Two committable fixes, both narrow:** (a) raise the `freshness()` timeout well past the observed cold path and give it a retry, and (b) make `ph_postcheck` non-fatal -- a read-only observability call must never fail a run that has already written. **And the bigger implication:** this is the most likely cause of the 2026-07-19 run dying mid-import (same script, same unguarded 30s read, same endpoint), which is the defect FU-056 records. So the shape is confirmed and now quantified: with a 600s TTL and a cold recompute in the 20–300s band, roughly one request every ten minutes eats the full recompute and **any client with a sub-30s timeout gets nothing at all**. That band straddles the default timeout of most HTTP libraries, which is why this keeps presenting as three different bugs (slow / hung / crashed a run) depending on who called it. Nothing here changes the diagnosis in the 7/20 and 7/21 logs above — same per-machine cache, same unguarded reader — but it retires the "latency annoyance" reading for good: on the public surface this is intermittent unavailability of the moat's staleness oracle. Watch-side note for whoever fixes it: CHECK B in this task now needs a >60s client to read its own primary signal, so the watcher is one timeout tweak away from being blind to freshness for the same reason `ph_postcheck` was.
  - 2026-07-21 cadence-jobs-daily-trigger: **fourth independent measurement today, and it lands squarely in the band the entry above predicted.** From the tower immediately after the cadence jobs closed: a GET of `https://mcprisky.io/freshness` with a 20s client timeout **failed** (`HttpClient.Timeout of 20 seconds elapsing`), and an immediate retry with a 60s client returned **200 in 31.4s**, payload `computed_at 2026-07-21T06:41:47-04:00, cache_age_seconds 30.0, cache_ttl_seconds 600` — i.e. the cache had just been recomputed by my own first (abandoned) request, so the 31.4s reading IS the cold path and the 20s failure was the same recompute seen from the losing side. Practical note for anyone scripting against this surface: a 20s timeout is not merely tight, it is **below the observed cold path**, so any client at or under 20s is guaranteed to fail on a cache miss rather than merely occasionally — the fixes already listed as (a) and (b) remain the cheap ones and remain unshipped.
  - 2026-07-22 chairman (attended): **the DB resize fixed the LATENCY half by ~10x but not the whole entry; priority STAYS P1, and the reason it stays is now the correctness bug, not the speed.** Re-measured `/freshness` from the tower immediately after the resize (20GB / 2GB RAM / work_mem 48MB): **35.9s, down from the >300s / 20–300s band this log documents.** So the acute "staleness oracle is unreadable" driver of the 7/20 P2→P1 escalation is largely gone — a patient (>40s) client now gets an answer. **But two things the resize does NOT touch keep this at P1:** (1) 35.9s is still *above* the 30s default client timeout, so the sub-30s-client failure mode (the HTTP 000 / TimeoutError readings above) persists for ordinary callers — this is a caching-topology bug (per-machine cache + 600s TTL + slow cold recompute), not a capacity bug; (2) the correctness defect is entirely independent of box size — the cold machine still serves a stale in-process snapshot while asserting `cache_age_seconds` inside TTL (THE LINE violated on the endpoint that implements it). ### FU-124 | The app is invisible to crawlers — an indexability gap FU-075..079 never captured, and the one axis where conduid is unambiguously ahead
- date: 2026-07-27 · source: chairman (attended) — "found conduid by googling for a Qualys MCP" · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: **This is how the chairman found the competitor in the first place**, and it is the gap the 07-22 sweep missed. [[FU-077]] logged their landing page as better-converting; nobody logged that we are *not in the index at all*. mcplookup/mcprisky is authenticated-only, so Google and AI crawlers see nothing — **no `robots.txt`, no `sitemap.xml`, no `llms.txt` exist anywhere in the repo** (verified 07-27). conduid is `Allow: /` with `Disallow:` only on `/api/`, `/dashboard/`, `/admin/`, `/auth/`, `/login`, a 39,966-URL sitemap, per-server SSR pages, and templated titles carrying the borrowed entity (`Contextplus — Developer Tools MCP Server | Trust: 92/100 | Conduid`). They rank on *other people's* server names; we rank on nothing. **Clerk is NOT the blocker and this is not an auth change** — our auth is per-router JWT verification (`org_risk_summary_api.py`, `risk_tier_summary_dashboard.py`, `verdict_breakdown_api.get_principal`), not global middleware, so public routes are purely additive and `/dashboard`, admin and the API stay exactly as they are. **Sequencing is the whole decision, and it is NOT "ship report cards now."** Split in two: **(step 1, do now, $0, zero content risk)** `robots.txt` + a `sitemap.xml` INDEX (never a flat file — see [[FU-079]] on their 50K ceiling) + `llms.txt`; include `Disallow: /*?q=` so we never accrue their liability — their `/marketplace?q=<anything>` server-renders a unique `<title>` over an UNFILTERED result list, which is a doorway/internal-search-results pattern named in Google's spam policies and is what a core update prunes. **(step 2, BLOCKED on [[FU-076]] exposing legible axes)** SSR `/server/{slug}` off `base.html` — NOT through the SPA (that is the [[FU-1481-class]] UI-orphan failure where the SPA never called `/vulns`). Publishing report cards today means 40K pages that all say CRITICAL ([[FU-058]]: 99.5% HIGH/CRITICAL) — thin, non-differentiating, and a garbage score published at scale is worse than no score. Tier the index to slugs with >=1 differentiated signal (CVE link, dispute, official status, freshness event) per MARKETING_SEO_WAITLIST_STRATEGY_2026_07_10.md §1. **Never branch rendering on User-Agent** — that is cloaking and a manual-action risk; gate on auth state with signed-out as the default rendered state, and mark gated regions with schema.org `isAccessibleForFree:false` + `hasPart`/`cssSelector`, which is Google's sanctioned mechanism for exactly this. Related: [[FU-075]], [[FU-076]], [[FU-077]], [[FU-058]], [[FU-079]], [[FU-054]].
- verify: `python -c "import urllib.request,sys;ok=all(urllib.request.urlopen('https://mcplookup.app/'+p,timeout=20).status==200 for p in ['robots.txt','sitemap.xml']);sys.exit(0 if ok else 1)"`  # robots.txt and sitemap.xml are served -- the crawler gap is closed

<!-- FU-115 status=open priority=P1 filed=2026-07-27 last_touch=2026-07-27 stale_days=37 -->
### FU-115 | Daemon liveness read from log files is UNRELIABLE — healthy daemons appear dead for hours
- date: 2026-07-27 · source: deploy-runtime-from-main · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: **This run came within one command of restarting a perfectly healthy builder.** On the runtime, `/proc/6797/fd/1 -> /home/workspace/logs/goose_runner.log (deleted)`, and the last write to that inode was `06:51:23Z` — 141 minutes earlier, on a 60-second cycle loop, with the process alive (`S`, 2 threads, 3s CPU, no children, no network/DB fds). Every sibling daemon had written within 9 minutes. That is a textbook alive-but-wedged signature and matches the [[builder_dead_61h_undetected]] scar exactly. **It was a FALSE POSITIVE.** Reading `goose_runner.py` before acting showed `log()` does `open(LOG_FILE, "a")` **per line** rather than writing to stdout, so when something deleted `/home/workspace/logs/*.log` at ~06:51 the daemon transparently recreated the path and kept going — the real file was at `Cycle 186, 09:13:41Z`, ten seconds stale. The stale `fd/1` was an orphaned stdout handle from before the deletion. **Both obvious probes are broken, in opposite directions:** daemons using `logging.FileHandler` (sentinel_directive_generator_goose, ladder_shim, proposed_to_pending_promoter — all show `(deleted)`) hold the fd open and keep writing into the ORPHANED inode, so the on-disk path goes stale and *reading the log file* under-reports them (generator's path-file froze at `06:48:56Z` while the process logged at `09:08:07Z`); daemons that reopen per line write to the NEW inode, so *reading `/proc/pid/fd/1`* under-reports them. Either probe will report a live daemon dead, and on process exit the orphaned-inode output is LOST outright. Note the asymmetry with the [[phase5_anchor_refill_shipped]] lesson, which taught the `/proc/<pid>/fd/1` trick for deleted logs — that trick is correct for reading a deleted log but is a **false-death generator** when used as a liveness probe.
- the reliable probe (verified this run): the `service_health` table via the write-service bus — `curl -s -X POST http://127.0.0.1:8772/query -d '{"sql":"SELECT service, last_heartbeat FROM service_health ORDER BY last_heartbeat DESC"}'` returned `goose_runner 09:16:54Z` at `09:17:02Z`, i.e. 8 seconds stale. `goose_runner.py` runs `heartbeat_loop()` on a 30s `HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL` in a background thread, independent of any log file. Any check that concludes "daemon X is dead/wedged" MUST confirm against `service_health` before restarting anything; a log-silence read alone is not evidence.
- not yet diagnosed: **what deleted `/home/workspace/logs/*.log` at ~06:51Z.** It is NOT `tools/sentinel_janitor.sh` (its `.sentinel_janitor_sweep_stamp` is 09:01 but it only runs `sweep_ghost_done.py`; it never touches `.log`), and a repo-wide grep found no log-rotation or unlink of `logs/*.log` in any `.py`/`.sh`. Left open rather than guessed at — filed here because the deleter is the root cause and it will orphan these fds again on its next pass. Forensics preserved at `/home/workspace/zo_sentinel_state/forensics/20260727_goose_runner_wedge/` (log tail at the apparent wedge, ps snapshot, proc status) with a README marking it a FALSE POSITIVE so a future run does not mistake it for a real wedge.
- verify: `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\zo_probe.py" --sql "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM service_health WHERE last_heartbeat < now() - interval '15 minutes'" --assert "v is not None and int(v)==0"`  # no service_health row is stale; the log-file probe is not consulted at all
- verify_seen_red: 2026-07-29T13:39:15Z

<!-- FU-117 status=open priority=P1 filed=2026-07-27 last_touch=2026-07-27 stale_days=37 -->
### FU-117 | goose does NOT fail when a stdio extension fails to start — a dead bridge yields a live-looking architect at +0
- date: 2026-07-27 · source: goose-shadow-research · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: Observed on the wire, twice, in goose-canary runs 30262516540 and 30262672181. When the `zo_directive_bridge` stdio extension dies before initialisation, goose emits **`Warning: Failed to start extension 'zo_directive_bridge' (...), continuing without it`** and then **starts the session anyway** with only the builtin tools (`bash`, `python`, `search`). The agent duly emitted a correctly-named, correctly-shaped tool call — `{"name": "zo_directive_bridge__read_protected_files", "arguments": {}}` — against a tool that was not registered, got nothing, and finished the turn. **This is the 1.38 starvation shape (scar #454) reproduced from a completely different cause.** The mesh's model of that scar has always been "a future goose version might RENAME our tools"; the real exposure is broader and more likely: *anything* that stops `directive_mcp.py` importing — a bad dep, an unwritable path, a stale `argparse` backport (all three already recorded in that file's own header comments) — takes the bridge down, and goose's loudest signal is a **warning on stderr**. The architect process stays up, the daemon heartbeat keeps writing to `service_health`, `goose_runner` keeps cycling, and the directive queue quietly goes to +0. Note the interaction with [[FU-115]]: the reliable liveness probe we just established (`service_health` heartbeats) would report this architect **healthy**, because the process genuinely is alive — heartbeat liveness and *functional* liveness are different assertions, and we currently only measure the first. Also the same silent-green family as [[FU-114]] (4 active services declare no router and are "skipped", not failed).
- what is now covered vs what is NOT: **covered in CI** — the canary's namespacing step (PR #2058) greps for `Failed to start extension 'zo_directive_bridge'` and fails the build with `CANARY FAIL (a): EXTENSION_START_FAILURE`, held explicitly distinct from `CANARY FAIL (b): genuine rename`, per the [[score_import_shepherd]] rule that EXTRACTION_FAILURE must never be absorbed into the same bucket as DEGENERATE. **NOT covered on the tower** — nothing in the live mesh asserts that the architect's goose session actually has the bridge tools registered. That is the open half and the reason this entry stays P1.
- proposed fix (filed, not fixed — it changes architect runtime behaviour and wants a directive + PR rather than a hand-edit): a startup assertion in `sentinel_directive_generator_goose.py` that scans the goose subprocess output for `Failed to start extension` and treats it as a **hard cycle abort with an alarm**, not a warning; plus a `bridge_tool_registration_probe` that runs the one-call namespacing recipe against the LIVE tower config on a schedule, so a bridge that breaks at 03:00 is caught before a day of +0. A directive spec for the reporter half is queued in `GOOSE_WATCH.md`.

### FU-190 | 18,560 log lines said "Recorded OSV vuln"; the table holds ZERO of them, because ws_write fails by RETURNING None
- date: 2026-07-30 - source: deploy-runtime-from-main (09:10Z slot) - status: open - priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: With `[[FU-187]]` and `[[FU-189]]` fixed, the daemon finally reached its WRITE stage -- and announced a success the database denies. Runtime measurement: **18,560 log lines claiming `Recorded OSV vuln`, 55,690 HTTP 500 `Binder Error: There are no UNIQUE/PRIMARY KEY constraints that refer to this table` from write_service, and 0 rows in `mcp_threat_associations` for the vuln logged as Recorded.** The specific check: the log says `Recorded OSV vuln GHSA-4h5r-5jm8-jxjm for server 62d0e3ef... (severity: high)`; `SELECT count(*) ... WHERE threat_type='osv:GHSA-4h5r-5jm8-jxjm'` returns **0**, and the three 500s immediately preceding that line are its own retries. **Mechanism: `ws_write` signals TOTAL failure by RETURNING None, not by raising**, so the caller's `try/except` saw no exception, incremented `vuln_count` and announced success -- a bare try/except cannot see a sentinel return. **Root cause of the write rejection (the open half): `mcp_threat_associations` has NO UNIQUE/PRIMARY KEY constraint at all -- `duckdb_constraints()` returns only `NOT NULL` -- so write_service's `ON CONFLICT` upsert can never bind and EVERY write to that table fails.** The table also has no `source` column though the payload sends one (`Referenced column "source" not found`), so the write shape does not match the schema either.
- fix: code half **DONE, PR #2407** -- `ws_write` logs a loud terminal failure naming the table and documents that None means nothing persisted; the caller checks the return value, counts `write_failures`, logs `NOT RECORDED:` instead of `Recorded`, and the scan summary reports `N REJECTED by write_service (NOT persisted)` rather than presenting the run as clean. **Schema half NOT TAKEN and escalated:** adding a UNIQUE constraint (and the missing `source` column) to a live moat table is a migration, not a $0 reversible code change, and `[[FU-053]]`-era experience plus the standing note that *DDL via /query fails silently* make a unilateral deploy-lane migration the wrong instrument. Proposed: a numbered migration adding `UNIQUE(server_id, threat_type)` -- which is exactly the conflict target the caller's upsert semantics assume -- plus `source TEXT`. Needs a decision on whether existing duplicate rows must be collapsed first.
- verify: `curl -s -X POST http://127.0.0.1:8772/query -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"sql":"SELECT constraint_type FROM duckdb_constraints() WHERE table_name = '"'"'mcp_threat_associations'"'"'"}'`  # must list a UNIQUE/PRIMARY KEY, not only NOT NULL
- verify_seen_red: YES, 2026-07-30 -- it currently reads only `NOT NULL`, which is what opened this entry, and the paired row-count check reads 0 against a log line asserting 1. The CODE half's control reconstructs the pre-fix caller and asserts it BOTH returns 1 AND logs `Recorded OSV vuln` on a None write, pinning the false count and the false message separately.
- log:
  - 2026-07-30T09:5xZ deploy-runtime-from-main: **I published the wrong claim first, and the correction is the finding.** Mid-run I reported "the lane is recording vulnerabilities again -- first data since 2026-06-06" on the strength of the `Recorded OSV vuln` log line. Checking `mcp_threat_associations` returned 0. **The artifact's own success message convinced the agent auditing it, in the same run in which that agent was writing about not trusting success messages.** R1 exists for this: resolve the outcome from the RUNTIME (the table), never from the actor's report of itself.
  - 2026-07-30T09:5xZ deploy-runtime-from-main: **adding the honesty check FAILED two `[[FU-189]]` tests**, because their stub was `lambda table, rows: mod.written.append(rows)` and `list.append` returns None -- the double was accidentally mimicking a rejected write, so those tests had been asserting on a fabricated success exactly as production was. Corrected to return a truthy response.
- lesson: **A FAILURE SIGNALLED BY A RETURN VALUE IS INVISIBLE TO A try/except, AND THE SUCCESS MESSAGE THAT FOLLOWS IT IS THE MOST EXPENSIVE LINE IN THE FILE.** This is `[[FU-164]]`/`[[FU-167]]` at the data layer: not a gate that passed while printing its own failure, but a WRITER that reported an outcome it never confirmed, 18,560 times, against a table it had never once populated. The 54-day outage of `[[FU-187]]` was *discoverable at any moment* by one `SELECT count(*)` -- what prevented that was that everything reading the daemon read its log. **Never let a writer log the past tense on the strength of not having raised.** And the harness rule that follows: **a test double that returns the failure sentinel reproduces the bug it is meant to exclude** -- `list.append` returning None is a trap sitting in every `lambda: xs.append(...)` stub in the repo.
- resolution:
