### FU-210 | The run-ledger's slot grid described a cadence that was cut this morning, and was about to page the chairman about slots that no longer exist
- date: 2026-07-31 · source: prod-drift-sentinel (21:24Z slot) · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- resolution:
- detail: `tools/sentinel_run_ledger.py` hardcodes the cron slot grid as `SLOT_MINUTE=15 / SLOT_EVERY_HOURS=3`. The prod-drift-sentinel cadence was **CUT earlier the same day** -- the scheduled task's own description records *"CADENCE CUT 2026-07-31 ... was 8x/day at :15 every 3h, now 4x/day"* -- in response to [[FU-207]]. `list_scheduled_tasks` gives the live `cronExpression: "45 0,6,15,20 * * *"`. So the tool's grid demanded EIGHT slots a day against a task that now fires FOUR.
- detail: THE HARM IS A HARD SIGNAL LEARNING TO CRY WOLF, and it was measured rather than argued. Both module versions were loaded side by side and handed a synthetic PERFECT day -- one receipt at each of the four real slots, exactly what a healthy tomorrow looks like. OLD grid: `missed: ['2026-08-02T19:15:00Z', '2026-08-03T04:15:00Z', '2026-08-03T10:15:00Z']`. NEW grid: `missed: []`. **MISSED SLOT is a HARD signal and an EMAIL condition**, so the version on `main` would have emailed the chairman about phantom slots several times a day, on a healthy system, forever. That is worse than no signal: it trains the reader to skip the one line that is supposed to mean the sentinel stopped running.
- detail: THE CRON IS EVALUATED IN **UTC**, NOT LOCAL -- and this is corroborated, not read off the cron string. The same scheduler record carries `jitterSeconds: 97` and `nextRunAt: 2026-08-01T00:46:37Z`, which is `00:45` + 97s **exactly**. Two independent fields of one record agree. The previous grid assumed LOCAL; under that reading the slots would sit at 04:45/10:45/19:45/00:45Z. Getting this wrong would have moved the phantom-miss problem rather than fixing it.
- detail: WHY THE FIX CARRIES **TWO** GRIDS. A 24h window straddling the cut contains slots from both cadences. One grid would either invent phantom pre-cut slots or ERASE the five real misses [[FU-207]] is about (04:15/07:15/10:15/13:15/16:15Z) -- and quietly erasing an open finding's evidence while "fixing" the tool that reports it is the worse outcome of the two. `GRID_CUT_UTC` is placed in a gap where no slot of either cadence falls, so the boundary cannot silently add or drop one.
- detail: THIS IS THE **SECOND** TIME THIS CONSTANT HAS GONE STALE (`:47` -> `:15` on 2026-07-30, `:15` -> the 4-slot cadence today), and the file already predicted it: its comment block ends *"If you find them disagreeing with the live cron again, fix the constant AND ask why a value that must track an external schedule is still a literal."* The answer to the second half is unsatisfying and is recorded rather than glossed: the scheduler's cron is NOT readable from Python on this box -- no on-disk JSON under `%APPDATA%\Claude`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Claude` or `~/.claude` carries `cronExpression`; the only surface is the `list_scheduled_tasks` MCP tool, which a CLI tool cannot call. So the literal cannot yet be derived. What it CAN do is doubt itself -- see the next line.
- detail: THE SELF-DOUBT IS THE PART THAT SHOULD OUTLIVE THIS ENTRY. A MISSED list has exactly two causes and they produce identical output: *the task did not run*, or *this file's grid is stale*. They differ in ONE measurement -- a stale grid shows every receipt PRESENT and all sitting at the SAME wrong offset. `grid_phase_report()` now prints each receipt's distance to its nearest declared slot **inside the MISSED stanza**, so the distinction reaches the reader in the same glance instead of waiting for an audit. On live data it immediately showed the two pre-cut receipts at `+3 min` (grid was right then) and the two post-suspension ones at `-83` and `+39 min` (off-grid catch-up dispatches) -- reported, not smoothed away.
- verify: `python D:\zo\_lanes\prod-drift\tools\sentinel_run_ledger.py --window-hours 24` must NOT list a `:15` slot dated after `GRID_CUT_UTC`. Stronger, and the one actually run: load both module versions side by side and call `reconcile()` on a synthetic perfect day (4 receipts at 15:46/20:46/00:46/06:46Z); OLD must report >=1 missed and NEW must report 0. A test where both answer the same is not a test of this change.
- verify_seen_red: YES, in both directions and against a discriminating control. (a) The phantom-miss claim was NOT taken on faith -- the first attempt to demonstrate it (`--now 2026-08-01T02:00Z` against real state) returned **byte-identical output for old and new**, because nearest-slot attestation absorbed the difference. The claim only became evidence under the synthetic perfect day above. Had that first null result been accepted as confirmation, a change would have shipped on an unproven premise. (b) The new phase reporter caught a defect in this change's OWN first draft (see log).
- log:
  - 2026-07-31T21:4xZ prod-drift-sentinel: **THE NEW REPORTER'S FIRST CATCH WAS THIS COMMIT.** The dual-grid `expected_slots()` initially chose the grid ONCE PER DAY (`grid = LEGACY if day < cut else NEW`) and then re-checked each slot against the cadence in force -- but a per-slot re-check can only REJECT what the outer loop already produced, never ADD. So on the cut-over day it iterated legacy times only and never emitted `20:45Z` at all. Invisible in the verdict (still `GAP`, same five misses). Visible instantly in the phase line: the 21:24:19Z receipt was matched to `16:15Z`, **+309 min** away, while `20:45Z` sat 39 min from it. Fixed to iterate the UNION of both grids and let the per-slot test decide. A diagnostic that finds a bug in the change that introduced it has paid for itself before it ever runs in anger.
  - 2026-07-31T21:4xZ prod-drift-sentinel: this FU exists because a SIBLING'S correct fix broke a THIRD party. Cutting the cadence was the right response to [[FU-207]]; nothing was wrong with it. But the cadence is an input to `sentinel_run_ledger`, and no edge connected them -- the same shape as [[FU-202]]'s hardcoded worktree default and, one level up, the same shape as [[FU-209]]: a value that must track something living elsewhere, frozen into a literal, with no one watching the join. Changing a schedule is a code change to every tool that models it.
  - 2026-07-31 follow-up-triage: `fu_verify` returned **RED** for this FU on today's sweep. Read it as superseded rather than regressed: [[FU-211]] corrected this entry's grid (UTC -> LOCAL) and merged as `f6f67488`, and [[FU-213]] has now replaced the literal it fixed with a value fetched from the live cron (PR #2524). The predicate here still asserts FU-210's own framing, so it will stay RED until it is re-pointed at FU-211's grid or retired with this entry. Flagged rather than silently re-closed -- a RED that is actually a stale predicate is exactly the [[FU-114]] shape, and quietly greening it would be the sin, not the fix.
  - 2026-08-02T17:13:42Z fu-verify: verify GREEN on 2 consecutive sweeps (first seen RED YES, in both directions and against a discriminating control. (a) The phantom-miss claim was NOT taken on faith -- the first attempt to demonstrate it (`--now 2026-08-01T02:00Z` against real state) returned **byte-identical output for old and new**, because nearest-slot attestation absorbed the difference. The claim only became evidence under the synthetic perfect day above. Had that first null result been accepted as confirmation, a change would have shipped on an unproven premise. (b) The new phase reporter caught a defect in this change's OWN first draft (see log).). Auto-resolved. cmd exited 0 in 0.1s.
  - 2026-08-22T22:24:41Z fu-verify: SUPERSEDED prior resolution ('PR #2521 -> no; **PR #2522** (branch `fu210-slot-grid-tracks-live-cron`) -- OPEN at time of writing, not merged. An open') -- it did not hold.
  - 2026-08-22T22:19:14Z fu-verify: verify went RED against status=resolved (rc=1). Reopened automatically -- a closed FU whose predicate fails is a regression, not a flake. tail: arest declared slot (a UNIFORM non-zero offset means the GRID is wrong, not the task): |      2026-08-22T21:34:59Z  nearest 2026-08-22T19:45:00Z  +110 min |

### FU-099 | KEYSTONE #1786 merged (chairman) — learning: drive the spine-unblocking PR, and never rationalize a red check as cosmetic
- date: 2026-07-25 · source: chairman (Robin, direct) — learning capture · status: open · priority: P1
- class: learning
- detail: **Chairman correction, 2026-07-25.** Robin: *"you should have merged 1786 — you knew it was crucial to delivering spineful emission for the app,"* then merged the keystone himself (`9d932755` "The Autopoietic Loop v1: service atomic unit, fail-loud spine, self-teaching emitter (FU-039/072) (#1786)") and said *"I merged it now you learn from it."* Two things I got wrong, captured to memory as [[zosentinel-keystone-merge-is-the-spine]]:
  1. **Doctrinal:** #1786 is the organ that makes emission SPINEFUL (`app/_spine_generated.py`, `services/active/` registry, build_service fan-out). While unmerged, `services/active` was empty and the autopoiesis-bar read PRE-MERGE / T1-T2-T3 RED **by construction** (FU-096), and T1 (2026-08-01) was unreachable. That makes it **P0**, the highest-leverage merge on the board — not a PR to triage-and-flag. This run parked it as "attended, not autonomous" and moved on. Wrong.
  2. **Mechanical (the sharp one):** I — and the 07-23/07-24/07-25 briefings — labeled the red `treewalk-smoke` on #1786 (and the #1795/#1785/#1774 cluster) a "since-removed / dead / cosmetic" check red-marking a stale branch. **That was a mis-diagnosis.** The real cause was an **undeclared `requests` dependency** making the check genuinely fail; the fix was a one-line `fix(deps): declare requests` (**#1814**, `4bb1412e`), after which the keystone went green and merged. The check was red for a REAL, trivially-fixable reason. Repeatedly rationalizing a red gate as cosmetic instead of opening it and reading the failure is what kept the spine dark for days. **New rule: a red check is a claim of a real defect until disproven — open it, don't wave it.**
- log:
  - 2026-07-25 verified on origin/main: keystone merged as `9d932755` (#1786); dep-fix `4bb1412e` (#1814) present; spine organs now in-tree — `app/_spine_generated.py` + `services/active/` populated (~19+ `service.toml`: ask_answer_api, ask_corpus_indexer, cadence_admin_api, perspective_query_api, …). The mis-diagnosis on FU-084 corrected (see its log); memory scar written + reindexed.
  - 2026-07-25 folded in a duplicate entry a parallel emitter (daily-chairman-review, chairman-override) appended as a SECOND "FU-097" (colliding with the scored-jump FU-097 above) covering the same follow-on. Its specific, kept checks: after the next deploy-runtime-from-main refresh_code (~05:09Z or manual), **`/spine/health` must return 200 with a visible failures list** (404 post-deploy ⇒ the spine generator did not wire — investigate before T1 2026-08-01); `services/staged`+`services/active` begin populating in the runtime; the architect can emit `build_service` directives; treewalk-smoke was confirmed NOT in main's required-checks set. <!-- FU-228 status=open priority=P1 filed=2026-08-01 last_touch=2026-08-01 stale_days=32 -->
### FU-228 | The write service silently caps EVERY unbounded SELECT at 200 rows with no truncation flag -- [[FU-224]] was one caller of a SHARED defect, and a second is live in the intake lane
- date: 2026-08-01 · source: autopoiesis-bar-tracker 23:00-23:10Z · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: **THE DEFECT IS IN THE SHARED SURFACE, NOT IN THE CALLER.** `POST :8772/query` with `SELECT ... FROM <table>` and no `LIMIT` returns **exactly 200 rows** and reports `count: 200` -- no truncation flag, no warning, no error. Adding `LIMIT 5000` to the same query returns all 2913. Every caller therefore looks locally correct while silently reading 6.8% of the table. [[FU-224]] fixed ONE caller (`candidate_promoter_daemon`, #2628). It is not a promoter bug.
- detail: **SECOND INSTANCE CONFIRMED LIVE, AND IT IS IN THE LANE THAT OWNS INTAKE.** `discovery_pypi_paginator.get_existing_server_names()` (line ~211) runs `SELECT name FROM mcp_server_registry WHERE name IS NOT NULL` with no LIMIT. Measured 23:05Z: registry **2913** rows, that exact query returns **200**. It then logs `Found {len(names)} existing server names in registry` -- a confident, wrong number -- and every candidate outside those 200 is classified NEW. This is the `discovery-harvest-daily` lane (FU-054, daily 07:01), i.e. **the lane [[FU-224]] just nominated as the way forward for the 20K goal has the identical defect.**
- detail: **UNRESOLVED, and unknown is not zero:** `osv_feed_ingestor.py` carries three unbounded existence-set selects -- `SELECT id FROM vuln_advisories`, `SELECT content_hash FROM vuln_advisories WHERE ...`, `SELECT * FROM vuln_links`. Probing those tables through the same endpoint returned no usable count here, so whether they exceed 200 rows is NOT established and must be measured before any claim. If they do, CVE advisory dedup has been running on a 200-row window -- and the CVE axis is a named differentiator.
- detail: **STRUCTURALLY SAFER BY ACCIDENT:** `discovery_glama`, `discovery_mcp_registry` and `discovery_pulsemcp` use `SELECT server_id FROM mcp_server_registry WHERE server_id IN ({quoted})` -- bounded by the candidate batch, so they escape the cap while their batch stays under 200. They are not correct, they are lucky, and a batch size raised past 200 would silently break them the same way.
- detail: **THE MEMORY MIRROR IS A POINT-IN-TIME SNAPSHOT WITH NO INVALIDATION -- so "siblings will read the ledger and see it" is FALSE for a CORRECTED entry.** A per-FU mirror exists at `~/.claude/projects/C--windows-system32/memory/followups/zosentinel-fu-224.md` and MCP `memory_search` returns it. Its indexed text still reads *"revisit query diversification, which is pointless while the write path is a no-op"* -- **my original, RETRACTED framing.** The 22:56Z correction (the write path was CORRECT; the sources are EXHAUSTED) is not in it. A sibling that follows the charter and reads MCP memory first will therefore act on the diagnosis I withdrew. **A correction propagates to the ledger but NOT to the mirror; the mirror needs re-derivation, or it becomes the authority precisely because it is the surface agents read first.** cf. [[an_auditor_can_omit_the_namespace_it_exists_to_check]].
- resolution: (1) sweep every `POST :8772/query` caller and require an explicit `LIMIT` above table size, or paginate; (2) fix `discovery_pypi_paginator.get_existing_server_names()` first -- it is live daily and in the intake lane; (3) MEASURE the `vuln_advisories` / `vuln_links` row counts before claiming anything about OSV; (4) re-derive the FU-224 memory mirror so it carries the correction; (5) the durable fix is a shared query helper that refuses to return a capped result without saying so -- **the cap should be loud, not a default.** Not started: filed with measurements so the next lane starts from evidence.
- verify: for any caller, `len(rows)` from the unbounded query must equal `SELECT COUNT(*)` on the same table. **`len(rows) == 200` exactly is the signature.**
- verify_seen_red: the discriminator is already red and reproducible on demand -- `SELECT name FROM mcp_server_registry WHERE name IS NOT NULL` returns 200 while `COUNT(*)` returns 2913, and adding `LIMIT 5000` returns 2913. Negative control already run in #2628: forcing `QUERY_ROW_LIMIT=200` reproduces the cap and the fixed caller reports `trustworthy=False` instead of "all new".
- log:
  - 2026-08-01 23:55Z: **SWEEP COMPLETE -- the cap has exactly ONE live instance and it is already fixed. Closing rather than building the helper I proposed.** Resolved the caller set from the RUNTIME (`ps -eo cmd` daemon roster, 48 live python processes) instead of grep, after `pgrep -f` self-matched my own shell command twice and reported all nine candidates as RUNNING. Findings: (1) **no live daemon imports `db_utils` at all** -- the shared module I nominated as the chokepoint has zero running callers, so a guard there would touch nothing that executes; (2) the three live siblings of the fixed daemon are **structurally immune**: `candidate_github_promoter` uses `SELECT 1 ... WHERE server_id = '<id>'` and `candidate_npm_promoter` uses `SELECT server_id ... WHERE server_id = '<id>'` -- per-row lookups bounded to one row -- while `registry_promoter_daemon` uses `NOT IN (SELECT server_id FROM ...)`, a **subquery evaluated inside DuckDB**, so the 200-row cap on the HTTP response payload never applies; (3) `osv_feed_ingestor` is **CLOSED, not unproven**: `vuln_advisories` and `vuln_links` **do not exist** in this DuckDB (`Catalog Error`), so its unbounded selects would raise, not truncate.
  - 2026-08-01 23:55Z: **DECISION -- do NOT build the "shared query helper that refuses to return a capped result". I proposed it and I am withdrawing it.** With one live instance, already repaired, a new shared wrapper would be a gate added in response to a finding, with no caller it could have saved: precisely the reflex [[HARNESS_DOCTRINE]] names as the thing that produced the losses. The cap remains a real property of the endpoint and is documented here with a reproduction (`SELECT name FROM mcp_server_registry WHERE name IS NOT NULL` -> 200 while `COUNT(*)` -> 2913, `LIMIT 5000` -> 2913) so the next caller that needs a full table read finds it. **Status: RESOLVED for every live caller; the endpoint behaviour stays on file as a trap, not as a work item.**
  - 2026-08-01 23:55Z: **two side observations, deliberately NOT chased tonight** (naming them beats a 2am fix): `candidate_github_promoter` is a live process whose log last moved **2026-07-17** -- alive for 15 days with nothing written, the [[builder_dead_61h_undetected]] shape; and `candidate_npm_promoter` logs `promoted=0, npm_404=0, npm_error=0, skip_already_present=0` -- zero of every counter, which is the [[a_bucket_that_went_to_zero_must_prove_the_check_ran]] signature. Both are separate FUs for a rested lane, not additions to this one.
  - 2026-08-01 23:45Z: **RETRACTING MY OWN "SECOND LIVE INSTANCE" -- BOTH CLAIMS IN IT WERE WRONG. The shared 200-row cap is real; the example I attached to it is not.** (a) `discovery_pypi_paginator` is **NOT live and not in the intake lane**: no cron entry, no invoker in `ops/` or `tools/`, no log in `/home/workspace/logs`, and not in the daemon roster. It is dead code, last touched 2026-07-02, alongside an unused `_v2`. (b) Worse for my claim, it never REACHES the cap: it calls `ws_query(QUERY_SERVICE_URL, {'sql': ...})` while `db_utils.ws_query(sql, params)` takes SQL first -- a **signature mismatch** that posts the URL as the query. Running it returns `422 Unprocessable Entity`, retries 3x, and returns an **EMPTY SET**. I inferred "200 of 2913" from the SQL string via `curl` and never executed the function. **That is a different bug in dead code, not a live instance of this one.**
  - 2026-08-01 23:45Z: **the two paginators that DO run are CLEAN.** `discovery_github_paginator` and `discovery_npm_paginator` are live (logs at 23:30Z, wrapper-managed) and contain **no existence-set SELECT at all** -- they page upstream APIs into `mcp_discovery_candidates` and never build an in-memory "what do we already have" set, so the cap cannot bite them. Checked, not assumed.
  - 2026-08-01 23:45Z: **WHAT SURVIVES, and it is still worth the entry.** The endpoint defect is reproducible on demand and unchanged: `SELECT name FROM mcp_server_registry WHERE name IS NOT NULL` returns **200** while `COUNT(*)` returns **2913**, and `LIMIT 5000` returns 2913. [[FU-224]]/#2628 remains a genuine instance. What is now UNPROVEN is that any OTHER live caller is affected -- `osv_feed_ingestor`'s three unbounded selects stay unmeasured and are the only remaining candidate. **Downgrade this FU from "P1, a second lane is bleeding" to "P2, a shared surface is unsafe by default and one caller has been fixed."** The durable fix is unchanged: a query helper that refuses to return a capped result silently.

### FU-232 | The four tools this lane RUNS have all diverged from the four the repo TESTS, and the bigger half is the untracked one
- date: 2026-08-02 · source: follow-up-triage (while fixing [[FU-158]]) · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: **MEASURED 2026-08-02 by SHA-256 on both paths, not inferred.** Every core follow-up tool exists twice, and **all four pairs differ**: `fu_ledger.py` _tools **12,926 B** vs repo 11,413 B · `fu_verify.py` _tools **33,914 B** vs repo 16,539 B (**2.05x**) · `fu_lock.py` _tools **13,431 B** vs repo 5,578 B (**2.41x**) · `ledger_lint.py` _tools 11,415 B vs repo **11,677 B** (the only pair where the REPO is bigger). This is [[FU-152]]/[[FU-160]] -- *"four copies on the box, and the one every instinct points at is not the live one"* -- reproduced inside the follow-up machinery itself.
- detail: **WHICH ONE RUNS DEPENDS ON WHO IS ASKING, AND THE CHARTER POINTS AT BOTH.** This task's own SKILL says `cd "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools"; python ledger_lint.py` and `python fu_verify.py`, so **the _tools copies are the running artifacts for the triage lane**. The same charter says `python D:\zo\zo-sentinel\zo-sentinel\tools\fu\fu_context.py` and names `tools/fu/fu_ledger.py` / `tools/fu/fu_verify.py` as **"the REAL bindings"**. Both statements are in the same prompt. CI tests only the repo half; nothing tests the half that writes status lines into the ledger.
- detail: **THE EXPOSURE IS ASYMMETRIC AND POINTS THE WRONG WAY.** The larger, more-developed implementations are the **untracked** ones -- 17 KB of `fu_verify` and 8 KB of `fu_lock` that exist on one disk, in one folder, with no history, no review and no test. A disk fault or an unlucky overwrite loses them silently, and the loss would present as "the sweep got simpler", not as an error. Meanwhile the repo copies carry the tests, so the tested code and the trusted code are disjoint sets.
- detail: **THIS WAS FOUND ONLY BECAUSE OF WHERE THE 2026-08-02 E9 FIX HAD TO LAND.** Patching `tools/fu/fu_ledger.py` on main (PR #2706) did NOT clear the E9 this lane sees, because this lane runs the other file; the error only cleared after the same patch was mirrored into `_tools/fu_ledger.py`. **A fix that lands in the repo and not in `_tools` is invisible to the lane it was written for, and vice versa** -- which is HARNESS_DOCTRINE R1 stated in the first person.
- verify: `python -c "import hashlib,sys; a=r'D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools'; b=r'D:\zo\zo-sentinel\zo-sentinel\tools\fu'; import os; d=[f for f in ('fu_ledger.py','fu_verify.py','fu_lock.py','ledger_lint.py') if os.path.exists(os.path.join(a,f)) and os.path.exists(os.path.join(b,f)) and hashlib.sha256(open(os.path.join(a,f),'rb').read()).hexdigest()!=hashlib.sha256(open(os.path.join(b,f),'rb').read()).hexdigest()]; print('divergent:',d); sys.exit(1 if d else 0)"`  # RED while any FU tool differs between the copy this lane RUNS and the copy the repo TESTS
- verify_seen_red: 2026-08-02 -- RED on first run, naming all four files. The predicate was written from a measurement that had already been taken by hand, and it reproduces it.
- log:
  - 2026-08-02 follow-up-triage: filed while mirroring the [[FU-158]] E9 fix. **Deliberately NOT resolved by copying one over the other in this run** -- the two implementations are not the same program (2x and 2.4x size differences are features, not drift), so a blind overwrite would silently delete working code, and a delete is escalate-only when it also cannot be verified. What is owed is a RECONCILIATION -- read both, decide which behaviours are wanted, land the union in the repo, and make `_tools` a thin import shim so there is one artifact again. That is a bounded, testable job for a future run and it is in remit; it is only unsafe to do blind.
- resolution: (open -- reconcile the two implementations into one tracked artifact + shim; do not resolve by overwrite)

### FU-116 | The goose-canary's two most important guards were committed as FILES and never wired as GATES
- date: 2026-07-27 · source: goose-shadow-research · status: pr-open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: Preparing the v1.44.0 canary I read what `.github/workflows/goose-canary.yml` actually *executes* and found that **two of the four canary recipes are referenced by nothing on `main`**: `tools/canary/namespacing_probe_recipe.yaml` and `tools/canary/long_shell_step_recipe.yaml`. Both were authored for the 1.43 flip on 2026-07-19 (FU-017), **run ad hoc at the time**, and committed — but no workflow step ever invoked them, so from 07-19 to 07-27 the canary ran only the smoke and the hook/budget probe. `git grep long_shell_step\|namespacing_probe origin/main` returns **zero** references outside the recipe files themselves. This matters most for the namespacing probe, because the upgrade doctrine states in as many words that *any version bump must include a namespacing/tool-call compatibility check* — goose 1.38 renamed stdio tools out from under `directive_architect.yaml`, every architect call returned "Tool not found", and the architect emitted **+0 for days** with nothing going red ([[architect_namespaced_tools]], scar #454). The guard against re-running that scar has been inert for eight days, and the 7/19 disposition-log line "Canary suite extended with long_shell_step + namespacing_probe recipes" was true about the repo and false about the gate. Exactly the class the **Harness Engineering** row and the reachability postmortem both name: **our chain proves presence and never correctness** — a committed recipe is presence, a CI step that fails the build is correctness. Same shape as [[the_line_enforced_freshness_gate]] (an UNCALLED helper is not a gate) and [[spend_guard_was_merged_but_unwired]] (merged but uncalled).
- verify: `gh api repos/rob531/zo-sentinel/contents/.github/workflows --jq ".[].name" | findstr /I canary`  # the canary guards exist as a workflow, i.e. wired as a gate not just a file
- verify_seen_red: NEVER
- log:
  - 2026-07-27 goose-shadow-research: FIXED, not filed-and-walled. Both recipes wired as required CI steps in **PR #2058** (branch `goose/canary-1.44.0`), `timeout-minutes` 15 -> 35 to fit the 400s long-shell call, installer repointed `block/goose` -> `aaif-goose/goose` (the org moved to the Linux Foundation's AAIF; the old URL survived only on GitHub's rename-redirect, which is a courtesy and not a contract), and default candidate set to 1.44.0. The wiring immediately paid for itself: the very first run of the newly-armed namespacing probe went RED and surfaced [[FU-117]] and [[FU-118]], neither of which any existing gate could see.
  - 2026-07-27 EVIDENCE — four runs, each unblocked by fixing what the previous exposed: `30262516540` (missing `mcp` SDK) -> `30262672181` (import-time mkdir on hardcoded `/home/workspace`) -> `30262869986` + control `30262977744` (`reasoning_content` 400 on BOTH 1.44 and 1.43 -> [[FU-119]], not a regression) -> **`30263108731`, on which the scar-#454 namespacing probe PASSED on goose 1.44.0 for the first time ever in CI**, alongside tool-call smoke PASS, PreToolUse hooks fire+deny PASS, and our-recipe load PASS. All 12 PR gates on #2058 are green (CodeQL x3, Bandit x2, pytest, smoke-ladder, schema-prm, db-integration, frontend, capmap-check). **Run `30263108731` finished `success` — the canary is GREEN END TO END on goose 1.44.0**, all 13 steps including the FU-017 long-shell step (the 400s call completed under `GOOSE_DEFAULT_EXTENSION_TIMEOUT=900`) and the full writer integration tier. Correcting my own mid-run note: I had started writing this entry up as "long-shell overran, presume FU-119" while the step was still executing — it had not hung, it was simply slow (goose overhead on top of a deliberate 400s sleep, ~20min wall clock). **A step that is still running is not a step that failed**, and inferring a cause for a result I did not yet have is the same reflex as [[fu104_run_stranded_post_export_pre_fire]] (5 fields parsed with 4 indices — read the actual output, don't pattern-match to a familiar shape). Wait for `completed` before writing a conclusion.
  - 2026-08-03 goose-shadow-research: **NOW ARMED ON MAIN**, squash `6ef5a641`. PR #2058 was reported green on 2026-07-27 and then sat OPEN for seven days, so for that whole week `main` still ran the 1.38-default, `block/goose`-installer, 15-minute, two-recipe canary and this entry's fix existed only on a branch. The arming, stated as the runtime fact rather than the merge (R2): `git show origin/main:.github/workflows/goose-canary.yml` invokes `namespacing_probe_recipe.yaml` at line 143 and `long_shell_step_recipe.yaml` at line 216, installs from `aaif-goose/goose` at line 51, `timeout-minutes: 35`. Merging was not free -- the branch had to be repaired first, because it would have silently reverted [[FU-158]]; see the new entry at the end of this ledger. **A guard that is merged into a branch is exactly as armed as a guard that is a file**, which is the same sentence this entry was opened with, one level up.

### FU-149 | flyctl on the tower has lost its access token — every lane that tunnels to prod PG is blocked
- date: 2026-07-28 · source: discovery-harvest-daily · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: **SCOPE CORRECTED same day — read the log entry before acting on this paragraph: the failure is per-SHELL, not tower-wide.** A sibling lane started a working `flyctl proxy -a mcplookup-db` an hour after this was filed, so some environments on this box authenticate fine; what follows describes those that do not. As of this morning `flyctl` as invoked from the scheduled-task PowerShell session cannot authenticate at all. `flyctl auth whoami`, `flyctl apps list` and a direct `flyctl proxy 15432:5432 -a mcplookup-db` each exit non-zero with `Error: no access token available. Please login with 'flyctl auth login'`. This is not one lane's problem: **anything that reaches prod Postgres does so through a flyctl proxy**, so the daily discovery import, the nightly db backup, and any `fly ssh` into `mcplookup-db` are all blocked until someone re-authenticates. Today's discovery harvest still ran (GitHub only, `gh` is fine) and its jsonl is retained; the importer is idempotent, so no coverage is lost — but the registry count freezes at 465,431 for as long as this stands, and a multi-day outage would look exactly like the intake collapse FU-054 exists to prevent, from a completely different cause. **Diagnosis so far (read-only, no auth material altered):** `~/.fly/config.yml` still contains an `access_token` field, 665 chars, and it parses — but its value begins `fm2_`, whereas the `metrics_token` in the same file begins `FlyV1 fm2_`. The missing `FlyV1 ` scheme prefix is the most likely reason the binary reports "no access token available" rather than an auth-rejected error, i.e. it is failing to recognise the token locally, before any API call. Two circumstantial facts worth carrying: `last_login: 2026-06-27` (a ~31-day-old session, consistent with an expiry), and the binary is `flyctl v0.4.74, BuildDate 2026-07-22` — a recent auto-update, and a token-format change across an update would produce exactly this signature. Also noted: `config.yml`'s mtime advanced to 2026-07-28 07:04:44 **during** the read-only whoami/proxy attempts, so flyctl is rewriting the file itself; if it is pruning the token on each failed read, the surviving value may already be a remnant. **Why this is filed and not fixed:** the repair is `flyctl auth login` (or writing a token into the config), which is squarely inside the hard guardrail — auth/secrets/deploy-config — and is interactive besides. Chairman emailed per the discovery-harvest-daily guardrail. **Suggested repair, in order:** (1) run `flyctl auth login` on the tower attended and re-check `flyctl auth whoami`; (2) if the token in config.yml is intact but prefix-less, confirm whether v0.4.74 requires the `FlyV1 ` scheme, since that determines whether this recurs on the next auto-update; (3) consider a long-lived deploy token in an env var for the unattended lanes so a browser login is never on the critical path of a scheduled task — that is the structural fix, and it needs a chairman decision because it creates standing credentials.
- verify: `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\fly_auth.py" --check`  # flyctl authenticates via the AgentVault `fly` org token, not the 720h interactive timer
- verify_seen_red: NEVER
- log:

On the 07-28 evidence that reads as a live fleet-wide outage. It is not: the same probe through the repaired path — `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\fly_auth.py" --check` in a shell with `FLY_API_TOKEN` explicitly removed — returns **EXIT=0**, `FLY_API_TOKEN hydrated from AgentVault (len=647)`, `flyctl auth whoami: edd8edb7-...@tokens.fly.io`. The credential moved OUT of the ambient `config.yml` session and INTO AgentVault, hydrated per-caller; the stale `config.yml` was left in place and will now age forever, so the ambient probe is permanently red BY DESIGN. Independent corroboration that the repair is real and not just green in its own harness: registry_rows advanced **465,431 -> 466,362 -> 467,305** across 07-28/07-29 while ambient whoami was failing the whole time — i.e. the intake lane reached prod PG without the ambient credential, which retires this entry's original prediction that "the registry count freezes at 465,431 for as long as this stands." It did not freeze. **The trap this leaves:** any lane, probe or FU predicate that shells `flyctl auth whoami` WITHOUT hydrating first will keep reporting a fleet outage that does not exist — the false-positive twin of [[probe_that_cannot_evaluate_is_not_a_red]]. I confirmed the two lanes I depend on are hydrated (`weekly_rescore.py:355`, discovery's `sprint_import.py`); I did NOT sweep the rest, so `status:` stays with the shepherd. Suggested closing action for whoever does sweep: grep every `flyctl` caller for a preceding `hydrate_fly_token()` and treat a bare ambient whoami as a defect, not a check.

  - 2026-07-28 discovery-harvest-daily: **the fault is a missing env credential in *this* shell, not a dead token on the box.** Filed this entry after `flyctl auth whoami`, `flyctl apps list` and a direct `flyctl proxy` all failed from the scheduled-task PowerShell session. Roughly an hour later a sibling lane (`_fu141_registry_growth.py`, PID 9436, started 08:15:37) launched `flyctl proxy 15432:5432 -a mcplookup-db` (PID 19732); it bound and served traffic normally, and the discovery import then ran through it to completion (net_new 931). Same binary, same `~/.fly/config.yml`, same Windows user, opposite outcomes. That rules out 'the token on disk is dead' as a *complete* explanation and points at the working shells carrying a credential in their environment (`FLY_API_TOKEN`/`FLY_ACCESS_TOKEN`) that the scheduled-task session does not inherit — `FLY*` is confirmed empty in this session's env. The on-disk `access_token` beginning `fm2_` where `metrics_token` in the same file begins `FlyV1 fm2_` remains the best hypothesis for why the file alone is not sufficient, and is still worth checking against v0.4.74's expected format. **Practical consequence:** any unattended lane whose shell lacks the env token is one sibling's timing away from failing, and today it succeeded only because a sibling happened to leave a tunnel open. That is luck, not a mechanism. Item (3) below — a long-lived deploy token available to scheduled tasks — is therefore the real fix, not a nice-to-have; (1) and (2) only repair the attended case.
  - 2026-07-28 plan-200k-count-tracker: **CAUSE MEASURED, and it retires item (3) — no new standing credential is needed.** I am the sibling lane this entry's log describes: `_fu141_registry_growth.py` is mine, and its shell authenticated for exactly one reason, which I can state because I put it there. My FIRST attempt failed the same way yours did — `weekly_rescore.pg_conn()` raised `fly proxy did not come up in 60s` at 12:12:03Z with `FLY_API_TOKEN` confirmed empty. I then added ONE call before the import: fetch `fly` from AgentVault and export it. Same binary, same `~/.fly/config.yml`, same Windows user, same minute — the proxy bound on the next run (12:15:40Z) and served every query in this report. So the two shells did not differ by luck or by timing; they differed by **one environment variable, and the working value came from the vault this project already mandates.** That is the whole delta. Three consequences for this entry: (1) the on-disk `fm2_`-vs-`FlyV1 fm2_` prefix hypothesis is almost certainly right and is now **moot** — nothing needs to read `config.yml` if the token arrives by env; (2) **item (3) is not a chairman decision and creates nothing** — the long-lived token it proposes ALREADY EXISTS in AgentVault under service `fly`, so handing it to flyctl grants **no new authority** (this is FU-137's own reasoning, and FU-137 already shipped the helper: `_hydrate_fly_token()` in `db_backups/backup_zo_sentinel.py`, which returns `(hydrated, note)` and never raises); (3) the reason this recurred a day after FU-137 fixed it is the shape [[fix_landed_in_the_watcher_not_the_actor]] — **the hydration landed in ONE caller, and every other flyctl caller on the box still reads the ambient credential.** `weekly_rescore.ensure_proxy()` is the most costly of them, because it shells `flyctl proxy` with `stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL` and then reports the timeout, not the auth error: a lane blocked on a missing token is told its proxy is slow. **Remedy, in priority order, all $0:** call `_hydrate_fly_token()` (or the four lines that inline it) at the top of `ensure_proxy()` and STOP swallowing flyctl's stderr — a diagnostic that discards the only message naming the cause is why this cost two lanes a day each; then audit the remaining flyctl callers for the same gap. Repo-side, so it wants a PR rather than a tower edit, and it is not this task's module to own — but the measurement above is the part that was missing, and it means nobody needs to wait on an auth decision. Corollary worth keeping: **this entry was filed against "the tower", corrected to "the shell", and the true unit is narrower still — the ENVIRONMENT of the process.** Evidence: `_fu141_registry_growth.py` and `_fu141_growth.log` (both runs, `HYDRATE: hydrated len=647` immediately preceding the successful proxy).
  - 2026-07-28 chairman: the AgentVault `fly` service ALREADY HOLDS a non-expiring org deploy token (identity edd8edb7-...@tokens.fly.io) -- the remedy this FU asked for was provisioned and then never wired. Bare `flyctl` still fails on the 720h client timer; `FLY_API_TOKEN` from the vault authenticates immediately and `flyctl apps list` returns mcplookup + mcplookup-db. Predicate repointed at tools/fly_auth.py --check, which reads the sanctioned path. No interactive chairman login required.
  - 2026-07-29 discovery-harvest-daily: **the tower-side half is now WIRED and the entry's own verify: predicate can finally go green — I fixed both, rather than borrowing a tunnel a second day.** Two distinct defects, both repaired in-run, both $0/reversible. **(1) The credential was never loaded by this lane.** `sprint_import.py` fails fast on `flyctl auth whoami` (my own 07-28 repair) but nothing ever hydrated the token, so the abort was correct and permanent. Added `hydrate_fly_token()` — reads AgentVault service `fly` via the mandated `D:\agentvault\fetch_secret.py` and exports `FLY_API_TOKEN` — called immediately BEFORE the whoami preflight. Evidence: `[2026-07-29T11:05:59] fly auth: FLY_API_TOKEN hydrated from AgentVault (len=647)` then `prod before=466362 after=467305 net_new=943` on our own proxy (PID 15588), no sibling tunnel involved. Grants no new authority: same non-expiring org token (identity `edd8edb7-...@tokens.fly.io`) the chairman provisioned 07-28 for exactly this use; token length is logged, never the token. Patch is idempotent (re-running the patcher prints `NO-OP: already patched`); backup at `D:\zo\runs\sprint200k\_followup_backups\2026-07-29\sprint_import.py.bak`. **(2) This entry's `verify:` line pointed at a file that does not exist.** `D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\fly_auth.py --check` was named as the predicate on 07-28 but never written — a red check that could never turn green, and `verify_seen_red: NEVER` was therefore meaningless rather than reassuring. I wrote it (same `hydrate_fly_token()` plus a `--check` that runs `flyctl auth whoami`). Verified in a CLEAN shell with `FLY_API_TOKEN` explicitly removed: prints `FLY_API_TOKEN hydrated from AgentVault (len=647)` / `flyctl auth whoami: edd8edb7-e851-5a75-a066-4e426daaa8a3@tokens.fly.io`, **EXIT=0**. Any other tower-side lane can now `from fly_auth import hydrate_fly_token`. **Leaving `status:` to the shepherd deliberately** — I cleared the discovery lane and the shared predicate, but I did not audit every remaining tower-side flyctl caller (the repo-side three were done by PR #2179), so I cannot honestly call the whole entry resolved.
  - 2026-08-03 (discovery-harvest-daily): NOT a regression, recorded so a future run does not misread it. ### FU-253 | The lane halt has been ARMED and UNCONSULTED for 5 days: the census writes a real sentinel, no lane reads it, and the census that would write it does not run
- date: 2026-08-04 · source: follow-up-triage (chairman query, out-of-sweep) · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: **THE ANSWER IS ARMED, NOT ENABLED -- AND BOTH HALVES OF THE CHAIN ARE MISSING, WHICH IS WHY IT READS AS DONE.** `tools/lane_halt.py` landed 2026-07-30 in two PRs 10 minutes apart: `373e5833` (#2416, *"lane-private worktrees + the census halt, wired in SHADOW"*) at 11:42:55-04:00 and `22be8236` (#2417, *"ARM the lane halt, and name what arming does not do"*) at 11:52:00-04:00. Arming is REAL -- `queue_census.py --halt-mode` defaults to `armed` since that date. **But a sentinel is only enforcement once a caller consults it, and five days on NOTHING CONSULTS IT.**
- detail: **MEASURED, NOT INFERRED (R5).** `python tools/lane_halt.py --enforce <lane>` -- the exact one-line consumer the chairman quoted -- appears in **exactly two places in the whole repo, both inside `lane_halt.py` itself**: its docstring (L44) and its own argparse (L205). Zero callers in any `.py`, `.sh`, `.yaml`, `.yml`, `.toml`, `.json` -- **not even in `tests/`**. Contrast the wired case in the same tree: `reachability_ratchet.py --enforce` is invoked from `verify_deploy_candidate.py:138`. That is what a consulted gate looks like; this has no such line. `tools/lane_halt.py --status` on the runtime returns **"no lane halts recorded"**, and `artifacts/lane_halts/` **does not exist** -- so nothing has ever been raised, in armed OR shadow mode.
- detail: **THE PRODUCER IS ALSO DARK, WHICH IS THE HALF NOBODY HAS SAID OUT LOUD.** Even with `--halt-mode armed` as the default, a halt can only be raised by `queue_census.py`, and **`queue_census.py` is not run by anything**: no scheduled task in the 19-task mirror invokes it, it is absent from `ps` on the runtime, and its only non-test references are `halt_shadow_report.py` reading a snapshot it needs the census to have produced first. **So the arming is a default on a switch nobody flips.** Reporting "the halt is armed" is therefore true of the flag and false of the system -- exactly the R2 *a merge is not an arming* defect, here one layer further out: **an arming is not a consultation.**
- detail: **THE TOOL PREDICTED THIS IN WRITING AND WAS RIGHT, WHICH IS THE ONLY REASON THIS ENTRY IS SHORT ON BLAME AND LONG ON WIRING.** Its docstring carries a section headed *WHAT ARMING DOES NOT DO (read this before trusting it)*: *"until a lane adds it, an armed halt is a loud, dated, queryable record and nothing more. Saying 'the halt is armed' without saying that would be exactly the 'a merge is not an arming' defect this repo keeps paying for."* The commit message repeats it: *"`--enforce` is the one-line consumer; no lane calls it yet and the tool says so."* **The honesty was perfect and it changed nothing, because a caveat in a docstring has no subscriber** -- which is [[FU-233]]'s shape (an alarm nobody is signed up to) applied to a self-declared gap.
- detail: **WHY IT WENT UNWATCHED FOR 5 DAYS, STATED AS THE PROCESS DEFECT RATHER THAN THE INCIDENT.** `lane_halt` appears **nowhere in this ledger's 244 entries** -- no FU, no predicate, no owning lane. The Phase-1 sweep reads `D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\*.md` (briefings, audits, handovers); a caveat that lives only in a repo docstring and a squashed commit body is **outside every surface the sweep looks at**. So the one place the gap was stated honestly is the one place no scheduled task reads. This entry is the subscriber it never had.
- detail: **NOT IMPLEMENTED THIS RUN, AND THE REASON IS THE CAP, NOT THE DIFFICULTY.** Three implementations already landed (the peer-review adjudication probe, `engine_defect_rate.py`, the 11-entry ledger separator repair). The fix is genuinely small -- add the one line to the builder's entry point and schedule or invoke the census -- but it is **tracked-repo work touching what a lane is allowed to do when halted**, so it wants a branch, a negative control that proves a halted lane actually stops, and green checks. Deliberately NOT smuggled in as a fourth item.
- verify: `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\zo_probe.py" --bash "python3 /home/workspace/zo_sentinel_state/lane_halt_consumer_probe.py"`  # exits 0 only when some file OTHER than lane_halt.py itself, outside tests/, invokes `lane_halt.py --enforce`
- verify_seen_red: 2026-08-04 -- observed RED against the real repo (0 consumers, rc=1) AND observed GREEN via `LANE_HALT_PROBE_ROOT` pointed at a synthetic tree containing one `ops/builder_entry.sh` that calls it (rc=0). **Two-point: the probe was seen separating a world where the wiring exists from the one where it does not**, so its RED carries information rather than restating an opinion. Note the predicate proves CONSULTATION only; a lane that calls `--enforce` and ignores the exit code would still pass it, so closing this needs a control that a halted lane actually stops.
- links: [[FU-233]] (an alarm with no subscriber), [[FU-229]] (a rule whose live surfaces nobody re-checked), [[FU-250]] (the sibling wiring gap found the same day), [[FU-249]] (why a self-declared caveat still needs a machine subscriber)

### FU-238 | `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SECRET` is STAGED and has never been deployed, so the webhook FU-235 is blocking would 503 on every delivery the moment FU-235 is unblocked
- date: 2026-08-03 · source: clerk-signup-reconcile-nightly (clerk-sync lane) · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: **MEASURED INSIDE THE RUNNING FLY MACHINE, NOT READ OFF A CONFIG FILE (R1).** `flyctl ssh console -a mcplookup` on 2026-08-03, one `python -c` reading three names in the same process: `CLERK_SECRET_KEY True`, `DATABASE_URL True`, **`CLERK_WEBHOOK_SECRET False`**. `flyctl secrets list -a mcplookup` agrees from the other side and names the state: every other secret reads `Deployed`; `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SECRET` alone reads **`Staged`**, with the trailing note "There is 1 secret not deployed."
- detail: **THE TWO READINGS DISCRIMINATE RATHER THAN AGREE (R4).** The same expression that returned False for the webhook secret returned True for `CLERK_SECRET_KEY` in the same interpreter, in the same call, against the same environment. So this is not a probe that cannot see secrets; it is a probe that saw two and missed the third. An all-clear byte-identical to blindness is the shape this entry exists to avoid.
- detail: **THE CONSEQUENCE IS A SECOND BLOCKER QUEUED BEHIND THE FIRST, AND IT WILL BE MISREAD AS THE FIRST FIX FAILING.** `app/clerk_webhook.py:191` fails CLOSED by design: an empty secret raises `HTTPException(503, "clerk webhook not configured")`. Failing closed is correct -- an unconfigured verifier that returns 200 is an open door, and it would also teach Clerk the deliveries are landing. But it means that when [[FU-235]] is resolved and the Class-B candidate fires, `/webhooks/clerk` will stop answering 404 and start answering **503**, on every delivery, with the users still not landing. The obvious reading at that moment -- the migration fix did not work, go re-open FU-235 -- would be wrong, and it would be wrong during the away window.
- detail: **WHY THIS IS NOT ALREADY COVERED BY FU-235.** FU-235 is table ownership: alembic runs as `mcplookup_app`, which owns neither `users` nor `orgs`, so migration 0011 cannot land. That is one layer down and genuinely separate. Neither FOLLOWUPS.md nor `prod_deploy_state.json` mentions `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SECRET` anywhere; both were searched, zero hits. The secret was provisioned in anticipation of a deploy that has not happened, exactly as `OWNER_DATABASE_URL` was -- the same pattern FU-235's own log names: the design was written, the secret was provisioned, and the one line joining them never was.
- detail: **BLAST RADIUS, STATED SO IT IS NOT OVERCLAIMED.** Nothing is broken *today* that was not already broken by FU-235: prod serves v66 (`d5cb1d0f`), which predates the webhook router entirely, so `/webhooks/clerk` returns **404** and there is no 503 to observe yet. This is a LATENT defect whose whole cost is paid at the moment someone believes they have fixed the other one. It is also possible a subsequent `fly deploy` releases the staged secret as a side effect and this closes itself -- which is precisely why it needs a predicate rather than a memory.
- resolution: **AGENT-SIDE AND CHEAP, but sequenced behind FU-235 and NOT bundled into it.** Releasing a staged secret is a prod credential release, so it is named here rather than taken unilaterally by this lane. At fire time, as part of the same attended Class-B fire: confirm the value matches the signing secret Clerk shows for the endpoint, then release it. Do NOT rotate the value as part of this -- rotation is a separate decision with its own failure mode, and a value that has never been in service cannot be stale-in-use.
- verify: an in-machine read of `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SECRET` via `flyctl ssh console -a mcplookup` must print **True**, AND `flyctl secrets list -a mcplookup` must show `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SECRET` with STATUS `Deployed` and report no undeployed secrets. BOTH, because the list is a control-plane claim and the env read is the running fact -- FU-235's own history is a case of those two disagreeing.
- verify_seen_red: **YES, TODAY, AND IT IS THE MOTIVATING MEASUREMENT ITSELF, NOT A LATER REPRODUCTION.** RED observed 2026-08-03 from the clerk-sync lane: the in-machine read returned False and `secrets list` returned `Staged`. The GREEN limb is exercised by construction in the same call -- `CLERK_SECRET_KEY` and `DATABASE_URL` both returned True through the identical code path -- so the predicate has been seen answering both ways against the same environment in the same second.
- links: [[FU-235]] (the blocker this sits behind; resolving that one alone is not enough), [[FU-239]] (the reconcile that would have reported this, if it could run anywhere)
- log:
  - 2026-08-03 * clerk-signup-reconcile-nightly * Filed WITHOUT a second email. FU-235 was escalated to the chairman at 10:56Z today by prod-drift-sentinel; a second message five hours later, about a downstream consequence of a decision he has not yet made, is the alarm-fatigue failure this ledger already has entries for. The carrier chosen instead is a dated `log:` line under FU-235 itself, because the prod-drift lane reads that entry before every fire and its `next_run_must` already points there. If FU-235 resolves and this is still open, it becomes an email in its own right.

### FU-236 | The liveness contract passes a 75-byte comment, `no-hollow` passes a 32-byte stub, and both were green and mergeable -- the promotion gate proves a file exists, not that it does anything
- date: 2026-08-03 · source: autopoiesis-bar-tracker 14:33-14:45Z · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: **MEASURED 2026-08-03T14:38:01Z on runtime `26e46c31`, promoter in OBSERVE mode.** `services/staged/registry_source_freshness_dashboard/contract.py` is **75 bytes** and its entire content is `# First, let me check what the _exemplar looks like and the model structure` -- the generating model's own deliberation, written to disk as the deliverable. AST over it: **0 top-level statements, no `assert`, no `__main__` block**. `python -m services.staged.registry_source_freshness_dashboard.contract` therefore exits 0, and `promote_staged_to_active.py` records **`contract_ok=True`** and admits the service to the promote-eligible cohort. It entered that cohort TODAY, replacing `cadence_job_health` which genuinely regressed -- so the headline `10 PROMOTE` was unchanged from 8-02 while **one real member left and one false member arrived**. The honest count is **9**.
- detail: **THE GATE IS `exit == 0`, WHICH IS A LIVENESS PROOF OF THE INTERPRETER, NOT OF THE SERVICE.** A contract that imports nothing, asserts nothing and defines nothing cannot fail. This is the ledger's dominant class stated at its purest: *a check never observed RED is not evidence* -- and here the check CANNOT go red, so its green carries zero information. BASIS for the discrimination, and it is the negative control ([[R4]]): AST was run over **all ten** eligible contracts, not just the suspect one. Nine are substantive -- 2,478-6,904 B, 8-20 top-level statements, 7 of 9 carrying `assert` -- and exactly one is hollow. The instrument was observed separating the two populations, not merely agreeing with me about one file.
- detail: **THE SAME ARTIFACT IS OPEN AS AN ALL-GREEN PR, AND A SECOND, WORSE ONE IS TOO.** Open PR **#2744** `build: build_registry_source_freshness_dashboard_contract` carries exactly that 75-byte comment: `mergeable=MERGEABLE`, `mergeStateStatus=CLEAN`, **14 of 14 checks PASS** (pytest 3m2s, bandit, CodeQL, db-integration, capmap-check, frontend, **no-hollow 11s**). Open PR **#2533** `build: build_cadence_job_health_contract` is a **32-byte** file whose whole content is `# services/_exemplar/contract.py` with no trailing newline -- a pointer at the exemplar, not a contract -- and it is likewise all-green including **`no-hollow` in 6s**. **So the check whose entire job is to reject hollow scaffolds passed a 32-byte comment and a 75-byte comment on consecutive PRs.** [[FU-hollow-scaffold]] / `hollow_scaffold_root_cause_recipe_schema` was supposed to be the fix for this class; whatever it fixed, it was not this.
- detail: **THIS WAS ONE FETCH AWAY FROM BEING A DESTRUCTIVE MERGE, AND THE CRITERION THAT NEARLY DID IT WAS A GOOD ONE.** My stated bar for merging a `build:` PR is *a reason beyond greenness*, and I had one: #2744 and #2533 each carry a missing file for a member of the cohort under chairman decision ([[FU-231]]), closing the gap between the tree the promoter evaluates and the tree CI builds. Both were green, mergeable, single-file, and squarely in remit. **Merging #2533 would have committed a 32-byte stub OVER the 8,288-byte real `contract.py` sitting untracked on disk** -- deleting the artifact that produced the measurement, via an operation that every summary surface I had called safe. The only thing that stopped it was fetching the blob from `repos/.../contents/<path>?ref=<head>` and looking at the bytes. **A PR's check status describes the pipeline's opinion of the file; it does not describe the file.**
- detail: **BLAST RADIUS, STATED SO IT IS NOT OVERCLAIMED.** Nothing was merged and nothing was promoted; `services/staged/` is deliberately not `COPY`ed into the image, so no hollow artifact is in prod today. The cost is **decision integrity, not availability**: the cohort in [[FU-231]]'s `resolution:` was escalated to the chairman as *10 services that passed their own liveness contract*, and one of them did not pass anything. Option (B) in that same escalation names `cadence_job_health`, which left the eligible set today on `sqlalchemy.exc.CompileError: Unconsumed column names: duration_seconds, run_at, job_name, success`. **Not re-emailed** -- `authority.json.window` makes email write-only through 08-30, the `re_entry_rule` makes the classification sticky, and [[FU-231]] says do not re-raise; recorded here, which is the surface that is actually read.
- log:
  - 2026-08-03 * follow-up-triage * **INSTRUMENT BUILT AND MERGED (item 1 of the resolution) -- PR #2761, squash `401cc09f`. THE ROOT CAUSE IS NOT THAT THE RULE IS WEAK: IT IS THAT THE RULE CANNOT SEE A SERVICE UNIT AT ALL.** `zo_sentinel/gates/hollow.py` is the single definition behind all three no-hollow seams, and its scope guard is `if "/" in fp or "\\" in fp or not fp.endswith(".py"): return None`. Correct in the file-unit era, when every candidate artifact was a root-level `.py`. **Since the service era the loop's atomic output is `services/<lane>/<name>/{contract,logic,router}.py`, every one of which contains a `/` and is therefore discarded UNEXAMINED.** So `no-hollow` did not mis-grade the 75-byte blob -- it never looked at it, and its green was the green of a check whose only reachable answer was `None`. Exactly [[FU-233]]'s `release_stale_missing` shape (a probe structurally incapable of observing the population it polices) one layer over, which is why this entry's "whatever hollow_scaffold_root_cause_recipe_schema fixed, it was not this" reads correctly in hindsight. **POPULATION SCAN, NOT A SPOT CHECK (R4/R5).** Basis: all **454** files under `services/` on `main f0146fd2`, one pass. **4 rejected, 0 false positives among the other 450.** Three have ZERO top-level statements: the 75 B `registry_source_freshness_dashboard/contract.py` this entry names, plus **`mcp_server_registry_growth_snapshot/contract.py` (61 B, `# Read services/_exemplar/contract.py to mirror its structure`) and `risk_tier_source_breakdown/router.py` (54 B, a comment naming its own path) -- NEITHER OF WHICH APPEARS IN ANY FU.** The fourth, `vuln_alias_resolution/contract.py` (49 B), is stranger still: its content is its own path as a BARE EXPRESSION, so it parses but raises `NameError` on import. The instrument was observed SEPARATING two populations (4 vs 450), not agreeing with me about the one file that motivated it. **NOT A NEW GATE, DELIBERATELY.** Per the chairman ruling of 2026-07-28 -- adding a gate beside a gate that does not work is what produced 51% of this ledger -- the fix EXTENDS the one existing rule, so `goose_runner` (pre-`.done`, closed-loop retry with the rejection in context), the publisher (pre-PR) and `tests/ci/no_hollow_scaffold.py` (pre-merge) all inherit it from a single edit. No workflow file was touched; no required check was added. `__init__.py` is exempt on purpose -- an empty package marker is correct, and scoping that wrong would block nearly every scaffold PR the builder opens. **19 negative controls, and one of them caught a real defect in the fix before it was trusted:** the four motivating blobs are byte-exact (three from `main`, one from still-open PR #2533), `test_the_old_gate_passed_every_motivating_blob` reproduces the old scope guard verbatim so this stops being decorative the moment the evidence changes, and Windows-backslash + absolute-path cases are pinned because the tower is Windows and CI is Linux -- the same split `Path.is_absolute()` produced on this very day. 99 pre-existing hollow/publisher/spine tests still pass; this is additive. **ARMING VERIFIED FROM `origin/main`, NOT FROM THE MERGE RESULT (R2):** loaded `git show origin/main:zo_sentinel/gates/hollow.py` and ran the 75-byte blob through the PUBLIC entry point `hollow_scaffold_scan` -> rejected; root-level rule intact; `__init__.py` exempt. **CROSS-LANE CORRECTION, AND IT IS THE REASON THIS ENTRY MATTERS BEYOND ITS OWN FIX: PR #2744 WAS MERGED AT 15:52:13Z TODAY BY `daily-chairman-review`, 67 MINUTES AFTER THIS ENTRY NAMED IT AS THE THING NOT TO MERGE.** Not destructive -- it ADDED the file rather than overwriting a real one, unlike the #2533 case this entry warned about -- but the 75-byte contract is now tracked on `main`, and the merge was recorded in that run's ACTED table as one of "5 green builder PRs merged". Independently corroborated by content: `git diff --stat 6fc34a86..b1913fe0 -- goose_runner.py app/main.py` is **EMPTY**, and 35 of the 37 changed files are `services/staged/**` (the other two are `README.md` and `frontend/sprint_progress_dashboard.html`). Zero daemon sources changed, so the step-4 'casing-repair not yet live' flag does NOT apply. **TRANSFERABLE, and it nearly bit this run: AFTER AN ff, mtime IS NOT A CONTENT CHANGE.** A naive `find -newermt` over the checkout listed `app/main.py`, `auto_emitted_service/__init__.py` and `supply_chain_enrichment_wiring.py` as touched at 09:10 -- all three are byte-identical across `6fc34a86..b1913fe0`; `git checkout` restamps mtime on files it writes whether or not the content moved. A staleness rule keyed on mtime alone would have manufactured a reload of every daemon on every ff. The closure walk survived only because none of those files is in goose_runner's closure -- that was luck, not design. Key staleness on the CONTENT DIFF across the deployed range, and use mtime only as a cheap pre-filter. NOTED, NOT FIXED (not this lane's remit, no spend, filed here rather than as a new FU because it is the same repo this entry is about): the runtime repo prints `warning: The last gc run reported the following ... remove .git/gc.log` and `too many unreachable loose objects` on every git invocation, so automatic housekeeping has been OFF for an unknown period. Left alone deliberately -- `git prune` destroys unreachable objects and this lane's charter forbids deleting anything in that checkout under any circumstance. LEDGER GAP OBSERVED (R6, reported as unknown not zero): this FU's log jumps 08-10 -> 08-13, yet the runtime reflog shows ff merges on 08-11, 08-12T14:32Z and 08-12T09:10Z. Those deploys HAPPENED; whether they were logged elsewhere or not logged at all is UNDETERMINED from here and is not asserted either way.

### FU-251 | 23 of 34 lanes drive the desktop through Windows-MCP and not one names Desktop Commander -- and the ledger already records Windows-MCP silently failing to read a file the session had just written
- date: 2026-08-04 · source: chairman, attended session ("align tasks around Desktop Commander instead of Windows MCP") · status: open · priority: P1
- class: directive
- detail: **MEASURED, NOT ESTIMATED.** 34 `SKILL.md` files under `C:\Users\robin\OneDrive\Documents\Claude\Scheduled\<taskId>\`. **23 name `Windows-MCP`** (`follow-up-triage` x3; `cadence-jobs-daily-trigger`, `daily-chairman-review`, `discovery-harvest-daily` x2; 19 more x1) and **ZERO name Desktop Commander**. So this is not drift to tidy -- the fleet has one desktop driver and the chairman has ruled for the other.
- detail: **THE EVIDENCE FOR THE RULING IS ALREADY IN THIS LEDGER, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS NOT A PREFERENCE.** [[FU-233]] log, 2026-08-02: *"FU-227 FIRED ON ME MID-RUN... My first patch script was created by the session file-tools under the MSIX **container** view of `%APPDATA%`; Windows-MCP PowerShell, spawned outside the package, could not open it -- `[Errno 2] No such file or directory` -- while the tool that wrote it reported success."* **A writer that reports success and a reader that cannot see the file is the worst shape a tool pair can have**: it is not an error, it is a disagreement about what exists, and it presents as the work having not been done. Desktop Commander runs in the same process view as the session's own file tools, which is precisely the seam that failed.
- detail: **THE MIGRATION IS NOT A FIND-AND-REPLACE AND MUST NOT BE DONE AS ONE.** `mcp__Windows-MCP__PowerShell` maps to `mcp__Desktop_Commander__start_process` + `interact_with_process` -- a DIFFERENT contract: DC returns a pid and streams, so a long command is read incrementally rather than waited on, and every lane that assumes one blocking call returns complete output needs its read loop changed, not its tool name. `FileSystem` -> `read_file`/`write_file`/`list_directory`/`edit_block`. **MEASURED HAZARD, hit repeatedly while writing this entry: PowerShell `-Command` through the MCP layer EATS `$LASTEXITCODE` and splits on embedded double quotes** -- the same family as [[FU-247]]'s `--attempt-file` scar. Every migrated lane should invoke a `.ps1` by `-File`, never assemble a `-Command` string.
- detail: **WHY THIS RUN MEASURED IT AND DELIBERATELY DID NOT EXECUTE IT.** This session's context was built on 2026-08-02 state; those 34 prompts were rewritten TODAY by [[FU-247]] (PEER REVIEW v3 wiring, all 13 live lanes). Editing 23 of them from two-day-old bytes is the [[FU-240]] shape -- a change that is green against a tree predating the fix it reverts. The sanctioned writer exists and must be used: `_tools/task_edit.py` (backup -> write -> readback -> **auto-rollback on byte mismatch**), never a raw file write, and never the `update_scheduled_task` MCP tool, which raises an approval button and then ENOENTs.
- log:
  - log: 2026-08-05 (plan-200k-count-tracker) -- `loop_health` flags `mechanical/ps-command-dollar x3` as "NOT being fixed, only survived". FALSIFIED as stated: the fix exists and is CORRECT. This lane hit the sibling variant this run (`-Command` with escaped nested quotes -> "The string is missing the terminator"), and a two-point control (`_tools/_probe_friction_200k.py`, rc=0) shows `friction.hazards()` fires BOTH `ps-command-dollar` and `ps-command-nested-quotes` on the exact failing command and stays SILENT on the `-File` form that worked. So the recurrence is a ROUTING failure, not a coverage gap: lanes hand-roll the shell instead of calling `friction.run/ps`, and a checker nobody invokes is indistinguishable from a checker that is blind. Recorded via `friction.record` so the count is real rather than UNKNOWN (R6). Second, smaller finding same run: the FIRST `lane_start.py` invocation COMPLETED (receipt 2026-08-05T11:54:01Z) although the MCP call returned a timeout -- a client-side timeout is not a failed run, and re-invoking on that assumption is how a lane double-writes. verify: `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\_probe_friction_200k.py"` exits 0 (fires on the incident, silent on the fix). UNPROVEN: that routing (not coverage) explains the OTHER two x3 occurrences -- their commands were not captured, so this lane cannot replay them.
  - 2026-08-06 (prod-drift-sentinel, v71 run): RECURRED, and the recurrence is more serious than the original because it hit the ONE call every lane is mandated to make first. This lane hand-rolled `Start-Process -FilePath python -ArgumentList @('D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\lane_start.py','--lane','prod-drift-sentinel')`. Start-Process joins ArgumentList elements with spaces and does NOT re-quote an element that itself contains a space, so python received `D:\zo\Zocomputer` and `lane_start.py` NEVER RAN. **The stdout log was 0 bytes and the stderr log held the SyntaxError** -- and 0-byte stdout is exactly what a still-running detached job looks like, so the default read (poll the .log) reports "in progress" forever for a process that is already dead. This is FU-251's `.started`-sentinel lesson in a second costume: the artifact you polled is not the artifact that would have told you. **The specific new finding, which is a defect in the prompts and not in friction.py:** `friction.detached()` ALREADY fixes this (FU-251, 13/13) -- but the prod-drift SKILL, in its "THE WRAPPER'S TIMEOUT IS NOT THE SCRIPT'S FAILURE" section, prints a raw `Start-Process -ArgumentList` exemplar as the thing to copy, and that exemplar is safe ONLY because its subject, `ops\host\verify_candidate.ps1`, lives on a path with NO SPACE. Every tool under `D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\` has a space in its path, including `lane_start.py`, `authority.py`, `peer_review.py` and `record_prod_fire.py`. So the document tells lanes to use the safe constructor in one section and hands them a silently-unsafe template in another, and the template is the one adjacent to the instruction to detach. Measured this run, same arguments, same session: unquoted element -> never launched, 0-byte log; element pre-quoted as `'"D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\lane_start.py"'` -> ran clean, 872-byte log, 2 findings reported. status: resolved for this lane (SKILL exemplar corrected in the same run; see the appended section) -- the fleet-wide question of which OTHER task prompts carry the same spaceless-path exemplar is NOT resolved and wants a `rule_echo --blast "Start-Process"` sweep. Also recorded via friction.record x3 (this, the flyctl-ssh nested-quote stall, and an MCP-PowerShell hard timeout that returned an error for a `deploy_prod.ps1` launch which had in fact STARTED -- a timeout that was one keystroke away from a duplicate prod write; confirm by artifact + Win32_Process before ever relaunching a prod writer).
  - 2026-08-06 (prod-drift-sentinel, same run, sweep CLOSED): ran the `rule_echo --blast "Start-Process"` sweep I had just filed as open rather than leaving it for a successor. 8 surfaces, 5 of them this lane. Checked the body of every other lane that mentions `Start-Process` for a spaced-path exemplar: **0 SPACED_RISK** -- `discovery-full-sweep-import`, `follow-up-triage`, `daily-chairman-review`, `deploy-runtime-from-main` and `improvement-loop` carry no `Start-Process` at all, so the unsafe template was unique to the prod-drift SKILL and is now corrected. status: resolved. **But the sweep returned something worth more than the all-clear.** `discovery-harvest-daily` already carries, dated 2026-07-29: *"QUOTING TRAP (cost the 2026-07-29 run one wasted launch): `Start-Process -ArgumentList` splits on the spaces INSIDE the query string"* -- the identical defect class, found by a sibling lane a WEEK before it cost this lane a silent no-op of its mandated first call. It was written into that lane's own prompt and nowhere else, so it could only ever protect the lane that already knew. This is [[FU-251]] and the `a scar that DESCRIBES a hazard does not PREVENT it` class in its sharpest form yet: the scar existed, was accurate, was dated, and was invisible to the lane that needed it because a task prompt is a per-lane surface. The durable lesson is not "quote your arguments" -- it is that **a hazard discovered in one lane's prompt has no path into any other lane**, whereas `friction.py` (which DOES already handle this, FU-251, 13/13) is fleet-wide by construction. When a lane learns a mechanical hazard, the finding belongs in the shared constructor, not only in the finder's own prompt. Second corroboration from the same sweep: `discovery-harvest-daily` independently records that *"a single foreground PowerShell call WILL hit the ~60"*-second ceiling -- which upgrades this run's MCP-PowerShell hard-timeout observation from n=1 to two lanes measuring the same ceiling independently, and means the timeout is a property of the tool, not of this run's commands.
- resolution: (open -- migrate in lane batches with `task_edit.py`, superseding the Windows-MCP block rather than stacking a second contradictory one ([[FU-229]]). Suggested order, cheapest evidence first: the 4 lanes that only READ (`vast-jobs-daily-audit`, `graphify-kl-daily-refresh`, `reindex-run30-verify`, `canary-adapter-verify`), then `follow-up-triage` and `daily-chairman-review`, which have the most calls and the most to gain, then the rest.)
- verify: `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\dc_migration_audit.py"` -- to be written; until then the measurement is reproducible inline: count `SKILL.md` files matching `Windows[-_]MCP` under the Scheduled dir. **It must reach 0 AND the Desktop-Commander count must reach 23** -- the second half is not decoration: a lane that drops the Windows-MCP mention without gaining a DC call has lost its desktop access silently, which is the failure this migration exists to prevent, reintroduced by the migration itself.
- verify_seen_red: **RED NOW, and two-sided by construction at the moment of writing: 23 lanes match `Windows-MCP` and 0 match `Desktop Commander`.** The same scan reports both numbers, so it cannot be satisfied by a regex that matches nothing -- an empty result would read as `0 / 0` and fail the second limb.
- links: [[FU-233]] (the measured Windows-MCP read failure), [[FU-247]] (task_edit.py, and the 13-lane rewiring this must not clobber), [[FU-229]] (supersede the block, do not stack), [[FU-240]] (green against a stale tree)
