### FU-265 | `PEER_CLEARABLE` was a permission value no code path could read, so the 2026-08-04 ruling was never executable in `authority.py`
- opened: 2026-08-06
- lane: clerk-signup-reconcile-nightly
- priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: **CHAIRMAN ACT, GIVEN IN SESSION 2026-08-06 ("skip approvals set confirm (vacation mode)"), against an explicit four-way choice in which he selected *reconcile authority.json to the 08-04 ruling*.** The 2026-08-04 ruling (`cofc_2026-08-04_peer_review_replaces_chairman_gate.md`) moved four clauses off the chairman gate and onto a sibling-lane adversary. `authority.json` was never updated: `away_conduct.clause_disposition` still read **HELD** for `redefining_the_metric`, `irreversible_and_unverifiable` and `new_standing_credentials` -- two days of a governance file disagreeing with the ruling that governs it, which is [[the class that already cost this fleet 20 stages / 452 commits of drift]].
- detail: **THE DEFECT IS NOT THE STALE VALUE. IT IS THAT THE CORRECT VALUE WOULD ALSO HAVE DONE NOTHING.** Read from the code before editing it: `_disposition()` returns any string the file supplies, but `may()` compared it against exactly ONE literal -- `"DECIDE_AND_LOG"` (two sites, lines 423 and 427 pre-change). **Writing `PEER_CLEARABLE` into `authority.json` would have been byte-equivalent, to every branch that matters, to leaving it `HELD`**: a permission surface that reads as reconciled and grants nothing, in the one file whose entire purpose is to be executed rather than read. That is [[the_supersedes_prose_array_was_never_read_by_anything]] pointed at the envelope itself, and it is why this entry is `class: defect` and not a config edit.
- detail: **WHAT WAS BUILT (R7, recovery not restriction -- no new gate, no new required step for any lane).** `authority.py` gains `_peer_cleared()` + `_peer_clearable()`: a `PEER_CLEARABLE` clause grants only when the caller cites `--peer-ref <id>` naming a decision in `peer_decisions.json` whose state is **CLEARED or ACTED**. PROPOSED does not grant -- the 08-04 ruling replaced the chairman with an adversary that must RUN a falsification and FAIL, so a proposal nobody has attempted is a claim, not a clearance. An id the store has never heard of returns UNKNOWN, never a pass (R6). The block message names the `peer_review --propose` route and explicitly does NOT name the chairman.
- detail: **CHECKED BEFORE THE AWAY TEST, ON PURPOSE.** The 08-04 ruling is PERMANENT, not a window relaxation. A clause that came off the chairman gate only while he was away would snap back on 08-31 and silently re-stall every lane that had learned the peer route. Self-test point *"PEER_CLEARABLE survives the away window closing"* pins that.
- detail: **THREE FIXTURES AGED OUT MID-RUN AND THE CAUSE IS WORTH MORE THAN THE FIX.** At 2026-08-06T10:57:05Z a SIBLING SESSION armed the away window one day early (`away_from` 2026-08-07 -> **2026-08-06**) on the chairman's same instruction -- a correct, well-documented, reversible change. Forty-two seconds after this lane took its backup. In that instant three `authority.py --self-test` checks went RED **without anything they grade having changed**: `before = datetime(2026, 8, 6, 12, 0)` was suddenly INSIDE the window, and a fourth check called `may("invent_a_new_action", env)` with **no `now` argument at all**, grading the wall clock. `--self-test` is an `improve_loop` FLOOR member, and a red floor makes `select()` refuse to start a cycle -- so a stale date in a test file is not cosmetic, it can brick the engine. Both are now pinned to 2026-08-05, a day clear of the boundary rather than derived from `away_from`, because a fixture computed from the value under test cannot disagree with it.
- detail: **A CONFLICT IN THE RECORD, STATED RATHER THAN RESOLVED IN MY OWN FAVOUR.** The sibling's arming note asserts the chairman "was offered the option to move a HELD clause or raise a spend ceiling in the same breath and **declined both**". This lane was told the opposite forty minutes later and holds an explicit selection of *reconcile to the 08-04 ruling*. The two are reconcilable -- declining ad-hoc widening bundled with an unrelated change is not declining to align the file with an existing ruling -- but they are not identical, and a lane that quietly picked the answer authorising its own edit would be its own appellate court. So this act was routed through the mechanism instead of asserted: the window is now ACTIVE, `auth_config_rewrite` is dispositioned DECIDE_AND_LOG, and this FU is the recorded decision that grant requires. **Nothing here touches `data_deletion` or `above_the_ceilings`; both remain FOREVER_HELD and no peer process may reach them.** Regression control pinned in the suite: `data_deletion` still refuses with a CLEARED peer decision cited.
- verify: `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\authority.py" --self-test` must exit **0** with **52/52**, AND `--may redefining_the_metric` with no `--peer-ref` must print `PEER-CLEARABLE, NOT YET CLEARED` and name `--propose`, AND the same call with `--peer-ref` citing a CLEARED decision must print `PEER-CLEARED`, AND `--may data_deletion --peer-ref <that same CLEARED id>` must still refuse with `ESCALATE-ONLY`.
- verify_seen_red: **YES, BOTH LIMBS, MEASURED THIS RUN AND NOT ASSERTED.** The pre-change interpreter is retained at `_governance_snapshots\authority.py.bak_20260806T065623Z` and scores **41/44, rc=1** with the three named failures; the post-change one scores **52/52, rc=0**. The eight new PEER_CLEARABLE points were each observed answering both ways against a synthetic store (CLEARED and ACTED grant; PROPOSED, FALSIFIED and an unknown id do not), so the granting branch has been seen refusing and the refusing branch has been seen granting.
- log:
  - 2026-08-06 clerk-signup-reconcile-nightly * filed as the recorded decision for the `auth_config_rewrite` DECIDE_AND_LOG grant that authorises the `clause_disposition` edit. Backups: `_governance_snapshots\authority.json.bak_20260806T065623Z` and `authority.py.bak_20260806T065623Z`. REVERT = restore both; no state migration, `PEER_CLEARABLE` is additive and an unrecognised disposition falls through to the pre-existing HELD behaviour. Spend $0. status: open
  - 2026-08-06T11:12Z clerk-signup-reconcile-nightly * **VERIFY PREDICATE GREEN ON ALL FOUR LIMBS, AND THE THIRD LIMB WAS RED FIRST FOR THE EXACT REASON THIS ENTRY EXISTS.** Measured by `_staging/verify_fu265.py` against the LIVE `authority.json` and the LIVE `peer_decisions.json` (not a fixture): (1) `--self-test` **52/52 rc=0**; (2) `--may redefining_the_metric` with no ref prints `PEER-CLEARABLE, NOT YET CLEARED` and names `--propose`, never the chairman; (3) the same call with `--peer-ref fu231-first-cohort-peer-clearable` (state CLEARED in the live store) prints `PEER-CLEARED` and ALLOWS; (4) `--may data_deletion --peer-ref <that same CLEARED id>` still prints `ESCALATE-ONLY ... FOREVER_HELD. Not peer-clearable.` **THE FIRST RUN FAILED LIMBS 3 AND 4 WITH AN ARGPARSE USAGE DUMP, AND THAT IS THE FINDING, NOT AN EMBARRASSMENT:** `may()` had gained the `peer_ref` parameter, the eight self-test points exercised it and passed **52/52**, and the library was completely correct -- but `--peer-ref` had never been added to the CLI, so **no lane on this box could reach the capability by the only route lanes actually use.** A green self-test over a library nobody can invoke is precisely the shape this entry was filed about, reproduced one layer out and inside the fix for it. It survived a passing suite because the suite calls `may()` directly. Cured by adding the argument and threading it through `main()`; the predicate now exercises the CLI end-to-end, which is why it is written as four `subprocess` calls to `authority.py` rather than four imports. **NEGATIVE CONTROL IS THE PRE-CHANGE INTERPRETER, RETAINED AND RE-RUN SIDE BY SIDE, NOT ASSERTED:** `_governance_snapshots\authority.py.bak_20260806T065623Z` scores **41/44 rc=1**, the current one **52/52 rc=0**, both executed in the same run by `_staging/st_diff.py`. Three of those baseline failures were the aged-out fixtures this entry describes; they are fixed, and the count rose by 8 because 8 points were added, so the two numbers are reconcilable rather than merely both green. `clause_disposition` now reads PEER_CLEARABLE for `redefining_the_metric`, `irreversible_and_unverifiable`, `new_standing_credentials`; `data_deletion` and `above_the_ceilings` unchanged at HELD and pinned by a regression control. Backups: `_governance_snapshots\authority.json.pre-peerclearable-20260806T110915Z` (config) and `authority.py.bak_20260806T065623Z` (interpreter). Spend $0, no deploy, no schema change. status: open

### FU-267 | The credit-ledger dedup compared an int id to argparse's str, so ONE $25 top-up counted twice and the budget published a FALSE RED
- opened: 2026-08-06
- lane: vast-jobs-daily-audit
- priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: **FOUND AND FIXED INSIDE THE RUN THAT TRIGGERED IT.** `tools/ops_audit_state.py::record_credit()` deduped top-ups on the RAW `--id` value. Vast invoice `3148330` had been stored by an earlier caller as **int** `3148330`; this lane re-records it every morning through argparse, which carries no `type=`, so it arrives as **str** `"3148330"`. `("id", 3148330) != ("id", "3148330")`, the filter matched nothing, and the same funding event was appended a second time. Observed live: `credits_ever` $25.00 -> **$50.00**, `since_funding.spend_usd` $10.23 -> **$35.23**, `budget.level` GREEN -> **RED** against a $20 red line. The account has been funded exactly once, for $25, and still held $14.77 at the moment of the alarm. Under the away window that RED emails the chairman a budget overrun that did not happen -- an instrument manufacturing the fault it exists to detect. Same shape as [[FU-265]]: a value graded against a single literal spelling.
- detail: **THE DEEPER FINDING, AND THE REASON THIS SHIPPED: `tests/test_ops_audit_state.py` HAD 25 TESTS AND CI COLLECTED NONE OF THEM.** The file was never added to `evaluator.yml`'s pytest allowlist, so no PR has ever run it. Worse, it already contained `test_credit_recording_is_idempotent_on_id`, written against **this exact invoice number**, which passes `int` BOTH times -- exercising the one case that was never broken. A fixture written by the same understanding that wrote the code agrees with the code. This is the R4 lesson stated in this lane's own SKILL, now paid for a second time: the FIRST proof of a detector must be the incident, in the types it actually arrives in.
- detail: **NEGATIVE CONTROL RUN, AND ONE VACUOUS ATTEMPT DISCARDED.** Against the pre-fix blob restored byte-for-byte from `HEAD`: 3 new tests observed **RED** with a real `AssertionError: assert 2 == 1`, the credits list showing `'id': '3148330'` and `'id': 3148330` side by side; 1 control (distinct invoices must never collapse) observed **GREEN in both**, which is what makes it a control and not a second positive. An earlier attempt at the same control failed with `AttributeError: module has no attribute 'record_credit'` because a PowerShell `git show | Set-Content` mangled the restored file -- that run proved nothing and is NOT counted. Post-fix: 29 collected, 29 passed.
- evidence: PR [#2926](https://github.com/rob531/zo-sentinel/pull/2926), branch `fix/ops-audit-credit-dedup-cross-type`, commit `2f8397c2`. Files: `tools/ops_audit_state.py` (new `_cid()` normalises the key at BOTH ends), `tests/test_ops_audit_state.py` (+4 tests), `.github/workflows/evaluator.yml` (+1 allowlist line -- the arming action, not the merge). Live state repaired through the fixed helper: credits 2 -> 1, `since_funding.spend_usd` $35.23 -> $10.23, `budget.level` GREEN. Backup `D:\zo\runs\ops_audit_state.dupfix.bak.20260806.json`. Spend $0, reversible, no deploy, no schema change.
- verify_seen_red: NEVER
- log: **2026-08-06 MERGED AND ARMED BY COUNT, NOT COLOUR.** PR #2926 squashed to `eb910b33`. The evaluator's `pytest` check went **493 passed on main -> 522 passed on this PR: exactly +29**, which is the whole of `tests/test_ops_audit_state.py` (25 tests CI had never once collected, plus the 4 written today). A green would have proved nothing; the DELTA is what proves the allowlist line armed. R2 arming proof by blob identity, not a merge receipt: `git rev-parse origin/main:tools/ops_audit_state.py` == the worktree blob `2c679a2ba89e865541f0841378d32dce59c70296`, and `def _cid` plus the allowlist line are both present in `origin/main`. This lane's worktree is re-pointed at merged main by `lane_worktree.py --ensure` every run, so the fix is armed tomorrow rather than never.
- verify: `cd D:\zo\_lanes\ops-audit && python -m pytest tests/test_ops_audit_state.py -q` must collect 29 and pass 29; AND `python tools/ops_audit_state.py show` must report `credits: 1` with `since_funding.credits_ever == 25.0`. A second credit row for invoice 3148330, in any type, fails this. status: resolved

### FU-272 | The builder emits one service as two PRs into two incompatible lifecycle directories

- date: 2026-08-06
- source: daily-chairman-review
- status: open
- priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: For a single service the builder opens TWO PRs whose halves target different lifecycle
  directories: `router.py` into `services/active/<name>/` (the promotion DESTINATION) and
  `service.toml` into `services/staged/<name>/` (the SOURCE). Neither half alone is a complete
  service. The router-in-active half creates an active registry entry with no toml, whose
  import_path then defaults to the bare directory name and resolves to nothing. Confirmed on
  PR #2920 (`gate_health_probe`), which is TITLED `scaffold_..._service_toml` and whose only
  changed file is `router.py`. Two independent gates catch it and BOTH ARE CORRECT:
  `capmap-check` -> `STRICT: 1 UNLISTED broken active service(s): gate_health_probe=NO_TOML`;

So an in-memory call PERSISTED its partial state over `D:\zo\runs\ops_audit_state.json`: 11 balance samples and `schema: 2` deleted, the real 2026-07-17 top-up re-dated to 2026-08-06, and a FABRICATED invoice `9999999` for $25 appended. `ops_audit_state.py show` then read `entries: 0`, `credits_ever: 0.0`, `budget.level: UNKNOWN` -- the budget instrument [[FU-267]] had repaired four hours earlier was blinded again, by the supported API, from a different lane.

That one came from a hand-rolled writer; this one came through the HELPER. Two lanes, nine days apart, same file, same outcome. The no-write idiom `path=False` already exists and a prior session's probe used it -- so the guard is present, undocumented in the signature (`path: Optional[Path]` typed, `False` accepted), and **opt-in**. A safe mode nobody can discover from the signature is not a safe mode. Every one of the 17 real call sites passes `path=` explicitly, so defaulting `state=`-without-`path=` to NO WRITE changes zero existing behaviour: `git grep record_credit` -> 16 test calls (all `path=statefile`) + 1 CLI call (`path=path`).
- detail: **REPAIR AND ITS INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION.** Clobbered file preserved at `D:\zo\runs\ops_audit_state.clobbered-by-shepherd-20260806.json` (forensics before restore). The restore is NOT self-attested: `ops_audit_state.py show` now reports `since_funding.spend_usd` **$10.2348** and `credits_ever` **$25.00**, and the vast invoices API (`vast_spend.py --summary`, invoice 3148330) independently reports `spent` **$10.23** / `credit` **$14.77**. Two unrelated sources -- a local balance-sample history and a billing API -- agreeing to the cent. `budget.level` GREEN, `entries` 11, `credits` 1.

(2) It then counted history under `balances`/`balance_samples` when the real key is `entries`, read 0, and was one line away from publishing UNPROVEN for an intact file: R6, a guessed key returns ZERO, not None. ### FU-269 | The dashboard's headline card calls 474,689 servers "scored" when 191,273 of them carry risk_tier='unassessed'
- opened: 2026-08-06
- lane: clerk-signup-reconcile-nightly
- priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: **MEASURED ON THE LIVE PRODUCT SURFACE, THEN AGAINST THE TABLE IT SUMMARISES (R1).** `https://mcprisky.io/app`, logged in as admin 2026-08-06, Overview cards read `474,689 servers scored` and `474,689 in registry` -- **the same number twice** -- beside a risk distribution of CRITICAL 16,053 (3%), HIGH 81,406 (17%), MEDIUM 180,265 (38%), LOW 5,692 (1%). Those four sum to **283,416**, which is **59.7%** of the headline, and the four printed percentages sum to **59%**. A distribution that can only account for three fifths of its own total is the tell; the cards were then checked against `mcp_server_registry` rather than argued about.
- detail: **THE TABLE SETTLES IT AND THE MISSING ROWS ARE NOT MISSING, THEY ARE LABELLED.** `select risk_tier, count(*) from mcp_server_registry group by 1` returns **`unassessed` 191,273**, MEDIUM 180,265, HIGH 81,406, CRITICAL 16,053, LOW 5,692; total 474,689; `tier in (CRITICAL,HIGH,MEDIUM,LOW)` = **283,416**, matching the dashboard's own four buckets exactly. So the registry itself already carries a fifth tier that says, in plain language, that **40.3% of the corpus has never been assessed** -- and the card renders that population as `scored`. The number is not wrong; **the LABEL is**, which is the harder failure because nothing is inconsistent enough to break.
- detail: **CORROBORATED FROM AN INDEPENDENT TABLE.** `mcp_llm_axis_scores` holds 1,983,940 rows across **283,420 distinct server_ids** -- within 4 of the 283,416 four-bucket population, from a table the dashboard query does not touch. Two independent counts agreeing on ~283.4k while the card says 474.7k is what makes this a mislabel rather than a stale cache. **The 4-row gap is NOT explained and is not hand-waved**: 4 servers have axis scores but no CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW tier. Small, but it is exactly the shape that turns into a silent drop later, so it is recorded rather than rounded away.
- detail: **WHY THIS MATTERS MORE THAN A COSMETIC FIX.** `20k MCPs assessed with defensible signals` is the product goal, and the surface that reports progress against it currently reports the *registry size* under the word *scored*. A number that flatters by 191,273 on the first screen anyone sees is the same class as [[a_carried_forward_value_is_indistinguishable_from_a_measured_one]] and [[the_refresh_cycle_stretched_to_14_weeks_while_the_sla_stayed_green]]: the instrument is green because it is answering an easier question than the one it appears to answer. Two further readings from the same query, recorded because they bear on "defensible signals" and neither is visible on the dashboard: `verdict` is **`unknown` for 471,919 of 474,689 (99.4%)**, and `trust_score` is non-null for only **82,770 (17.4%)**.
- detail: **NOT PROPOSING A NEW GATE (R7).** The fix is a label and a denominator, not a check: the card should read `283,416 scored` (or `474,689 in registry / 283,416 assessed`), and the four percentages should be computed against the assessed population -- 5.7 / 28.7 / 63.6 / 2.0, which sums to 100 -- or else render `unassessed` as a fifth bar so the bars account for the whole. Showing the gap is strictly more informative than hiding it, and it is the number the 20k goal is actually measured against.
- verify: the `/api/dashboard/summary` payload must expose the assessed population separately from the registry total, and the value rendered under the word `scored` must equal `select count(*) from mcp_server_registry where upper(risk_tier::text) in ('CRITICAL','HIGH','MEDIUM','LOW')` at the same instant. Predicate is a comparison of two live numbers, so it can go red on its own.
- verify_seen_red: **YES, TODAY, AND IT IS THE MOTIVATING MEASUREMENT.** RED observed 2026-08-06: card said 474,689, the query said 283,416, delta 191,273. The GREEN limb is exercisable immediately by pointing the predicate at the four-bucket count, which returns the identical 283,416 the dashboard already draws -- so both limbs are reachable against the same live table in the same second.
- log:
  - 2026-08-06 clerk-signup-reconcile-nightly * Found while driving the app surface at the chairman's request during the FU-245 investigation, not by a scheduled check -- no instrument on this fleet was asking whether a card's label matches its query. Read-only; no app change made. Spend $0. status: open
- resolution:

- date: 2026-08-03 · source: follow-up-triage (exposed immediately by [[FU-241]]) · status: open · priority: P1

- detail: **THE MOMENT `zo_probe.py` WAS MADE TO SPEAK, ALL NINE OF ITS UNKNOWNS GAVE THE SAME ANSWER.** Re-ran every zo_probe-backed UNKNOWN predicate from this run's sweep and captured the cause: **`{"detail":"Catalog Error: Table with name score_runs does not exist!"}`** (FU-001, FU-104), **`...name servers does not exist!`** (FU-054), **`...name server_scores does not exist!`** (FU-058, FU-090, FU-093, FU-108), and `first column IS NULL` (FU-024, FU-107). Not a bus outage: `--sql "SELECT 1"` returned GREEN through the identical path minutes earlier, so the endpoint is up and answering. The tables the predicates name are simply not in the catalog it serves.
- detail: **"Catalog Error" IS A DUCKDB STRING, AND THE MOAT IS FLY MANAGED POSTGRES.** That is the discriminating detail and it is worth more than the table names. It suggests `:8772/query` is not backed by the datastore these nine predicates were written against, in which case they were never going to evaluate on any day, from any host, no matter how healthy everything was. **NOT CLAIMED:** that the engine is definitely DuckDB, or which store is "right". Establishing that is the first step of this entry, not its premise -- read `/home/workspace/shared/BRIDGES.md` and the `write_service` config before assuming any route (this SKILL's own instruction).
- detail: **THE COST IS NINE ENTRIES THAT LOOK POLICED AND ARE NOT.** `ledger_lint --stats` counts them under `runnable verify: 102`, and the E7 articulation-debt metric reads **0 OPEN P0/P1 unverifiable**. They pass the linter because they *have* a predicate. They have never once produced a verdict. **This is the ledger's dominant class turned on the ledger's own instrument: a check never observed RED is not evidence, and these have never been observed at all.** It is also why the fix must not be to drop their priority -- that is the FU-114 sin the SKILL names, and the chairman refused exactly that move for FU-031/FU-117 as D4 today.
- log:
  - 2026-08-07 follow-up-triage: STILL OPEN AND STILL UNMEASURED, four days on. This run's detached `fu_verify.py --once` (126 predicates, 42 UNKNOWN / 19 RED / 65 GREEN) reproduces it exactly: all nine listed FUs returned rc=2. Seven still carry the DuckDB string verbatim -- `Catalog Error: Table with name X does not exist!` for `score_runs` (FU-001, FU-093), `servers` (FU-054), `server_scores` (FU-090, FU-104, FU-108, FU-058). The other two have drifted to a DIFFERENT rc=2 shape and that drift is worth recording rather than smoothing over: FU-024 now reports `first column IS NULL -- an aggregate over no rows is not a zero (R6)`, and FU-107 no longer names a table at all. So the population is no longer homogeneous, and a single repoint may no longer fix all nine. NOT ACTED ON THIS RUN, and the reason is the entry's own resolution clause: it says diagnose the route first and do NOT bulk-edit nine predicates on an assumption. I confirmed only the negative half of that diagnosis -- `curl localhost:8772/sql` and `/query` on the ZoComputer runtime both return `{"detail":"Not Found"}`, so the path those predicates use is not even the path I probed, and I stopped rather than guess a route. Naming that honestly: I did not establish what backs `:8772`; BRIDGES.md is present at /home/workspace/shared/BRIDGES.md and unread by me. The entry stays P1 open with its verify unchanged. SHEPHERD NOTE: this is 7 of the 42 UNKNOWNs in one cause, the largest single cluster, and the other buckets from the same sweep are 3 timeouts at 60s, 3 predicates carrying UNSUBSTITUTED PLACEHOLDERS (`<staged-sha>`, `<tmp>`, `<any>` -- those have never run at all, which is worse than a wrong route), and 2 that invoke `select-string` as if it were a binary in cmd.exe.
- resolution: **DIAGNOSE THE ROUTE FIRST, THEN REPOINT OR REWRITE -- and do NOT bulk-edit nine predicates on an assumption.** (1) Establish what `:8772/query` is actually backed by, from `BRIDGES.md` and the running `write_service` config, resolved from the RUNTIME (R1) and not from a repo path. (2) If a Postgres route exists, repoint these nine at it. (3) If these tables genuinely no longer exist under any name -- a real possibility, since the moat schema has moved -- then the predicates are asserting about a world that ended and must be rewritten against what replaced them, which is a finding in its own right about the entries that own them. (4) Whatever the answer, `fu_verify.py` should surface a `catalog/route` UNKNOWN distinctly from a bus-down UNKNOWN, because the two need opposite responses. **Not attempted this run:** it needs the runtime's own config in hand and this run had already taken three implementations to done.
- verify: all nine predicates (FU-001, FU-024, FU-054, FU-058, FU-090, FU-093, FU-104, FU-107, FU-108) must return rc 0 or 1 from `fu_verify.py` -- **NOT rc=2**. The entry is not about which way they go: a RED here is a success for this FU, because it means the predicate finally evaluated. A GREEN that arrives by deleting or weakening a predicate does not count and must be refused.
- verify_seen_red: **YES -- the nine causes above ARE the RED, harvested 2026-08-03 and written to `_tools/_fu241_harvest.txt`.** The discriminating GREEN limb was exercised in the same session and through the same code path: `--sql "SELECT 1" --assert "int(v)==1"` -> rc=0. So the instrument that produced these nine failures has been seen succeeding against the same endpoint, which is what makes the failures a measurement rather than an outage.

<!-- FU-119 status=open priority=P1 filed=2026-07-27 last_touch=2026-08-10 stale_days=23 -->
### FU-119 | The goose-canary tests a TRANSPORT the mesh does not use — it drives goose straight at a provider, production drives it through the ladder shim
- date: 2026-07-27 · source: goose-shadow-research · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: The canary sets `OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://api.cerebras.ai/v1` and points goose **directly at the provider**. The mesh does not do that: the architect and builder point goose at the **ladder shim on `127.0.0.1:8796`**, which is our own OpenAI-compatible server. So the gate that decides whether a goose version may touch the runtime exercises a request path the runtime never takes. This is not theoretical — it produced both a false alarm and a blind spot in the same run: **(false alarm)** on the direct path, the second turn of any tool-calling conversation against a *reasoning* model dies with `400: messages.2.assistant.reasoning_content ... is unsupported`, because goose preserves `reasoning_content` across tool-call turns (upstream #10366, re-listed in v1.44) and the provider rejects the property on replay. Production is immune, because `ladder_shim.chat_completions()` reads only `role` and `content` off each message (lines 82-88), joins them into one prompt string, and `escalation.ask()` builds a **fresh `[system, user]` payload** upstream — `reasoning_content` never leaves the box. The string `reasoning_content` does not appear anywhere in the repo. **(blind spot)** the converse is the expensive half: because every request in the canary bypasses `ladder_shim.py` and `escalation.py`, **no canary step can detect a regression in the shim, the rung rotation, the key hydrator, or the tool-call salvage** — which is precisely where our own scars live ([[minimax_toolcall_ladder]], [[key_hydrator_timeout_rung_502]], [[goose_143_prose_salvage_shadow_watch]]). We built a version gate that cannot see the layer that breaks most.
- corollary worth its own thought: the shim **flattens** goose's multi-turn conversation into a single system+user text blob on every turn. Whatever else that is, it means the agent loop goose thinks it is running and the request the provider actually receives are structurally different objects. That is a plausible contributor to the convergence and tool-calls-as-prose classes and deserves a deliberate look rather than being discovered again sideways.
- proposed fix (filed, not fixed — CI would need provider keys as repo secrets to run the shim, which is the auth/secrets guardrail): add a **second canary tier** that boots `ladder_shim.py` on the runner and sets `OPENAI_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8796/v1`, so at least one tier exercises the production transport end to end. Keep the direct-provider tier as well — it isolates "is this goose version sane" from "is our shim sane", and conflating those is how the 2026-07-27 run nearly recorded a nonexistent 1.44 regression. Cheaper interim if secrets stay off-limits: a `--dry-run` shim mode with a stub upstream, which still exercises the shim's request parsing, tool-call assembly and SSE emitter without a single paid token.
- verify: `python -c "import io,sys,re; s=io.open('.github/workflows/goose-canary.yml',encoding='utf-8',errors='replace').read(); sys.exit(0 if re.search(r'OPENAI_BASE_URL=https?://127[.]0[.]0[.]1:8796',s) else 1)"`  # RED while the canary drives goose STRAIGHT AT A PROVIDER instead of the ladder shim the mesh actually uses
- verify_seen_red: 2026-08-02 — RED on first run: `.github/workflows/goose-canary.yml:54` sets `OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://api.cerebras.ai/v1`. The predicate is asserting the exact substitution this entry asks for, and it currently fails.
- log:
  - 2026-07-27 goose-shadow-research: partially mitigated in **PR #2058** — the namespacing step now runs on a NON-reasoning model (groq llama-3.3-70b) with the full reasoning explained inline, so scar #454 is testable again instead of the gate being permanently unable to reach a verdict. That restores the guard; it does **not** close this entry, because the transport divergence itself is untouched. Also worth recording as method: the thing that prevented a false "1.44 regression" finding was running a **1.43 negative control** (run 30262977744) against the identical workflow — it failed identically, which is what converted "1.44 broke it" into "our test harness never worked on either version". Run the control before believing a canary result.
  - 2026-08-03 goose-shadow-research: **THE SPEC THIS ENTRY'S GOOSE_WATCH ROW PROMISED DID NOT EXIST.** The row has read "Needs a second shim-backed tier -> FU-119, spec below" since 2026-07-27, and there was nothing below it -- the ledger made a claim about its own contents that a single read disproved. Written now, in GOOSE_WATCH.md under Queued directive specs (`build_ladder_shim_canary_tier`). The objection that parked it -- a GitHub runner cannot reach the tower's `:8796` -- dissolves on inspection: the tier should stand up `ladder_shim.py` FROM THE CHECKOUT inside the runner, which is strictly better, because it then gates the shim code the PR changes rather than whatever happens to be running on the tower. The spec carries a negative control as its ACCEPTANCE (a deliberately truncated tool name must turn the new assertion RED), because goose 1.45 `#10659` now truncates overlong function names in provider requests and our namespaced names are long -- which would reproduce the 1.38 starvation shape from a cause no current gate can see. Still `open`: a spec is not a tier.
  - 2026-08-10 goose-shadow-research: **BUILT, not filed -- PR #3167** (branch `goose/fu119-shim-canary-tier`). The spec written on 08-03 was never queued and never opened as a PR, which this task's SKILL requires in the same breath as writing it; it sat 7 days. **The objection that parked this entry for two weeks was answerable and wrong.** The entry records the blocker as "CI would need provider keys as repo secrets, which is the auth/secrets guardrail". It does not: the assertion is about the REQUEST goose sends, not the RESPONSE a provider returns, so the paid ladder is replaced by a recorder (`tools/canary/shim_tier_recorder.py`). Zero tokens, zero keys -- and critically **no permanent skip**, which is what a secret-gated tier would have been, and a skip that reads as a pass is this repo's most expensive class. The tier boots `ladder_shim.py` FROM THE CHECKOUT on 127.0.0.1:8796 (gating the shim code a PR changes, rather than whatever runs on the tower) and carries the assertion the direct tier structurally cannot make: that the 41-char `zo_directive_bridge__read_protected_files` reached the wire byte-identical, because 1.45 `#10659` truncates overlong function names and a silent truncation reproduces the 1.38 starvation shape from a cause no existing gate can see. **Four poles run locally before push** -- full name PASS, truncated-to-12 FAIL, zero-requests FAIL as TRANSPORT, no-tools FAIL as BRIDGE-ABSENT -- and the failure modes report distinctly rather than one bucket absorbing another. Negative control wired as a REQUIRED step: if truncation ever passes, the guard is vacuous and the build fails. **verify predicate widened** from the shell-assignment spelling (`OPENAI_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8796`) to also match the YAML `env:` key, which is the correct implementation shape -- recorded rather than done quietly; the substance asserted is unchanged, and it now reads GREEN having been seen RED on 08-02. **Two corrections against myself.** (a) The spec's `reads` array named `zo_sentinel/ladder_shim.py`; the file is at the repo ROOT. A spec whose anchors name a path that does not exist is the builder's own worst family (the `app.models` spelling class) committed by the lane that writes the specs -- anchors are now grepped against `git ls-files` before a spec is written. (b) This entry's detail says the shim "reads only role and content off each message", which is true and was nearly over-read into "goose's tool payload never reaches the ladder". It does: `ChatCompletionRequest.tools` is an explicit pass-through forwarded to `escalation.ask(tools=...)`, with a source comment saying so. Had I not checked, the tier's central assertion would have been measuring something that does not exist on the production path. **STATUS STAYS OPEN: the tier is committed but NOT YET OBSERVED GREEN.** In run 31384666172 all four of its steps read `skipped`, not `pass` (R3). Close this only against a run in which `shim_tier` reports `SHIM_TIER_VERDICT::overall::PASS`.
  - 2026-08-10 goose-shadow-research (same run, later): **THE TIER IS GREEN IN CI — the mesh has measured its own production transport for the first time.** Run `31385571295`, job `93445...` (`shim_tier`), all steps `success`: `SHIM_TIER_VERDICT::shim_reached::PASS::requests=1` · `tools_advertised::PASS::distinct=9` · `name_untruncated::PASS::expect=zo_directive_bridge__read_protected_files` · `overall::PASS`, and the **negative control step passed**, meaning truncation-to-12 did turn the assertion RED as required. `ladder_shim.py` booted from the checkout and answered `/health` with `{"ok":true,"version":"1.2","model":"zo-ladder-v1"}` — the real shim, and `escalation.py` imported for real, running in CI for the first time. **Substantive verdict, not just a green:** goose **1.45.0 does NOT truncate** our 41-char namespaced tool name on the wire, so `#10659` is measured-clear against our names rather than feared. That is a claim no previous canary could have made in either direction. **Two defects were found by RUNNING it, and both were repaired in-run rather than filed.** (1) Run `31384666172`: the tier's four steps read `skipped`, because it had been appended to the `canary` job and step 9 went red above it — a tier that can only execute once everything else is green cannot report on the condition it exists for. Split into its own job. (2) Run `31385033251`: the split then LOST `GOOSE_PROVIDER=openai`, which the `canary` job writes to `$GITHUB_ENV`; with `CONFIGURE=false` there is no config to fall back on, so goose exited in 0.03s having sent nothing. **The tier reported that correctly and specifically** — `shim_reached::FAIL::requests=0`, "treat this as a TRANSPORT failure, not a rename" — which is the bucketing this PR added, working on its own first real failure instead of collapsing into the nearest alarming diagnosis. Both fixed on the branch. **STATUS STILL OPEN, DELIBERATELY: PR #3167 IS NOT MERGED, AND THE HOLD IS ON EVIDENCE, NOT ON AUTHORITY** (`repo_prs` is delegated; merging a green PR of mine is part of the action). Two things are not yet observed: `pytest` on #3167 was still `pending` at end of run, and the `canary` job carrying the namespacing-classifier fix had **not reached a verdict** — I hold a local replay control for it but no CI observation. Merging on a partially-observed suite is the exact move this ledger keeps paying for. **Next run: check `pytest` on the COUNT and not the colour (a red-and-empty check dammed 87 PRs), re-run `git log <merge-base>..origin/main -- .github/workflows/goose-canary.yml tools/canary/` (empty at 11:5xZ, but the branch will be a day old), then merge and close this entry.**

log: 2026-07-27T11:45Z vast-jobs-daily-audit (**the find: the audit could not answer the questions it is required to answer**) — the task is told to report `instance_id, dph, uptime, status` for every live instance and to flag `loading` past 90 min as a wedge (chairman ruling 2026-07-17). `audit()` **cannot do either**. `RealVastClient.list_instances()` projected every instance down to `{id, state, dph}`, discarding `label`, `start_date` and `status_msg` at the client boundary — so uptime was unreachable downstream and the 90-minute wedge guard existed **only as prose in the SKILL file**, re-derived by hand every single run with a raw `console.vast.ai/api/v0/instances/` call for fields the harness had already fetched and thrown away. And an `orphan_instance` alert carried only `{instance_id, dph}`: today's legitimate rescore GPU and a genuine $0.32/hr leak produce **byte-identical** output. This is the [[glama_fabricated_tool_count]] shape — a projection that drops a field manufactures a confident wrong reading downstream. FIXED under act-authority ($0, reversible, no spend/auth/deploy/data surface): **PR #2059** — `list_instances()` carries `label`/`start_date`/`gpu`/`status_msg`; new `instance_facts()` (uptime, `started_at`, `cost_so_far_usd`, `wedged`) where a missing `start_date` yields `uptime_min: None` and **never 0**, because a 0 reads as "just started" and would silently suppress the very guard it arms; `WEDGE_LOADING_MIN = 90` encoded once in code; new **`wedged_instance`** alert class surfaced with a `destroy_cmd` but **never fired** (forensics-before-destroy unchanged, asserted by test — `audit()` still destroys nothing); `report["live"]` now lists EVERY live instance plus `burn_dph_total`, because a ledger-only view of paid compute is structurally blind while ScoreWave/moat-rescore write no ledger row. Exit semantics deliberately unchanged — an unledgered paid instance stays loud; this adds evidence, it does not relax a guard. Verified LIVE read-only from a clean worktree at `origin/main`: one call now returns label / started_at / uptime_min 41.7 / cost_so_far_usd 0.223 / wedged false / destroy_cmd. status: resolved · evidence: PR #2059, `zo_sentinel/vast_jobs.py`, `tests/test_vast_jobs.py` (+6 tests).

log: 2026-07-27T11:45Z vast-jobs-daily-audit (**the MTD spend number was never real, in two independent ways**) — `D:\zo\runs\ops_audit_state.json` was a single `{"date","balance"}` object **overwritten every run**, so "month-to-date = balance delta since the first entry of this month" could only ever see YESTERDAY, and the audit reported a 24-hour delta labelled MTD against a $25/month budget with nothing in the output to reveal the basis. Second, independent bug: the delta is **wrong across a top-up** — credit raises the balance, so naive `first - current` goes NEGATIVE and a month in which $25 was burned reads as money earned. Correct: `spend = (first_balance + credits_in_window) - current_balance`, which requires recording credit EVENTS, not just balances. Measured today: month-delta says **$1.68** on a 1-day basis; burn since the 2026-07-17 funding is **$7.90** — the old math would have understated the burn **4.7x** against the very budget it exists to guard, and would have kept understating it as the month went on. FIXED under act-authority: **PR #2059** adds `tools/ops_audit_state.py` — schema v2 with `entries[]` + `credits[]`, v1 migrated on load rather than discarded, a corrupt file healed rather than fatal, same-date record **upserts** (idempotent re-run), `month_to_date()` publishes `basis_days` + `complete_month` so a thin history cannot masquerade as a full month, and `since_funding()` gives the number that needs no history at all (`credits_ever - balance`, exact for a prepaid account). - date: 2026-08-04 · source: autopoiesis-bar-tracker 16:30-17:15Z, chairman instruction · status: open · priority: P1

- detail: **MEASURED, NOT ASSERTED: 11 MECHANICAL roadblocks vs 1 PERMISSION roadblock in the session whose entire headline work was aimed at the permission gate.** Basis: `friction_ledger.jsonl`, 14 rows seeded from this session's own stalls, read by `loop_health.py`: **mechanical 11 events / 82 min, operator 2 / 10 min, permission 1 / 0 min**. The permission gate was the NAMED blocker so it got the day; the thing actually stopping work was brittle invocation. **The loop optimised what it could see, and it could only see its product.**
- detail: **EVERY EXISTING INSTRUMENT MEASURES THE PRODUCT: services promoted, contract failures, orphan census, freshness, casing, redirects. NOTHING MEASURED THE LOOP.** For a system whose stated product is ITSELF ([[AUTOPOIESIS.md]]), *how often did we get blocked, by what class, for how long* is the number that should be falling, and on 2026-08-04 nobody computed it. `loop_health.py` now does, in three readings: stalls-by-class with a RECURRING flag, peer-review adjudication latency, and a claim lint. It exits rc=1 on degradation and says so in the words that matter: **these are LOOP defects, not product defects -- the product can be green while the loop rots.**
- detail: **A SCAR THAT DESCRIBES A HAZARD DOES NOT PREVENT IT, AND THIS IS THE SESSION'S SHARPEST LESSON ABOUT MEMORY.** The `$`-eating PowerShell hazard was in MCP memory BEFORE this session opened. **It was hit THREE TIMES ANYWAY** -- `loop_health` flags it as `RECURRING (>=3x) -- NOT being fixed, only survived`. Memory is read when a session OPENS; the hazard fires at the moment of INVOCATION, twenty minutes later, inside a different train of thought. The fix is not a better scar. **`_tools/friction.py` makes the wrong call unconstructable**: `ps()` never uses `-Command`, `clone()` always passes `--no-hardlinks`, `detached()` for anything over 3 min, and `run()` REFUSES a command carrying a known hazard rather than warning about it. 8 hazards, each DATED and citing the incident that produced it -- a speculative guard is how you get a rule nobody trusts. 9/9 controls: every hazard must fire on its own motivating incident AND stay silent on the fix, `run()` must refuse a bad command AND still execute a safe one, and `ps()` is asserted to preserve a literal `$`.
- detail: **`rule_echo.py` GENERALISES THE ONLY LOOP THAT WORKED UNPROMPTED ALL DAY, AND IT EARNED ITSELF ON ITS FIRST LIVE RUN.** 90 minutes after the peer-review amendment, `authority.py --show` reported my own prompt still restating the rule it had just retired -- the single instrument in the fleet that answers *"I changed a rule, who still repeats the old one?"*. Generalised across 128 surfaces (docs + 13 lane prompts + tools) it immediately found **`stage, never fire` STILL LIVE IN `prod-drift-sentinel` -- a prohibition retired 2026-07-29, live for SIX DAYS, in the one lane that fires prod deploys.** That is [[FU-229]]'s exact class; that lane's own description even warns about residual Phase-1 labels, and nothing was checking. Fixed via `task_edit.py`; check now clean.
- detail: **TWO SECOND-ORDER DEFECTS IN THE FIX ITSELF, BOTH INSTANCES OF THE CLASS BEING FIXED.** (a) **My replacement text QUOTED the phrase it was retiring**, so the record of the retirement kept tripping the detector -- x3 became x1 and would never reach zero. **A surface that DOCUMENTS a dead rule is indistinguishable, to a substring match, from one that still OBEYS it.** Resolved two ways: the replacement now states what is true and cites the supersession by reference, and the matcher ignores an occurrence within 220 chars of a supersession marker. The load-bearing control asserts the matcher SEPARATES the two -- a citation must NOT fire while a bare restatement of the identical substring MUST -- because an exemption never seen to discriminate is just a silencer. `--blast` deliberately keeps RAW counts, since before retiring a rule you want every mention including the future citations. (b) The claim lint's first pattern matched a bare `cannot be` and flagged *"an apex that cannot be reached"* -- a fact about the chairman's diary, not a claim about the system. **A lint that cries on ordinary prose gets muted, and a muted lint is worse than none.** Narrowed to claims that a property of the SYSTEM is beyond testing.
- detail: **CLAIM LINT EXISTS BECAUSE OF THIS LANE'S OWN PROSE.** On 2026-08-04 *"chaos testing can't catch a lane that rubber-stamps"* was published beside measured results, in the same confident register, was never tested, and fell to a single question from the chairman. **An unfalsifiable claim about a system is indistinguishable from a defect in it.** R4 -- an assertion never seen RED is not evidence -- applies to a sentence in a report exactly as it applies to a gate in CI. The lint scans recent ledger entries for absolutes with no predicate within 260 chars and demands a measurement or an explicit `UNPROVEN` tag.
- detail: **WIRED, NOT MERELY BUILT.** All 13 lanes carry **SELF-STEERING v4**, superseding v1-v3 rather than stacking a fourth contradictory block (0 lanes retain a superseded block); re-running wires 0. Every lane now runs four commands first: `peer_review --sweep`, `rule_echo --check`, `loop_health`, `friction --list`. All five tools green together: friction 9/9, rule_echo 4/4, loop_health 3/3, task_edit 5/5, peer_review 20/20; chaos 12 scenarios, 0 unattended-fatal.

- verify_seen_red: every reading was observed BOTH ways. `rule_echo` found a real 6-day-old live echo and went clean after the fix; its matcher was proven to separate a citation from a restatement of the identical substring. `loop_health` reports UNKNOWN rather than zero on an empty friction ledger (R6) and refuses to imply health from zero adjudications. `friction` was seen refusing a hazardous command AND running a safe one.
- links: [[FU-247]] (peer review, whose adjudication latency this measures), [[FU-229]] (the stale-permission class rule_echo detects), [[FU-207]] (the usage-limit stall that motivates the starvation door), [[FU-233]] (an alarm with no subscriber)
- log:
  - 2026-08-04T17:07-18:50Z follow-up-triage: **THIS ENTRY'S PREDICATE WENT RED TODAY EXACTLY AS IT SAID IT WOULD, AND THE HAZARD BIT ME TWICE MORE INSIDE THE SAME RUN -- which is the strongest available evidence that a documented hazard is not a prevented one.** `loop_health.py` rc=1 on `RECURRING mechanical/ps-command-dollar x3`, as this entry predicted. **FOUR STALLS RECORDED TO `friction_ledger.jsonl` THIS RUN (~99 min), and the expensive one is a NEW hazard, not the known one:** I hand-rolled `Start-Process -ArgumentList` with a `-File` path containing spaces (`D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\...`). PowerShell split the path on the space, **the detached job never launched -- twice -- and the STALE `sweep.json` from 2026-08-03 was still sitting on disk with entirely plausible numbers (98 probed / 49 GREEN / 15 RED / 34 UNKNOWN).** I polled a job that did not exist for ~55 minutes and came within one step of publishing **the previous run's artifact as this run's result**. Caught by checking `LastWriteTime` and the `ran` field, not by anything complaining. **That is R1 -- the artifact you inspected is not the artifact that ran -- landing on the run whose job is to police it, and the near-miss shape is worse than a crash because a stale artifact FAILS SILENTLY AND PLAUSIBLY.** The real sweep, once actually launched: **107 probed / 54 GREEN / 15 RED / 38 UNKNOWN**, 1 CLOSE (FU-167), 0 reopens -- close enough to the stale figures that the substitution would not have looked wrong to a reader. **`friction.detached()` exists precisely to make that call unconstructable and I did not reach for it**, which is this entry's own thesis demonstrated at my expense: memory is read when a session opens, the hazard fires ninety minutes later inside a different train of thought. Also recorded a 4th dated instance of `ps-command-dollar` on a SIBLING lane (`moat-rescore-weekly`, 2026-08-04: `$0.00` written to the ledger as `\.00`), attributed to that lane -- `loop_health` cannot tell no-friction from nobody-wrote-it-down, so a sibling's unrecorded stall reads as fleet health it did not earn.

BUILT _tools/_lh_coverage_patch.py -- a read-side RECORDER COVERAGE block whose population comes from lane_receipts.json and NOT from the friction ledger, because deriving the population from the ledger makes every silent lane invisible by construction (the defect lane_start.py already records having shipped once). Deliberately PRINT-ONLY -- rc untouched, so no lane's check-in changes exit code, and --verify fails the change if the wiring is ever folded into `bad`. NOT SELF-CLEARED: filed as peer decision `lh-silent-lane-is-not-a-clean-lane` (clause redefining_the_metric), adversary slot open. SCOPE (R4): this BOUNDS the unknown, it does not remove it -- a silent lane remains clean-OR-not-recording and the output refuses to collapse the two; asserting a genuinely clean run is still UNPROVEN and deliberately unbuilt. ### FU-326 | The fix for the dam enumerated failure NAMES, and the next wave returned a name it did not list
- date: 2026-08-11 · source: moat-rescore-weekly · status: pr-open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: [[FU-321]] shipped `_terminally_finished` in PR #3209 (merged 08:11:02Z, `ad974195`) recognising `killed_*`/`abort*`/`abandon*`/`cancel*` and `fail`. **Ninety minutes later** wave `20260811-063956` hit its scaled cost ceiling and returned **`result: "cost_breach"`** -- destroyed, no predictions, unresumable -- which matched none of them, so MERGED MAIN was dammed again by the identical mechanism the merge had just fixed. `deadline` would have been the third. The verdict is not obscure: `weekly_rescore.py` already lists it THREE LINES AWAY in `if run.state.get("result") in ("ok", "fail", "cost_breach", "deadline")`, and `run.mark("watch", "failed", result="cost_breach")` is what writes it. So the fix was authored beside the enumeration that would have completed it. **The lesson is not "add cost_breach": it is that enumerating the failures already observed is the same defect in a new costume**, and this ledger now has the same class twice in one morning, the second instance introduced by the cure for the first.
- verify: `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\_moat_patch_test.py"` -- rc must be 0. Eleven assertions, THREE controls: (7c) **MERGED main STILL resumes the `cost_breach` run** -- the assertion that grades the deployed artifact and is red by design until #3210 lands; (7b) the pinned pre-merge copy resumes it; (1) the pinned pre-merge copy resumes the `fail` run; (8) SPEND GUARD -- a terminal verdict still HOLDING an instance is NOT skipped, so unreleased spend cannot hide behind this; (9) `ok` is never terminal; (4) a run with no verdict is still resumed.
- verify_seen_red: 2026-08-11T08:56:45Z -- assertion 7c returned `selected='20260811-063956'` against `git show origin/main:...`, i.e. the code merged 90 minutes earlier demonstrably resumes the dead run. Assertions 1 and 7b ran against `ad974195^1`. **The control was itself broken mid-session and that is the second lesson**: it originally read `origin/main`, and when #3209 merged, the "known-broken predecessor" silently BECAME the fix -- assertion 1 flipped red at 08:55Z for a reason that had nothing to do with the code under test. A control whose subject is a moving ref stops being a control the moment its subject changes. Now pinned to a COMMIT.
- resolution: PR **#3210** (`fix/rescore-terminal-verdict-not-a-word-list`), cut from a FRESH worktree at CURRENT origin/main rather than stacked on the merged branch -- a stale base is how a follow-up quietly reverts its predecessor. Replaces the word list with a rule: **a recorded non-`ok` verdict plus a destroyed instance is terminal**, because every phase after `fire` needs an instance. `destroyed` is load-bearing and `ok` stays explicitly non-terminal.
- log: 2026-08-11 · moat-rescore-weekly · The live dam was cleared the same way as its predecessor: run `20260811-063956` moved to `D:\zo\runs\weekly_rescore_aborted\`, `manual_closed_cost_breach_no_forensics` appended to the ledger (CRLF class measured in binary and preserved), `--check-open-runs` rc=0 afterwards (21 aborted ignored, no stranded), and **0 live instances confirmed against the live Vast API**. Nothing is burning and the pipeline is not blocked.

<!-- FU-239 status=open priority=P1 filed=2026-08-03 last_touch=2026-08-30 stale_days=3 -->
### FU-239 | The nightly Clerk reconcile has no host it can run on -- `tools/` is not COPYed into the image and the Clerk key is not on the tower, so the away-window negative control cannot execute anywhere
- date: 2026-08-03 · source: clerk-signup-reconcile-nightly (clerk-sync lane), first actual attempt to run it · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: **BOTH CANDIDATE HOSTS MEASURED, BOTH REFUSE, AND THE TWO REASONS ARE INDEPENDENT.** (1) IN-APP: `ls -la /app/tools/clerk_reconcile.py` inside the machine returns No such file; the image root is `/srv`, and `ls /srv` lists 40-plus entries while **`ls /srv/tools` returns No such file or directory**. Same command shape, different answers, so this is a measurement and not a broken probe (R4). The Dockerfile confirms it from the source side: 11 COPY lines -- `app`, `migrations`, `alembic.ini`, `zo_sentinel`, `services/active`, and three explicit root-file lists -- and **no token naming `tools` at any depth**. (2) TOWER-SIDE: `CLERK_SECRET_KEY` is unset in the tower environment and `fetch_secret.py clerk` returns **`AGENTVAULT_MISS service=clerk`**. Discriminating control run in the same minute: `fetch_secret.py github` RESOLVED a 40-character value, so the vault works and the service genuinely is not in it.
- detail: **THE SHAPE IS THE FINDING: A MERGED CORRECTNESS FIX IS NOT A RUNNING ONE.** `tools/clerk_reconcile.py` landed in PR #2700 (main `18ac5cf2`) on 2026-08-02 and is careful work -- it carries its own self-test, a Cloudflare User-Agent fix from PR #2705, and a canonical-host correction from PR #2707. Three PRs of maintenance on a tool that has never executed once. The scheduled task that invokes it was written against a path that exists only in the repo.
- detail: **THE COST IS SPECIFICALLY THE AWAY WINDOW, WHICH IS THE ONLY REASON THIS TOOL WAS BUILT.** Its own docstring names the threat: over a 23-day unattended window we would return to a healthy-looking users table and a webhook dead since the second week. The away window opens **2026-08-07**, four days after filing. As scheduled, this task would have produced 23 consecutive nights of neither a green nor a red -- and per its own instructions "silence is a valid report", so 23 nights of silence would have read as 23 nights of health. The negative control designed to make an invisible outage visible was itself invisible.
- detail: **WHAT THIS RUN COULD STILL ESTABLISH, so the night is not recorded as wasted.** Read-only probes reached prod by other means and the picture is unambiguous: prod serves v66 `d5cb1d0f`, which `git merge-base --is-ancestor 18ac5cf2` proves is an ANCESTOR of the webhook commit; `POST https://mcprisky.io/webhooks/clerk` returns **404** with `/` returning 200 as control; and the prod `users` table has columns `created_at, email, id, org_id, password_hash, role` -- **no `clerk_id`, no `clerk_synced_via`** -- with `count(*) = 1`. So migration 0011 has not landed, consistent with [[FU-235]], and the reconcile could not have upserted even if it had run.
- resolution: **PICK A HOST, THEN WIRE IT -- and the two options are not equivalent.** (A) IN-APP, PREFERRED: add a `COPY tools /srv/tools` line and invoke via `flyctl ssh console`; the secrets are already there, nothing new is provisioned, and the job runs where the data is. Note this widens the image surface, which `fire_gate` grades -- it is an image-reaching path and would correctly force a re-stage. (B) TOWER-SIDE: add `clerk` to AgentVault and run from the lane tree; no image change, but it puts a prod Clerk key and a prod DSN on the tower, which is a new standing credential and therefore escalate-only. Recommend (A). Whichever is chosen, the wiring must be proven by an execution, not by a merge.
- verify: the job must produce a run artifact with a **non-2 exit code** on a night when prod is reachable -- `python tools/clerk_reconcile.py --json` must exit 0 or 1 from whichever host is chosen, and the JSON must carry a `clerk_users_seen` value. rc=2 does not satisfy this and neither does a green log line: the whole entry is about the difference between a tool that is installed and a tool that has answered.
- verify_seen_red: **YES, TODAY, AND IT IS WHY THE ENTRY EXISTS.** The 2026-08-03 attempt is the RED: no runnable host on either side, each proven by its own discriminating control. The GREEN limb has never been observed, which is stated plainly rather than assumed -- that is the entire content of this finding, and the first non-2 exit code will be its first proof.
- links: [[FU-235]] (why the reconcile would have had nothing to reconcile), [[FU-238]] (the second blocker this job would have surfaced), [[FU-158]] (the other case of the running copy diverging from the repo copy)
- log:

The task's own contract says an rc=2 that is unset because secrets were never provisioned is a one-line report and a stop, explicitly not a defect against the code -- and the code is not at fault here. What is filed instead is the defect the contract did not anticipate: the tool is correct and has nowhere to run. The task file also forbids deploys from this lane, so option (A) was NOT taken tonight; copying the script into the running machine was considered and declined because `authority.py --may` returned UNKNOWN ACTION and the away window has not opened, so an unknown action escalates rather than converting to DECIDE_AND_LOG.
  - 2026-08-03 * follow-up-triage * **OPTION (A) MERGED -- PR #2754, squash `f0146fd2`, `COPY tools /srv/tools`. STATUS STAYS `open` BECAUSE THIS ENTRY'S PREDICATE IS AN EXECUTION AND A MERGE IS NOT ONE (R2).** The chairman decided the fork as D3 in the 2026-08-03 decision record and opened the PR; it was left OPEN when that run closed at 15:55Z. Merged here at 17:11:22Z after two checks. (1) All 16 real checks PASS (pytest 3m2s x2, bandit, CodeQL, db-integration, capmap-check, no-hollow, smoke-ladder, schema-prm, static-analysis, frontend, triage, 3 CodeQL analyses; 3 jobs `skipping`, which is `skipped != pass` and is why they are named rather than folded into a count -- R3). (2) **[[FU-240]]'s discriminator, applied to the very next PR after the entry that taught it:** `git log <merge-base>..origin/main -- Dockerfile` returned EMPTY, so this PR's green graded a tree still current for the file it touches. #2058 sat open seven days and its green went stale against a fix it would have reverted; that check is now two commands and it was run. Diff read before merging: one `COPY` line plus a comment, `+9/-0`, single file. The chairman's own pre-merge scan of what enters the image (121 files / 1.02 MB, no `.env`/`.key`/`.pem`, the one `whsec_` hit being Svix's published test vector inside a `__main__` self-test) is not re-performed here but is recorded as the basis. **IMAGE-LEVEL ARMING VERIFIED FROM `origin/main`, NOT FROM THE MERGE RESULT:** `git show origin/main:Dockerfile` contains exactly one `COPY tools /srv/tools`. **RUNTIME ARMING IS NOT DONE AND IS NOT MINE.** The predicate demands `python tools/clerk_reconcile.py --json` exit 0 or 1 with a `clerk_users_seen` value from the chosen host -- that needs the image to be BUILT and DEPLOYED. Prod deploy belongs to `prod-drift-sentinel` under FIRE_ON_GREEN and the chairman named that lane explicitly ("the deploy is deliberately not done here"); firing it from this lane would be a cross-lane seizure of another lane's grant, not an exercise of my own. **Two things the owning lane must not be surprised by.** First, `fire_gate` will correctly force a RE-STAGE: `COPY tools` widens the image surface, so candidate `de79e43a` is now stale by construction -- intended, not a fault. Second, and this is the sequencing trap [[FU-238]] was filed to prevent: when the deploy lands, `/webhooks/clerk` stops answering **404** and starts answering **503**, because `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SECRET` is still `Staged` and `app/clerk_webhook.py:191` fails closed. **A 503 at that moment is NOT evidence that [[FU-235]] was mis-diagnosed.** Recorded here because this is the entry whose resolution triggers that transition.

THIS ENTRY'S PREDICATE IS SATISFIED BY AN EXECUTION, WHICH IS THE ONE THING IT REFUSED TO ACCEPT A MERGE FOR (R2)** -- status line left alone, follow-up-triage owns it. Predicate as written: a non-2 exit code on a night prod is reachable, with JSON carrying a `clerk_users_seen` value. Delivered: `python tools/clerk_reconcile.py --json` inside machine `7813ee2a33e978` (image `mcplookup:deployment-01KZ52HZQGDFQ78G2MTBJYF6WC` = v69), **rc=1**, `clerk_users_seen: 3`. Option (A) is the host that worked, exactly as recommended -- `/srv/tools/clerk_reconcile.py` exists in the image now, and the runtime arming this entry insisted on was the v69 deploy on 2026-08-04T00:25:30Z, three PRs and two days after `COPY tools /srv/tools` merged as `f0146fd2`. Rather than assert the two copies agree, both were hashed -- lane `D:\zo\_lanes\clerk-sync` at `origin/main` 80fa29cb (clean worktree) gives `sha256 = 8dc1f16b28fa6eaa9f6327f4a5db3d7694f7e366b17f1bfb01c36d3284ae84b0`, and `sha256sum` inside the machine returns the identical digest. Byte-identical, so the artifact inspected IS the artifact that ran, measured rather than hoped (R1). **WHAT THE FIRST ANSWER SAYS, so this closure is not misread as good news:** rc=1 RED, 3 of 3 Clerk users had to be CREATED by the backfill and the oldest is 898.87h stale -- the webhook has never delivered a single signup. Filed as [[FU-245]], with the sting that tonight's backfill consumes the very control that detected it: tomorrow the same job exits 0 GREEN on the same dead webhook. ### FU-245 | The Clerk webhook has delivered 0 of 3 signups since 2026-06-27, and tonight's backfill is what makes tomorrow's run GREEN without anything being fixed

- date: 2026-08-04 · source: clerk-signup-reconcile-nightly (clerk-sync lane), FIRST EVER EXECUTION · status: open · priority: P1 · class: defect
- class: defect
- detail: **rc=1 RED, AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME THIS JOB HAS EVER ANSWERED.** Basis, published in full because the number is worthless without it (R5): `python tools/clerk_reconcile.py --json` run 2026-08-04 ~08:45Z from **inside** running machine `7813ee2a33e978` (the only `started` machine of two; the other, `683556ea47ee38`, is `stopped`), image `mcplookup:deployment-01KZ52HZQGDFQ78G2MTBJYF6WC` = v69. Output: `clerk_users_seen: 3`, `created: 3`, `updated: 0`, `duplicate: 0`, `skipped: 0`, `stale_after_hours: 2.0`, and **three webhook_misses aged 383.52h, 851.83h and 898.87h** (`user_3GiRDdWueNnAwGKte5ALGuL5fWk` 2026-07-19T09:11:24Z, `user_3FpK2d4BteD1bQ0ACacIhZ4ABDw` 2026-06-29T20:52:44Z, `user_3Fjmq7LvAdwDgfCngy5ksahoeIw` 2026-06-27T21:50:40Z). This satisfies [[FU-239]]'s predicate exactly -- a non-2 exit code carrying a `clerk_users_seen` value -- and it is an execution, not a merge (R2).
- detail: **THE MISS RATE IS 3 OF 3, WHICH IS A DIFFERENT CLAIM FROM "THE WEBHOOK DIED".** Every Clerk user in existence had to be created by the backfill; not one had been delivered live. The oldest miss predates the 2026-06-28 prod launch by a few hours. Corroborated from the other side by the column the project instructions forbid overwriting precisely so it can answer this question: `select clerk_synced_via, count(*) from users group by 1` returns **`(NULL, 1), ('reconcile', 3)`** -- the NULL being the pre-Clerk seed row -- and **`'webhook'` appears zero times.** So the correct reading is not "the live path stopped", it is "the live path has never once completed". A regression hypothesis would send someone looking for a change that broke it; there is no such change to find.
- detail: **THE APP SIDE IS NOW HEALTHY, AND THAT IS WHY THIS ENTRY POINTS UPSTREAM RATHER THAN AT OUR CODE.** `POST https://mcprisky.io/webhooks/clerk` with an unsigned `{}` body returns **401 `{"detail":"invalid signature"}`** (measured 08:44Z). Route mounted at `APIRouter(prefix="/webhooks")` + `@router.post("/clerk")`, `app.include_router(clerk_webhook.router)` at `app/main.py:60`. 401 is the discriminating answer: 404 would mean the route never shipped, 503 would mean [[FU-238]]'s staged secret was still unreleased, and 200 would mean the verifier was open. It is none of those -- the endpoint exists, is reachable on the canonical host, and is verifying signatures. **[[FU-238]] IS GREEN BY ITS OWN PREDICATE** (see the log bullet filed there): both limbs, with a negative control in the same call.
- detail: **A 301 ON THE ALIAS DOMAINS IS THE LEADING CANDIDATE AND IT IS TESTABLE WITHOUT THE CLERK CONSOLE.** Probed 08:46Z: `mcplookup.app` -> **301** `Location: https://mcprisky.io/webhooks/clerk`; `www.mcplookup.app` -> **301** same; `mcplookup.fly.dev` -> 401; `www.mcprisky.io` -> 401. Svix, which is what Clerk sends with, treats a 3xx as a FAILED delivery rather than following it for a POST -- and even a follower would arrive as GET and take a 405. So an endpoint registered against `mcplookup.app` (the name in every doc, the name of the Fly app, and the name a human would type) fails 100% of deliveries while the service behind it is perfectly healthy. That is the exact shape of [[the_fly_app_name_is_not_the_canonical_host]] and it predicts precisely the 3-of-3 observed. It is a CANDIDATE, not a finding: the competing hypothesis -- endpoint registered correctly but signed with a secret that does not match the deployed `whsec_` (38 chars, prefix read in-machine; the value itself was never printed) -- predicts the same 3-of-3 and is not distinguishable from this side.
- detail: **THE LOGS CANNOT ADJUDICATE, AND THEIR SILENCE IS NOT A ZERO (R6).** `flyctl logs -a mcplookup --no-tail` returns a 100-line buffer that is entirely `New SSH session` lines. **My own 401 probe from four minutes earlier does not appear in it** -- so the app emits no request-level access logs to Fly and the buffer cannot show a failed Clerk delivery either. Absence of delivery attempts in the log is therefore UNKNOWN, not evidence of no attempts, and must not be used to prefer the 301 hypothesis over the secret-mismatch one. This control is recorded because reading "no webhook lines in the log" as "Clerk never called us" was available, cheap, and wrong.
- detail: **THE THING THAT MAKES THIS URGENT RATHER THAN MERELY OPEN: TONIGHT'S RUN DISARMED THE DETECTOR THAT FOUND IT.** The control is `created` + `signed_up < stale_cut`, and it is ONE-SHOT PER USER. Those three rows now exist, so tomorrow the same job reports `updated: 3, created: 0, webhook_misses: []` and **exits 0 GREEN with the webhook still 100% dead**. It can only go RED again if a brand-new human signs up during a window when nobody is watching -- and the away window opens 2026-08-07. A green from this job tomorrow means "no NEW signup was missed", which on a corpus of 3 users in 5 weeks is almost entirely a statement about signup volume, not about webhook health. **Do not read a green from this task between now and the fix as this entry closing.** The job is not wrong; the detector is simply consumed by its own success, which is why the closing predicate below is a positive `'webhook'` row and not another green from this job.
- resolution: **CHAIRMAN DECISION, AND IT IS ONE THING: READ THE CLERK DASHBOARD ENDPOINT.** Nothing on the tower or in prod can see Clerk's endpoint configuration -- the Backend API does not expose the Svix endpoint list, so this is not an agent-side task that was skipped. Needed from the console (Configure -> Webhooks): (1) does an endpoint exist at all, (2) its **exact URL** -- if it is `https://mcplookup.app/...` or any alias that 301s, retarget it to **`https://mcprisky.io/webhooks/clerk`**, (3) its **Signing Secret**, compared against the deployed `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (38 chars, `whsec_` prefix), and (4) its recent delivery attempts, which settle the 301-vs-mismatch fork in one glance -- attempts present and failing = secret or redirect at the transport; no attempts at all = the endpoint was never registered. Deliberately NOT done from this lane: it is a third-party console credential and a prod webhook reconfiguration, neither of which is in this task's grant (`authority.py --may clerk_reconcile` returns UNKNOWN ACTION, and the away window has not opened, so an unknown action escalates rather than converting to DECIDE_AND_LOG). No agent-side change is proposed, because the app side is already correct and adding anything to it would be treating a healthy component.
- verify: `select count(*) from users where clerk_synced_via = 'webhook'` executed against prod must return **>= 1**. Positive proof that one signup traversed the live path, which is the only thing that closes this. Explicitly NOT "the nightly job exits 0" -- per the one-shot detail above, that predicate is already satisfiable by a dead webhook and would close this entry on nothing.
- verify_seen_red: **YES, TODAY, AND IT IS THE MOTIVATING MEASUREMENT ITSELF.** RED observed 2026-08-04 in-machine: the group-by returned `(NULL,1), ('reconcile',3)` with `'webhook'` absent from the result set entirely. The GREEN limb is exercised by construction in the same query -- `'reconcile'` returned 3 through the identical code path in the same statement -- so the predicate has been seen distinguishing two values of the same column in one read, and is not a probe that can only ever report absence. Note the shape deliberately: `count(*)` over a GROUP BY, not `SELECT 1 ... WHERE`, per [[zero_rows_is_not_an_error]].
- next: after the endpoint is corrected, do NOT wait on organic signup traffic to prove it -- Clerk's dashboard can replay/resend a past delivery, and replaying one of the three named `clerk_id`s exercises the live path end to end and flips the predicate the same minute.

<!-- FU-254 status=open priority=P1 filed=2026-08-04 last_touch=2026-08-31 stale_days=2 -->
### FU-254 | Every knowledge surface we own is PULL and enumerated from the wrong end -- 15 of 88 tools are built, paid for and consulted by nothing
- UNPROVEN: *every* knowledge surface being PULL is a universal quantifier over a population (FOLLOWUPS.md, the Graphify KL, the memory connector) enumerated by hand on 2026-08-04 and never re-enumerated. WHAT WOULD SHOW IT WRONG: any surface that emits without being asked -- `lane_start`'s report-on-siblings step is a candidate that POSTDATES the enumeration. The 15-of-88 dark-tool measurement this entry turns on is independent of the quantifier and stays open. Tagged 2026-08-12 by follow-up-triage, beside the claim rather than in the log, because the first attempt put it 21 lines away and CLAIM LINT reads a window (the FU-255 shape, again).
- date: 2026-08-04 * source: autopoiesis-bar-tracker 17:45-18:20Z, from follow-up-triage's lane_halt finding * status: open * priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: **THE INCIDENT (follow-up-triage, about work from 07-30): `tools/lane_halt.py --enforce` was built, shipped and ARMED on 2026-07-30 and had ZERO callers five days later** -- and so did its only possible producer, `queue_census.py`. `--status` on the runtime: *no lane halts recorded*. The arming was a default on a switch nobody flips. **The sharp part is that the tool PREDICTED THIS IN WRITING AND WAS RIGHT**: its docstring carries a section headed *WHAT ARMING DOES NOT DO (read this before trusting it)* and the commit message repeats it. **The honesty was perfect and changed nothing, because a caveat in a docstring has no subscriber.**
- detail: **WHY NONE OF THE THREE KNOWLEDGE SURFACES CAUGHT IT, AND THEY ALL FAIL THE SAME WAY.** (1) **FOLLOWUPS.md holds what a lane WROTE DOWN** -- `lane_halt` was never written down, so it was absent from all 247 entries. A ledger records noticed things; it structurally cannot report an unnoticed one. (2) **Graphify KL anchors map FU -> code, and the daily reconcile walks FUs and checks their anchors still resolve. It is ONE-DIRECTIONAL.** Nothing walks CODE and asks which objects have no FU and no caller. (3) **The memory connector indexes `.md` and transcripts**; this caveat lived in a `.py` docstring and a squashed commit body, outside its index -- and memory is PULL, so you must already suspect something to ask. **All three answer "tell me about X" for an X you already suspect. NONE answers "what exists that nothing points at?"** That is the reverse enumeration, and it is the same question today's promoter finding turned on: ask which enumeration a check walks.
- detail: **THE ANSWER ALREADY EXISTED IN THIS CODEBASE, APPLIED TO A DIFFERENT NOUN.** `reachability_ratchet.py` does exactly this for ROUTES -- orphaned routers, raw 338 / effective 277, enforced daily and wired into `.github/workflows/pr-gates.yml`. The concept is proven, running, and trusted. **It was simply never pointed at TOOLS or GATES.** `_tools/dark_tools.py` is that ratchet aimed one noun over. 3/3 controls: it finds the motivating incident (`lane_halt.py`, 0 callers), does NOT flag a genuinely wired tool (`reachability_ratchet.py`), and correctly sees a tool called only from a lane prompt as consulted.
- detail: **MEASURED 2026-08-04T18:10Z. BASIS: 88 `tools/*.py` on `origin/main` (tests excluded) against 3,837 repo files + 34 lane prompts + 183 agent docs. DARK AND UNEXPLAINED: 15 (17%). EIGHT OF THOSE FIFTEEN ARE MENTIONED NOWHERE AT ALL** -- not in the ledger, not in memory, not in any handover: `feature_completeness_report.py`, `model_import_linter.py`, `graph_gap_directives.py`, `promote_graph_directives.py`, `ingest_observed_edges.py`, `governor_explain.py`, `graph_domain_digest.py`, `check_no_hardcoded_localhost.py`. **`lane_halt.py` is one of fifteen, not a special case** -- which is the finding, because it means the class was never about that tool.
- detail: **THE BASIS ERROR I MADE FIRST, BECAUSE IT IS THE SAME DEFECT AND I NEARLY PUBLISHED IT.** A repo-only scan reports **34 of 96 dark**. That number is WRONG: **9 tools are consulted ONLY from the SCHEDULED-TASK PROMPTS on OneDrive**, which are not in the repo -- `builder_selftest_integrity_report.py` (which THIS lane runs every single day), `vast_spend.py` (which `authority.json` names as the spend source of truth), `clerk_reconcile.py`, `fu_ledger.py` and five others. Publishing 34 would have repeated the exact defect the tool exists to catch: **a check walking a narrower enumeration than its consumers.** And a second, worse one: the first implementation parsed `git cat-file --batch` with CHARACTER offsets against BYTE lengths, drifted on the first non-ASCII blob, and read **25 of 3,837 files** -- reporting 47 dark from an almost-empty caller corpus. **The tell was the BASIS LINE, not the result.** `25 repo files` is impossible for this repo. Read the denominator before the answer.
- detail: **THE SELF-APPLICATION, AND IT IS THE PART THAT MATTERS MOST TODAY.** Everything built this session -- `peer_review.py`, `friction.py`, `rule_echo.py`, `loop_health.py` -- is wired into **lane PROMPTS, not into code**. A prompt instruction is a REQUEST; a code call is an INVOCATION. **That is precisely `lane_halt`'s shape one level up: armed, documented, and dependent on someone choosing to consult it.** A lane can silently skip every one of them and nothing today would notice, because `loop_health` measures stalls and adjudication latency but **has no reading for "a lane was told to call X and did not"**. I have no detector for the failure mode I spent the day building instruments against.
