<!-- FU-207 status=open priority=P1 filed=2026-07-31 last_touch=2026-08-31 stale_days=2 -->
### FU-207 | A scheduled task's `lastRunAt` advanced for five consecutive slots that did no work and left no trace
- date: 2026-07-31 · source: prod-drift-sentinel (01:17Z slot, reporting at 19:1xZ) · status: open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: `sentinel_run_ledger --window-hours 24` returned `VERDICT: GAP rc=1` naming FIVE missed slots -- 04:15, 07:15, 10:15, 13:15, 16:15Z. **My first hypothesis was that the scheduler had stopped firing. `list_scheduled_tasks` refutes it: `prod-drift-sentinel.lastRunAt = 2026-07-31T16:17:36Z`.** The scheduler fired every one of them. Yet `prod_deploy_state.json` has an mtime of 01:17:45Z and its `run_receipts` array still ends at 01:17:45Z -- my own receipt. So five invocations were STARTED and not one of them reached step 0, which is the first statement in the SKILL. **`lastRunAt` is the scheduler's record that it ATTEMPTED an invocation. It is not evidence that the run did anything at all**, and it is written in the success-shaped place a reader checks. This is the FU-164 condition inverted: receipts were built because a run can do the work and leave no record; here a run left no record because it did no work, and only the receipt gap could tell me which. The likely cause is this very run: it recorded its receipt at 01:17:45Z, was SUSPENDED mid-session for ~17.5h (verified against an independent clock -- PR #2519 created_at 19:08:26Z), and resumed at ~19:00Z, holding the lane across all five slots.
- detail: THE SELF-IMPLICATING PART, stated plainly because it is the actionable part. The previous slot recorded as an honest gap that *"THE PREVIOUS SLOT WAS STILL WRITING SIX MINUTES BEFORE THIS RUN'S RECEIPT... that is the overlap hazard, very nearly realised."* It is now fully realised at 200x the magnitude: not a 6-minute overlap but a 17.5-hour one that starved five siblings. An overrun does not fail loudly -- it fails as SILENCE in other lanes, and the lane that caused it is the one lane guaranteed not to notice, because it is busy. Nothing in this system currently bounds a run's wall-clock duration or detects that a slot was swallowed by its predecessor while it was happening.
- verify: `python D:\zo\_lanes\prod-drift\tools\sentinel_run_ledger.py --window-hours 24` -- rc 0 CLEAN / 1 GAP / 2 ERROR. Cross-read against `list_scheduled_tasks`.`lastRunAt` for the same task: a slot present in lastRunAt but ABSENT from `run_receipts` is this defect, and the two surfaces disagreeing is the signal.
- verify_seen_red: YES, live and unambiguously -- rc=1 with five named slots at the moment of filing, against `lastRunAt` asserting the most recent of them had run. Both surfaces queried in the same minute.
- log:
  - 2026-07-31T19:1xZ prod-drift-sentinel: **I ALMOST FILED THE WRONG DEFECT.** The GAP verdict reads naturally as "the scheduler is broken", and that would have been a P1 escalation aimed at the wrong component -- FU-205's mistake exactly (a slot-grid constant misread as scheduler phase drift), six hours later, from the opposite direction. What separated them was one command against a DIFFERENT surface: `list_scheduled_tasks`. A missed-slot signal has at least three causes -- the cron did not fire, the run fired and died, or a predecessor was still holding the lane -- and the run-ledger alone cannot distinguish them, because all three look identical from inside the receipts file. **A signal that cannot name which of its causes fired is a prompt to investigate, never a diagnosis.**
  - 2026-07-31T19:1xZ prod-drift-sentinel: MISSED SLOT is an EMAIL condition in this lane's charter and it fired for real this time, so it was emailed -- bundled with FU-208 as ONE message naming one decision, not two status dumps.
  - 2026-07-31T19:3xZ prod-drift-sentinel: **CORROBORATED FROM A THIRD SURFACE, and it is a stronger form of the evidence than the receipt gap.** FU-207 rests on two surfaces disagreeing (`lastRunAt` advanced, `run_receipts` did not) -- but both are records ABOUT runs, so a sceptic can ask whether the receipt writer was simply broken. The nightly backup lane supplies an independent check: its launcher creates a per-run `.cmd` file on disk before it starts anything, and for 2026-07-31 that file does not exist. **A side effect that a working run cannot avoid producing is better evidence than any ledger**, because nothing has to remember to write it. Same conclusion, different mechanism: the slot fired and the session did no work.
  - 2026-08-01T07:2xZ mcplookup-nightly-db-backup: **A FOURTH SURFACE, AND IT SPLITS THIS ENTRY FROM [[FU-208]] RATHER THAN CONFIRMING IT.** This entry's mechanism is a predecessor holding the lane for 17.5h; the sibling backup slot at 07:10:01Z on 7/31 was NOT that. Its session transcript shows the run terminated five seconds in by a **weekly usage limit** ('resets 3pm America/New_York' = 19:00Z), with no predecessor involved. So `lastRunAt` advancing over an empty run has at least TWO distinct causes on this box -- lane overrun and quota exhaustion -- which are indistinguishable from every artifact-side surface (both leave exactly the same absence) and are distinguishable from the session transcript in one read. **Any future missed-slot diagnosis should read the session transcript before naming a cause**; the receipt gap and the missing launcher quartet are prompts to investigate, never diagnoses. Cadence-side detector for the backup lane logged under [[FU-208]].
  - 2026-08-01T10:3xZ cadence-jobs-daily-trigger: **SECOND LANE, SAME CLASS -- `cadence-jobs-daily-trigger` did not run at all on 2026-07-31, and both cadence jobs breached their 36h SLA as a result.** This entry was filed against `prod-drift-sentinel`; this datapoint says the defect is NOT lane-specific. Evidence the run never started, taken from a PRE-LAUNCH side effect rather than an outcome ([[a_missing_pre_launch_side_effect_proves_never_started]]): `tools_cadence_fire.ps1` writes its `FIRE_START` line BEFORE it POSTs anything, and `_cadence_fire.log` jumps 2026-07-30T06:27:17 -> 2026-08-01T06:24:18 with nothing between; `_cadence_launch.log` carries the identical hole; `cadence_pending_runs.json.updated_at` was still 2026-07-30T10:30:21Z; and prod run_ids go 60 (07-30) -> **61** (08-01) with no row issued on 07-31 at all. So this is not 'a run that failed' -- it is a run that never reached its first statement, which is FU-207's shape exactly. **Cost:** `perspective_snapshots.last_ok` and `ask_corpus_drift.last_ok` both sat at 2026-07-30T10:29/10:30Z, ~48h against a 36h SLA, so /health was `alert:true` with BOTH jobs `overdue:true` when this run opened; it closed `alert:false` after runs 61/62. Recovery needed one normal run, no re-fire, $0. **The sharpening this adds, and it cuts against adding a gate:** unlike FU-207's original five silent slots, this gap was LOUD the whole time -- the cadence `/health` endpoint reported `alert:true` correctly for ~12h. The detector already exists and is per-lane and honest. What does not exist is a SUBSCRIBER: nothing read that alert, so a correctly-raised alarm surfaced only when the next day's trigger happened to fire and look at it. Per R7 and the harness doctrine's 'do not answer a finding by proposing a new required check', the increment owed here is wiring an existing reader to the existing signal -- note `vast-jobs-daily-audit` (07:36 daily) ALREADY claims 'mcprisky.io cadence health' in its scope and runs ~1h after this lane, so the subscriber may already exist and simply not have fired on 07-31 either. That is the next thing to check, not a new alarm to build.
  - 2026-08-01T11:4xZ vast-jobs-daily-audit: **THIRD LANE, SAME MISSED DAY -- and this one leaves a MACHINE-READABLE fingerprint, which is the part worth keeping.** `prod-drift-sentinel` and `cadence-jobs-daily-trigger` are already recorded here as not having run on 2026-07-31 (weekly usage-limit suspension). This lane did not run either, and I did not learn that from `lastRunAt` -- which this very FU proves can advance for slots that did no work -- but from the lane's own OUTPUT: `D:\zo\runs\ops_audit_state.json` `entries[]` held 2026-07-26..2026-07-30 and then 2026-08-01, with **no 2026-07-31**. That array has exactly ONE writer, this audit, so a missing date is a missed run recorded by the work itself rather than by the scheduler's claim about the work. **A side effect the run cannot fake beats a status field it can** ([[an_execution_record_is_not_an_execution]], [[a_missing_pre_launch_side_effect_proves_never_started]]). Note the asymmetry that makes it usable: `record` UPSERTS on date, so a run that happens always leaves its mark and a run that does not can never be backfilled by accident -- the hole is durable. Nothing here is a new gate; see the log line below for the report that was shipped.

### FU-321 | A failed wave became "the newest unfinished run" forever, damming every future rescore
- date: 2026-08-11 · source: moat-rescore-weekly · status: pr-open · priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: Run `20260811-061104` fired at 06:11Z, the pod FATALed fetching the transfer bundle, the instance was destroyed and import was correctly skipped (no rows written, no invariant breached). The run then **dammed the pipeline**. `open_run()` resumes *the newest run directory whose state.json lacks `postcheck: done`* -- a question a FAILED run can never answer -- so this run became the newest unfinished run permanently. Two subsequent `--run` invocations each resumed it, aborted at import with `preds.jsonl.gz missing`, exited 1, and spent $0. **Next Tuesday's scheduled run would have done exactly the same and left the moat to go stale indefinitely: the silent-staleness failure this lane exists to catch, committed by the lane's own harness, in the one component whose failure is invisible from `/freshness`.** The deeper defect is TWO INSTRUMENTS DISAGREEING ABOUT ONE WORD: `--check-open-runs` reads the LEDGER's abort vocabulary (`manual_*`/`wedge_*`, 19 runs already in that bucket) and called this run deliberately closed; `open_run` reads only the FILESYSTEM phase map and never consults the ledger, so it called the same run unfinished. Both readings were defensible and nothing reconciled them. Appending a correct `manual_closed_*` ledger event did NOT unblock it -- proof the two surfaces are genuinely disjoint, not merely inconsistent.
- verify: `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\_moat_patch_test.py"` -- rc must be 0. Drives the REAL `open_run` over a fixture in exactly the damming shape (fired/failed/destroyed/postcheck-never-reached): (1) NEGATIVE CONTROL -- `origin/main`'s `open_run` RESUMES `20260811-061104`; (2) patched skips it; (3) patched opens a fresh run; (4) OVER-SKIP GUARD -- a genuinely partial run with no verdict is STILL resumed; (5) `bash -n` on the onstart script; (6) the control copy is removed from the worktree.
- verify_seen_red: 2026-08-11T06:45:41Z -- assertion 1 ran against `git show origin/main:tools/rescore/weekly_rescore.py` and selected `20260811-061104`, i.e. the unpatched artifact demonstrably resumes the dead run. The control was itself first RED for the WRONG reason: the extracted copy was parked in a temp dir and died on `from spend_guard import ...` (sibling-import hazard). Moved beside its siblings it reproduces the defect cleanly. Recorded because *control failed* and *control could not run* are different readings, and only one of them is evidence.
- resolution: PR **#3209** (`fix/rescore-terminal-run-and-onstart-forensics`, commit `bfd520d2`, 2 files, +67/-2). Adds `_terminally_finished(state)`; `open_run` skips a run whose own state records a terminal verdict (`killed_*`/`abort*`/`abandon*`/`cancel*`, or `fail` WITH the instance already destroyed) and opens a fresh one, logging the skip and writing `run_skipped_terminal` to the ledger -- a run silently stepped over is how a dam becomes invisible a second time. `result: "ok"` is deliberately NOT terminal: a successful run closes via `run_closed`, and one that did not IS the stranded shape that must keep alarming. Cut from a `git worktree` at origin/main, never a stash -- a paid wave was executing out of the canonical checkout at the time.
- log: 2026-08-11 · moat-rescore-weekly · UNBLOCKED IN-RUN so the week's wave could still fire, WITHOUT falsifying a phase: the dead run dir was MOVED to `D:\zo\runs\weekly_rescore_aborted\20260811-061104` rather than stamped `postcheck: done` (a skipped phase is not a passed phase, R3). Reversible by moving it back; forensics intact; `--check-open-runs` re-run BEFORE any spend and required rc=0, so the move could not convert an ABORTED run into a STRANDED one. Fresh run `20260811-063956` opened and fired on machine 57734.
  - 2026-08-31 moat-rescore-weekly: FU-321 mirrored in check_open_runs -- run 20260822-220319 (result=deadline, destroyed) was refused by open_run as run_skipped_terminal yet flagged STRANDED/STALE forever by the detector (abort vocabulary never learned the terminal word). Fixed: _state_terminal() ORs _terminally_finished + the _state_abandoned spend bar into the classifier; RED-first test with held-instance negative control, 29/29, live check rc=0. status resolved, PR #4302 commit 74513cfc.

### FU-302 | AN UNREVERTABLE PEER DECISION ALARMED IN 16 LANES FOR 48h AND NO VERB EXISTED TO END IT

- date: 2026-08-09
- lane: follow-up-triage--implement-agent-for-the-zo-sentinel-project
- class: defect
- status: in-progress
- priority: P1
- detail: `peer-review-status-must-not-report-a-silent-zero` was ACTED 2026-08-08 and the change it armed is GOOD. Its stored `revert_cmd` was `git -C . diff --stat  # no repo: ...` -- the prose after `#` is part of the string, so git parsed it as arguments and exited 129, and the decision fell to REVERT_FAILED. `sweep()` retries REVERT_FAILED every run, `_unrunnable_reason` correctly declines to re-run a command that never existed, and `alarms += 1` fires. MEASURED on the live store 2026-08-09T17:2xZ: **33 identical `revert retry SKIPPED` log lines from 16 distinct lanes in 48h**, each one making that lane exit rc=1. peer_review.py's own FU-291 comment predicted this ("a permanent false alarm is how a fleet learns to skip reading its own alarms") and then provided no exit from it -- there was no verb for *the stored command was never real, here is a real one*. Every lane could see it; none could fix it.
- resolution: PARTIAL, shipped this run. `--repair` added to `_tools/peer_review.py` (backup `_followup_backups/2026-08-09/peer_review.py.pre-repair`, 72769 B). It replaces a `verify_cmd`/`revert_cmd` ONLY when the current value is unrunnable by `_unrunnable_reason`, and REFUSES any field whose current value can be invoked -- so a genuine RED can never be repaired away, only an ABSENT predicate can be given a body. Replacing `revert_cmd` requires a `--revert-check` that exits 0. Self-test 22/22 unchanged after the patch; negative control observed REFUSING (rc=4) a repair of a runnable field on `align-silent-lane-window-to-36h`. Applied to the stuck decision: the `<s>`-placeholder verify was replaced with `_staging/probe_status_not_silent_zero.py` (control rc=1, real rc=0 -- 20/20 decisions named).
- residual: THE ALARM IS NOT YET SILENCED AND THIS ENTRY STAYS OPEN UNTIL IT IS. The revert was not repaired, because there is no honest one left: the action edited `peer_review.py`, and four days of unrelated repairs (FU-291, FU-296, FU-302) have since landed in the same file, so a file-restore would revert far more than the decision. A revert that has been overtaken by later work on the same artifact is a THIRD case, distinct from "failed" and from "absent", and the tool has no terminal disposition for it. NAMED NEXT ACTION, deliberately not taken here: a `SUPERSEDED_VERIFIED` state entered only on evidence -- revert unrunnable AND the repaired verify passing at that moment, with the verify output stored. NOT a new gate; a disposition. Do not implement it by widening `_unrunnable_reason`.
- verify: `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_staging\probe_repair_verb_discriminates.py"` exits 0 iff BOTH legs hold: `--repair` REFUSES a runnable field (rc=4) and ACCEPTS an unrunnable one, measured against a throwaway store. A one-sided probe here would be worthless -- the whole safety argument is the refusal.
- verify_seen_red: unproven
- log:
  - 2026-08-09T17:2xZ filed by follow-up-triage. The count of 33 is from the decision's own `log` array, not from lane transcripts; 16 lanes is the distinct set of names in those lines and includes `unnamed-lane` twice, which is itself a small finding -- two sweeps ran without `--lane`.
  - 2026-08-09T17:4xZ follow-up-triage: the predicate named above now EXISTS and was run -- `_staging/probe_repair_verb_discriminates.py`, rc=0, LEG A rc=4 (refused an invokable field) and LEG B rc=0 with the stored command verified CHANGED, both against a throwaway store; the live one was never opened. `verify_seen_red` stays `unproven` because a REFUSAL is the probe's EXPECTED outcome, not a red -- it has still never returned 1, and stamping trust onto an assertion never seen fail is the thing this ledger is made of.

That state is reachable purely by 3 accumulated UNKNOWNs (UNKNOWN_STRIKES_BEFORE_REVERT=3), i.e. by a predicate TIMING OUT, and a decision that arrives that way has invokable predicates by construction -- so it has NO EXIT AT ALL, and --sweep retries its revert forever. MEASURED: bar-csv-machine-writer-must-not-erase-graded-rows sits in REVERT_FAILED (strikes=3; sweeps 18:21Z, 19:48Z by this lane, 00:19:53Z) while its OWN verify measures GREEN rc=0 -- poleA_graded_survives=True poleB_machine_replaced=True controls=True / NO ERASE. The patch landed; only the receipt bar_eraser_patch_commit.txt was never written, hence revert rc=3 NOTHING TO REVERT RECORDED. This is [[FU-344]] inverted: FU-344 was REVERT_FAILED reading ZERO while a real failure hid elsewhere; this is REVERT_FAILED reading ONE for a change that is fine. <!-- FU-303 status=open priority=P1 filed=2026-08-09 last_touch=2026-09-01 stale_days=1 -->
### FU-303 | fu_verify HANGS ON WINDOWS FOR THE REASON peer_review ALREADY FIXED, SO PHASE 2a PRODUCED NO DATA

- date: 2026-08-09
- lane: follow-up-triage--implement-agent-for-the-zo-sentinel-project
- class: defect
- status: open  <!-- reopened 2026-09-01: recurred, see log -->
- priority: P1
- detail: `tools/fu/fu_verify.py` line 116 runs `subprocess.run(cmd, shell=True, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=timeout)` with `CMD_TIMEOUT_S = 60`. On Windows that call BLOCKS FOREVER in its cleanup when a grandchild still holds the pipe -- killing cmd.exe does not kill the process tree, and `communicate()` waits on a pipe nobody will close. So the per-command timeout the file's own docstring advertises ("Per-command timeout, and a wall-clock ceiling for the whole sweep") does not fire, and the ceiling is never reached because the process is not spinning. MEASURED this run: the detached `--once --json` sweep sat at **0 bytes of stdout and 0.20s CPU after 28 minutes** and was killed. Corroborating: `Get-Process python` showed orphans still alive from **2026-08-08 15:47** and from 08:55 and 10:33 today -- which is also the `mcp-timeout-orphan` recurring stall, x16 across 5 lanes in `loop_health`. THE CONSEQUENCE IS NOT COSMETIC: Phase 2a is the mechanical half of triage, and it returned NOTHING this run. That is UNKNOWN, and unknown is not clean (R6).
- detail2: THIS IS A SIBLING'S SOLVED PROBLEM, UNPROPAGATED. `_tools/peer_review.py::_run` carries the fix and the scar in a comment: `capture_output=True` with `shell=True` HANGS FOREVER on timeout when a grandchild still holds the pipe. Its cure is Popen to a FILE SINK with `stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL`, `p.wait(timeout=...)`, `p.kill()` on expiry, then read the sink. `stdin=DEVNULL` matters independently: a probe that reads stdin blocks forever otherwise, and several ledger predicates shell out to `git`, which prompts.
- resolution: RESOLVED 2026-08-10 by follow-up-triage. `_probe_once` now uses Popen -> FILE SINK + `stdin=DEVNULL` + `p.wait(timeout)` + `taskkill /F /T` on expiry, preserving the `{verdict: UNKNOWN, note: 'TIMEOUT after Ns -- probe could not answer'}` shape exactly so UNKNOWN is never read as RED. The lane turned out to be the UNTRACKED `_tools/fu_verify.py` that the scheduled sweep actually executes, not the git-tracked copy this entry named -- R1: resolve the artifact that RUNS.
- verify: python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_staging\probe_fu_verify_timeout.py"
- verify_seen_red: 2026-08-10 -- observed RED at 40.1s against the pre-fix file while its negative control returned in 5.0s
- log:
  - 2026-08-09T17:3xZ filed by follow-up-triage after the Phase 2a sweep was killed at 28min/0 bytes. The predicate named above is NOT YET WRITTEN; that is stated here rather than left implied, because this charter spent four days instructing every lane to run `fu_context.py` before it existed.
  - 2026-08-10T17:2xZ follow-up-triage -- REPAIRED AND PROVEN AT BOTH POLES, and the predicate this entry admitted was never written now exists. ### FU-346 | heading FORM, not heading LEVEL, is what makes an FU invisible to fu_verify -- and level-only was tested, failed, and left the cause reading UNKNOWN
- date: 2026-08-13
- lane: follow-up-triage (diagnosed and fixed in-run)
- class: defect
- status: open  <!-- reopened 2026-09-01: recurred, see log -->
- priority: P1
- detail: `fu_ledger.HEAD_RE` is `^### FU-(\d+)\b(?:\s*\|\s*(.*))?$`. An entry
  headed `## FU-343 -- title` OR `### FU-344 -- title` does not match it, so the entry
  does not exist to `fu_verify.py` and its `verify:` predicate is NEVER ONCE EXECUTED --
  while `ledger_lint.py` reports the file CLEAN, because it parses headings its own way.
  Measured at the start of this run: 339 headings, 335 parsed. FOUR entries invisible,
  three of them filed THAT MORNING, two of them carrying findings about this very class.
- detail: SEPARATELY, and found by the same count, a genuine FU-NUMBER COLLISION: two
  different entries were both filed as FU-342 on 2026-08-13 (improvement-loop's
  poll-progress finding, and score-import-shepherd's money-guard finding). Only the
  second was visible to the parser, so only the second had ever been exploded to memory
  or linked. The invisible one was renumbered to FU-345; the visible one keeps 342.
- verify_seen_red: NEVER
- log: 2026-08-13 WHY THE SIBLING LANE'S CONTROL FAILED, which is the actual lesson.
  - 2026-09-01 RECURRED, so status is reopened. 364 headings vs 361 parsed this run: FU-362, FU-368 and FU-369 were all filed today as `## FU-NNN -- title` and were therefore invisible to fu_verify -- FU-369 carried a full census and its predicate never ran. Healed in-run with _fut_headform_20260807.py (rewrote exactly 3, lost 0, 361 -> 364). THE CURE IS IN THE WRONG PLACE: ledger_headform_parity.py detects the bad form AFTER an emitter writes it, and this entry's verify_seen_red is still NEVER, so its green has never been evidence of anything. All three of today's were written by improvement-loop. The durable fix is at the emitter, not in a detector this lane runs.
  improvement-loop cycle-0045 hit this the same morning, hypothesised heading LEVEL,
  and tested it honestly with `_staging\cycle-0045\fix_heading_level.py` -- a script that
  demanded 334 -> 336 or it would restore the backup byte-for-byte. Demoting `##` to
  `###` moved the parsed count by ZERO, so the script auto-reverted and FU-343 recorded
  `Cause therefore UNKNOWN, which is not zero (R6)`. That reading was correct and the
  discipline was right. The reason the count did not move is that level is only HALF the
  predicate: `### FU-344 -- title` is already `###` and still invisible, because HEAD_RE
  wants ` | ` after the number, not ` -- `. Fixing level alone converts an invisible
  `##`-entry into an invisible `###`-entry. A control that changes one of two necessary
  conditions and observes no movement looks exactly like a refuted hypothesis.
- log: 2026-08-13 FIXED IN-RUN. Both conditions corrected on the four headings
  (`_followup_backups/2026-08-13/FOLLOWUPS.md.pre-headform`); the `#### FU-343 addendum`
  sub-heading was renamed `#### Addendum to FU-343` so it stops counting as an entry
  heading it never was. Parity now 338 headings / 338 parsed / 338 unique numbers.
  FU-343, FU-344 and FU-345 exist to `fu_verify.py` for the first time.
- log: 2026-08-13 The recurrence guard is CODE, not this paragraph -- see verify. Note
  it does not test heading level, or separators, or any other proxy: it asks the parser
  itself how many of the headings that are there it actually saw. A proxy for the
  contract is what made the sibling lane's control read false.
- resolution: `_tools/ledger_headform_parity.py` (new, self-tested 4/4 incl. the exact
  `### FU-NNN -- title` case that defeated the level-only control); ledger repaired;
  FU-342 collision resolved by renumbering the invisible entry to FU-345.
- verify: `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\ledger_headform_parity.py"` must exit 0
  -- every `^#+\s*FU-` heading is an entry `fu_ledger.parse` returns, and every FU number
  belongs to exactly one entry. Negative control: `--self-test` must stay 4/4, and it MUST
  keep the `### FU-NNN -- title` case, because that is the one a level-only check misses.

### FU-361 | `oldest_scored_at` has not moved since at least 2026-07-19 across 385,000 refresh slots -- the refresh half cannot move the corpus floor, and the SLA is measured somewhere it never looks
- date: 2026-09-01 | source: moat-rescore-weekly 06:2xZ run | priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: **MEASURED ACROSS TEN LANDED WAVES.** Every `state.json` with a refresh half since `20260719-003024` records `oldest_scored_at = 2026-06-24T15:46:24.527410`. Identical to the microsecond. That spans 385,000 cumulative refresh-server slots and **includes the 140,000- and 120,000-server waves that changed 97% and 99.99% of their cohorts** -- so this is not a consequence of [[FU-358]]'s zero-yield regime. Even when the refresh half is working perfectly, the floor does not move.
- detail: **THE LIKELY MECHANISM IS STRUCTURAL AND SITS IN `ph_export`.** The cohort is built from one distinct-URL REPRESENTATIVE per URL (`weekly_rescore.py` ~line 652-668, with a scored rep preferred so refresh lands in place). A scored server that is NOT its URL's representative is therefore ineligible for refresh on every run, forever -- its score can never age out by construction. With 498,702 registry rows against 296,109 scored servers there is a large population in exactly that position. NOT YET CONFIRMED: I have not proven the frozen row belongs to a non-representative server, only that the floor is frozen and that the selection makes such a freeze possible.
- detail: **WHY THIS MATTERS MORE THAN IT LOOKS.** `/freshness/policy` computes `corpus_age_days` from `newest_scored_at`, which is a `max()`. Today it publishes **1.05 days, `status: fresh`, `breaching_sla: false`** while the oldest score in the corpus is **69 days old and structurally unable to age**. The keyed surfaces are fail_closed against that number. The SLA is honest about cadence and silent about the floor -- and the floor is the half a customer would care about.
- detail: **LEFT OPEN ON PURPOSE.** Both available fixes are outside a single lane's grant: changing cohort selection moves spend and the moat's semantics, and changing what the SLA measures is `redefining_the_metric`. Reporting it is mine; deciding it is not. Filing it open is the fallback here rather than the reflex -- there is no $0 reversible version of this one.
- verify: every landed delta run's `baseline_freshness.oldest_scored_at` under `D:\zo\runs\weekly_rescore` is expected to become NON-CONSTANT once this is addressed; today `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_probes\oldest_scored_at_movement.py"` prints the same timestamp on all ten qualifying runs. That command going non-constant is the closure condition.
- resolution: NONE -- open. Both available fixes (changing cohort selection, or changing what the SLA measures) sit outside a single lane's grant, and there is no $0 reversible version of this one.
- verify_seen_red: YES -- IT IS RED RIGHT NOW, and that is the finding. `python "D:\\zo\\Zocomputer Agents\\_probes\\oldest_scored_at_movement.py"` prints `2026-06-24T15:46:24.527410` on all ten qualifying landed runs, identical to the microsecond. The predicate is not an untested assertion awaiting a failure; it is failing, which is why the entry exists.
- log: 2026-09-01 -- filed by moat-rescore-weekly while shipping [[FU-358]]. Recorded explicitly so the shallower fix is not mistaken for this one: shrinking `--refresh-cap` was NOT proposed, because the refresh half's problem is not its size and acting on the shallower diagnosis would have made this one harder to see.

### FU-364 | This lane's ENTIRE remit is an action `authority.json` has never named: `--may registry_insert` has returned UNKNOWN ACTION for 8+ consecutive days
- date: 2026-09-01 * source: discovery-harvest-daily 11:06Z * status: open * priority: P1
- class: defect
- detail: `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\authority.py" --may registry_insert` returns **UNKNOWN ACTION**. That is the ONLY action this lane performs -- every run INSERTs unassessed rows into the prod registry (today: net_new 1150, registry 499852) -- so the lane's whole remit is unnamed in the file that is supposed to BE the authority rather than a judgement call. It has never been blocking: during the away window an unnamed action converted to DECIDE_AND_LOG, and the lane has acted and logged on that basis every day. **The defect is not a stall, it is a BLIND SPOT in the authority surface.** `authority.py` raises rc=2 on an unknown action precisely so a quiet no cannot masquerade as a decision -- but the away-window conversion turned that loud unknown into a soft yes, and now the window has closed (2026-08-30) the conversion is gone while the action is still unnamed. So the steady state going forward is: the single most-repeated write on this fleet resolves against no clause at all. UNPROVEN and stated as such: I did not check whether `--may` now raises rather than converts post-window for this action; that check is the first thing whoever takes this should run, because it decides whether this is a latent gap or a live one.
- detail: NOT SELF-CLEARED, and the reason is the point. Naming an action in `authority.json` is granting an agent authority the file does not already grant -- escalate-only under the standing envelope, peer-clearable under SELF-STEERING v5.1, and NOT something a harvest run should hand itself mid-import. Editing the file to bless my own daily write is the exact shape of an agent widening its own envelope, which is why this is filed rather than fixed even though the edit is $0 and reversible. The chairman's away window closed 2026-08-31, so this is now askable through the ordinary route.
- detail: WHY THIS IS BEING FILED ONLY NOW, which is itself the finding worth carrying: the note has appeared in `D:\zo\runs\sprint200k\harvest_schedule_runlog.txt` on 2026-08-13, 08-24, 08-31 and today, phrased as "NOTE unchanged". A finding restated in a run log and never entered in the ledger is UNSEARCHABLE -- `memory_search` does not index the runlog, `explode_followups_to_memory.py` never saw it, and every successor lane re-derived it from scratch. Four repetitions of a note is not a record; it is four lanes independently noticing the same thing and none of them capturing it. Same class as [[FU-255]] (prose does not steer) pointed at this ledger's own intake.
- verify_seen_red: NEVER
- log:
  - 2026-09-01T11:06Z discovery-harvest-daily: filed. Run itself fully green -- harvested 2378, net_new 1150, registry 498702 -> 499852, continuity +8 foreign fully attributed, proxy self-reaped, nothing spent. Nothing was blocked by this gap today.
  - 2026-09-01T11:1xZ discovery-harvest-daily: **THE UNPROVEN LIMB ABOVE IS NOW MEASURED, AND IT IS LIVE, NOT LATENT.** Ran the check the entry said to run first. `authority.py --away` reports `away window CLOSED (2026-09-01 > 2026-08-30); it expired on its own and every away-only relaxation reverted with it` -- so the DECIDE_AND_LOG conversion that made this harmless for 25 days is GONE. `authority.py --may registry_insert` now exits **rc=2 UNKNOWN ACTION**: "'registry_insert' is in neither 'delegated' nor 'still_escalate_ONLY'. Do not infer a default." So as of today the single most-repeated write on this fleet resolves against NO CLAUSE and the tool RAISES on it. NOT a stall: this lane's own SKILL grants the unassessed registry INSERT as its entire stated remit, and today's run completed on that basis (net_new 1150, registry 499852) -- but the file that is supposed to end judgement calls is silent on it, so the grant currently lives only in prose, which is [[FU-255]] exactly. Escalating priority P2 -> P1: this is authority blindness on a daily write, not a watch item. NEGATIVE CONTROL, so 'rc=2' is a reading and not a guess: `--may data_deletion` still returns BLOCKED (FOREVER_HELD) from the same binary in the same shell, i.e. the tool is answering, the envelope is intact, and rc=2 is specific to this action rather than a broken install. STILL NOT SELF-CLEARED for the reason in the entry -- naming it is a chairman act, and an agent that blesses its own daily write in the authority file is the failure this ledger exists to catch. Chairman back as of 08-31; this is now askable by the ordinary route.
  - 2026-09-02: CORRECTION to the 2026-09-01 addendum's negative control, from discovery-harvest-daily. That entry cleared the tool by reporting `--may data_deletion` 'still returns BLOCKED' and concluded 'rc=2 is specific to this action'. Re-measured today in one shell: `--may registry_insert` -> rc=2 'UNKNOWN ACTION', and `--may data_deletion` -> rc=2 'BLOCKED: data_deletion ... FOREVER_HELD'. BOTH are rc=2. So the exit code does NOT discriminate unknown-action from forever-held, and a caller branching on rc alone would read a FOREVER_HELD clause as merely unnamed -- the more dangerous of the two mistakes. The discriminator is the MESSAGE PREFIX ('UNKNOWN ACTION:' vs 'BLOCKED:'), not rc. The 09-01 control was still a real control (it proved the binary answers rather than being broken); only the stated basis for it was wrong, and R5 says publish the basis, so this corrects the basis rather than the conclusion. FU-364 stays OPEN and UNCHANGED in substance: naming registry_insert in authority.json grants authority the file does not already grant, so it is not self-clearable, and today's import ran under the SKILL's own stated remit with nothing blocked. VERIFY predicate for a successor: `authority.py --may registry_insert` should stop printing 'UNKNOWN ACTION' once named; until then any code keying off rc=2 alone is UNPROVEN-safe and should be read as a live hazard.
- verify: `python "D:\zo\Zocomputer Agents\_tools\authority.py" --may registry_insert` exits 0 and names a clause, instead of returning UNKNOWN ACTION.
